🏰 Rajasthan History & Culture RPSC / RAS / EO-RO
📘 Part B: Act 2009

# 🏰 Rajasthan History & Culture: Master Coaching Notes & Revision Guide

Compiled from RBSE Official Textbooks, Rajasthan Hindi Granth Academy (राजस्थान हिंदी ग्रन्थ अकादमी), and 20+ Years of RPSC / RAS Exam Trends

CHAPTER 1
Chapter 1: History of Rajasthan — An Introduction (राजस्थान का इतिहास — एक परिचय)

🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Soul of Rajasthan (राजस्थान की आत्मा)
"Welcome to Rajasthan GK. If you ask a historian what defines this land, they will tell you two words: Geographical Hardship and Fierce Independence. Imagine a land bisected diagonally by the ancient Aravalli (अरावली) range—the oldest fold mountains on earth. To the west lay the unforgiving Thar Desert (थार मरुस्थल), where water was more precious than gold; to the east lay fertile river plains guarded by rocky plateaus. Why did foreign invaders struggle to subdue Rajasthan for over a thousand years? Because every pass in the Aravallis was a natural fortress, every sand dune an ambush, and the people developed an ethos where honor (आन, बान, शान - Aan, Baan, Shaan) was valued far above life itself. Let us explore how this land evolved from prehistoric stone tools to legendary kingdoms."

# 1. Nomenclature & Mental Map of Ancient Rajasthan (प्राचीन राजस्थान का नामकरण एवं मानचित्र)

🗺️ Mental Framework: Regional Geography of Ancient Rajasthan
Before memorizing names, build a spatial map in your mind:
  • North-West (Desert & Scrub): Maru (मरु) / Dhanva (धन्व) (Marwar / मारवाड़ - Jodhpur / जोधपुर) & Jangal Desh (जांगल देश) (Bikaner / बीकानेर - Nagaur / नागौर).
  • North-East (Plains & Hills): Matsya (मत्स्य) (Jaipur / जयपुर - Alwar / अलवर - Bharatpur / भरतपुर) & Shursena (शूरसेन) (Brij border: Bharatpur - Dholpur / धौलपुर - Karauli / करौली).
  • South-East (Mahi River Basin): Kanthal (कांठल) (Pratapgarh / प्रतापगढ़ riverbanks), Chhappan (छप्पन) (56-village plain of Banswara / बांसवाड़ा - Pratapgarh), and Vagad (वागड़) (Dungarpur / डूंगरपुर - Banswara).
  • Central & South (Aravalli Hills & Plateaus): Medpat (मेदपाट) / Mewar (मेवाड़), Girava (गिरवा) (Udaipur / उदयपुर bowl), and Uparmala (ऊपरमाल) (Bijolia / बिजौलिया - Bhainsrorgarh / भैंसरोड़गढ़ plateau).

# A. How the Land Got Its Name (Chronological Evolution)

  1. Vedic Times: The Rigveda refers to the arid desert tract as 'Maru' (मरु) or 'Dhanva' (धन्व). In the Valmiki Ramayana, it is called Marukantar (मरुकान्तार).
  2. Literary & Stone Inscriptions:
    • Vasantgarh Inscription (बसंतगढ़ शिलालेख - Sirohi / सिरोही - 625 AD / VS 682): Contains the term 'Rajasthaniyaditya' (राजस्थानीयादित्य) (earliest epigraphic mention of the root word).
    • Muhnot Nainsi ri Khyat (मुहणोत नैणसी री ख्यात - 17th Century): Nainsi uses the term Rajasthan (राजस्थान).
  3. George Thomas (जॉर्ज थॉमस - 1800 AD): The Irish military adventurer was the first to use the term 'Rajputana' (राजपूताना) for this geographical entity (documented in William Franklin’s 1805 book Military Memoirs of Mr. George Thomas).
  4. Colonel James Tod (कर्नल जेम्स टॉड - 1829 AD): The "Father of Rajasthan History" (राजस्थान के इतिहास का जनक / घोड़े वाले बाबा) popularized the names 'Raithan' (रायथान), 'Rajasthan' (राजस्थान), and 'Rajputana' (राजपूताना) in his seminal work Annals and Antiquities of Rajasthan (Central and Western Rajput States of India).
  5. Constitutional Integration (30 March 1949): The name 'Rajasthan' (राजस्थान) was officially adopted during the 4th stage (Brihat Rajasthan / वृहत् राजस्थान) of political integration (celebrated annually as Rajasthan Day / राजस्थान दिवस).
📖 Terminology Decoder: Ancient Regional Land Names (प्राचीन भौगोलिक नाम)
  • Jangal Desh (जांगल देश): The arid thorny region of Bikaner-Nagaur. Rulers of Bikaner held the imperial title "Jangal-dhar Badshah" (जांगलधर बादशाह).
  • Kanthal (कांठल): Derived from Kantha (कांटा/किनारा). Specifically refers to the territory along the banks of River Mahi (माही नदी) in Pratapgarh.
  • Chhappan ka Maidan (छप्पन का मैदान): The fertile riverine plain between Banswara and Pratapgarh drained by 56 rivulets and containing 56 agricultural villages.
  • Girava (गिरवा): Literally means "girdle of hills" (turban-shaped circle of steep hills encircling Udaipur city).
  • Uparmala (ऊपरमाल): The elevated, rocky tableland stretching from Bhainsrorgarh (Chittorgarh / चित्तौड़गढ़) to Bijolia (Bhilwara / भीलवाड़ा).
  • Bhomat (भोमट): The rugged, inaccessible Bhil tribal belt in the southern Aravalli hills (Udaipur, Dungarpur, Sirohi).
📖 Mythological Lore: Why the Thar Desert Exists (थार मरुस्थल की उत्पत्ति)
[Folk Belief / Epic Tradition] According to the Ramayana (रामायण), when Lord Rama (भगवान राम) was marching towards Lanka, the Ocean deity Varuna (वरुण) did not immediately yield passage. Rama angrily notched the infallible Amoghastra (अमोघास्त्र) arrow on his Kodanda bow to evaporate the sea. The Ocean deity terrified, emerged and begged for mercy. Since a divine arrow once set on the bowstring cannot be withdrawn, Rama discharged it towards the northern sea (Dhrumatulya / द्रुमतुल्य). The weapon incinerated the water, turning the lush prehistoric ocean basin into the arid sands of the Thar Desert (थार मरुस्थल).
⚠️ Examiner Trap: Common Nomenclature Confusions (परीक्षक का जाल)
  • Rajputana (राजपूताना) vs Rajasthan (राजस्थान): First person to call the region Rajputana = George Thomas (1800). First person to formally write Rajasthan / Raithan in a history text = Col. James Tod (1829).
  • Matsya Janapada (मत्स्य जनपद) vs Matsya Union (मत्स्य संघ): Ancient Matsya Janapada capital = Viratnagar / Bairath (विराटनगर / बैराठ). The 1948 integration stage Matsya Union capital = Alwar (अलवर)!
  • Shursena (शूरसेन) vs Sibi Janapada (शिवि जनपद): Shursena = Eastern border (Bharatpur / Dholpur / Karauli; capital Mathura). Sibi / Madhyamika (मध्यमिका / नगरी) = Mewar / Chittorgarh (capital Nagari / Madhyamika).

# 2. Prehistoric Era & Major Archaeological Civilizations (प्रागैतिहासिक काल एवं प्रमुख पुरातात्विक सभ्यताएं) 🔥 High-Frequency RPSC Area

🎙️ Cause & Effect: Why Did Civilizations Flourish in Desert Rajasthan?
"A common doubt among students: 'Sir, Rajasthan is a desert, so why are India's oldest civilizations found here?' Remember: 5,000 years ago, Rajasthan was not an arid desert! Two mighty Himalayan glacier-fed rivers flowed here—the sacred Saraswati (सरस्वती) (Drishadvati / दृषद्वती / modern seasonal Ghaggar / घग्घर) and the Kantarli (कांतली) & Banas (बनास) networks. When tectonic shifts redirected these glacial sources towards the Indus and Ganga, the Saraswati dried up, burying flourishing Harappan cities like Kalibangan beneath sand dunes."
🗺️ Mental Map of Rajasthan Archaeological Sites
  • North (Ghaggar River Valley): Kalibangan (कालीबंगा) (Hanumangarh / हनुमानगढ़), Pilibangan (पीलीबंगा), Rangmahal (रंगमहल).
  • South-East (Berach & Banas River Valleys): Ahar (आहड़) (Udaipur), Gilund (गिलूण्ड) (Rajsamand / राजसमंद), Balathal (बालाथल) (Vallabhnagar / वल्लभनगर, Udaipur), Ojhiyana (ओझियाना) (Bhilwara).
  • North-East (Aravalli Foothills & Banganga): Bairath (बैराठ) (Jaipur - Banganga River / बाणगंगा), Ganeshwar (गणेश्वर) (Sikar / सीकर / Neem Ka Thana - Kantali River).
  • Central / West (Kothari & Luni Valleys): Bagor (बागोर) (Bhilwara - Kothari River / कोठारी नदी), Tilwara (तिलवाड़ा) (Barmer / बाड़मेर / Balotra - Luni River / लूणी नदी).

# 1. Kalibangan (कालीबंगा - Hanumangarh) — "The Black Bangles Settlement" 🔥 Asked 8+ Times

  • Meaning & Discovery: Kali = Black, Bangan = Bangles (काली चूड़ियां - terracotta/clay bangles found in abundance).
  • Location: Left bank of dried Ghaggar River (घग्घर नदी) (ancient Saraswati/Drishadvati) in Hanumangarh district.
  • Excavation Team: Identified by Amalanand Ghosh (अमलानन्द घोष - 1952); excavated on a massive scale by B. B. Lal (बी. बी. लाल) and B. K. Thapar (बी. के. थापर - 1961–1969).
  • Stratification: Shows 5 cultural levels (Levels 1–2: Pre-Harappan / प्राक्-हड़प्पा; Levels 3–5: Mature Harappan / विकसित हड़प्पा).
  • Key Archaeological Discoveries (RPSC Core Fact-Bank):
    1. Ploughed Agricultural Field (जुता हुआ खेत): Earliest evidence of an agricultural field with criss-cross furrows (simultaneous dual-cropping of mustard / सरसों and gram / चना). Located outside the fortified settlement walls.
    2. Seven Fire Altars (सात अग्नि वेदिकाएं / हवन कुण्ड): Row of clay altars containing ash, charcoal, and animal bones, proving community sacrificial fire rituals.
    3. Earliest Brain Surgery (शल्य चिकित्सा / Hydrocephalus): Skull of a child with 6 pierced holes, showing bone healing (earliest surgical/craniotomy evidence in the world).
    4. Earthquake Evidence (भूकम्प के प्राचीनतम साक्ष्य): Deep fault lines in mud-brick walls prove India's earliest known seismic disaster (led to the decline of the pre-Harappan phase).
    5. Building Material: Built with sun-baked mud bricks (Ratio 4:2:1; size $30 \times 15 \times 7.5\text{ cm}$). Due to mud bricks, it is often termed a "Poor Man’s Settlement" (दीन-हीन बस्ती - Deen-Heen Basti).
    6. Unique Items: Wooden drainage pipes (लकड़ी की नालियां), cylindrical Mesopotamian-style seal (मेसोपोटामियाई मुहर), camel bones (ऊंट की हड्डियां), and a terracotta bull figurine.
🧠 Memory Hook: The "Doctor-Farmer of Kalibangan"
Imagine a Harappan farmer wearing Black Bangles (Kalibangan / कालीबंगा) who:
1. Ploughed two crops in his field.
2. Performed 7 Havan Kunds (अग्नि वेदिकाएं) when an Earthquake struck.
3. Rushed his child to the village surgeon for 6-hole head surgery (शल्य चिकित्सा)!

# 2. Ahar / Dhoolkot (आहड़ / धूलकोट - Udaipur) — "Tamravati Nagari" 🔥 Asked 6+ Times

  • Location: Banks of Ahar / Berach River (आहड़ / बेड़च नदी) (tributary of Banas / बनास) in Udaipur basin.
  • Ancient Names:
    • Inscriptions call it Tamravati Nagari (ताम्रवती नगरी) (City of Copper).
    • 10th–11th Century name: Aghatpur / Aghatdurg (आघाटपुर / आघाटदुर्ग).
    • Local colloquial name: Dhoolkot (धूलकोट) (Mound of Dust/Ashes).
  • Excavation: Discovered by Akshyakirti Vyas (अक्षयकीर्ति व्यास - 1953); further explored by R. C. Agrawal (आर. सी. अग्रवाल - 1954) and Dr. H. D. Sankalia (एच. डी. सांकलिया - 1961–62) of Deccan College, Pune.
  • Culture Type: Chalcolithic (ताम्र-पाषाण युगीन), dated 2000 BCE to 1200 BCE. Part of the broader Banas Culture (बनास संस्कृति).
  • Key Archaeological Finds:
    1. Copper Smelting Furnaces & Slag (तांबा गलाने की भट्ठियां): Proves Ahar was an industrial metallurgy hub smelting chalcopyrite ore from Khetri / Aravallis.
    2. Black and Red Ware (BRW) Pottery (लाल-काले मृद्भाण्ड): Fine pottery with geometric white slip designs painted on black backgrounds using inverted-firing technique (उल्टी तपाई विधि).
    3. Terracotta Bull ("Banasian Bull" / बनासियन बुल): Stylized terracotta figurines of humped bulls worshipped as symbols of fertility.
    4. Textile Dyeing Blocks (ठप्पे व छापे): Wooden stamp blocks prove dyeing and printing industry was active 3,500 years ago.
    5. Greco-Roman Connection: A terracotta seal showing God Apollo (अपोलो) with a quiver of arrows and Greek lettering, proving overseas trade links.

# 3. Ganeshwar (गणेश्वर - Neem Ka Thana / Sikar) — "Mother of Copper Age Civilizations" 🔥 Asked 5+ Times

  • Location: Banks of seasonal Kantali River (कांतली नदी) in Neem Ka Thana (नीम का थाना - formerly Sikar district).
  • Excavation: Excavated by R. C. Agrawal (रत्नचन्द्र अग्रवाल) and Vijay Kumar (विजय कुमार - 1977).
  • Why Called the "Mother" (ताम्रयुगीन सभ्यताओं की जननी)?
    • Radiocarbon dated to 2800 BCE, making it older than mature Harappan sites.
    • Ganeshwar was the chief mining and refining center that supplied nearly pure copper (99% copper purity) to Harappa and Mohenjo-daro.
  • Key Finds:
    • Ochre-Coloured Pottery (OCP / कृपिशवर्णी मृद्पात्र): Unique thin pottery with orange-red wash.
    • Over 400 copper arrowheads (बाणाग्र), spearheads, fishhooks (मछली पकड़ने का कांटा - proving Kantali was a perennial river supporting fishing), bangles, and chisels.
    • Stone-built flood embankments and houses (no mud bricks used!).

# 4. Bairath / Viratnagar (बैराठ / विराटनगर - Jaipur) — The Buddhist & Maurya Hub 🔥 Asked 7+ Times

  • Location: Banks of Banganga River (बाणगंगा नदी) amidst the granite hills of Bijak-ki-Pahari (बीजक की पहाड़ी), Bhim Dungri (भीम डूंगरी), and Moti Dungri (मोती डूंगरी).
  • Historical Identity: Ancient capital of the Matsya Janapada (मत्स्य जनपद) where the Pandavas (पाण्डव) spent their 13th year of exile (Agyatvas / अज्ञातवास).
  • Excavation History:
    • 1837: Captain Burt (कैप्टन बर्ट) discovered the celebrated Bhabhru Rock Edict (भाब्रू शिलालेख) of Emperor Ashoka on Bijak-ki-Pahari (now preserved in Asiatic Society Museum, Kolkata).
    • 1936: Dayaram Sahni (दयाराम साहनी) carried out systematic excavations, followed by Neelratna Banerjee (नीलरत्न बनर्जी) and Kailash Nath Dikshit (कैलाश नाथ दीक्षित - 1962).
  • Key Archaeological Finds:
    1. Ashokan Edicts (Bhabhru & Minor Rock Edict): Inscribed in Brahmi script; explicitly proves Ashoka’s unshakeable devotion to the Buddhist Trinity (Buddha, Dhamma, Sangha / बुद्ध, धम्म, संघ). Ashoka refers to himself as Priyadasi Raja Magadhe (पियदसि राजा मगधे).
    2. Circular Buddhist Temple (गोल मन्दिर / चैत्य): Earliest freestanding brick-and-wood circular temple in India (26 octagonal wooden pillars).
    3. Coin Hoard: 36 silver coins discovered inside a cloth pouch, including 8 punch-marked (आहत / धरण) coins and 28 coins of Indo-Greek (हिन्द-यूनानी) rulers (16 belonging to King Menander / Milinda / मिनाण्डर).
    4. Huna Invasions: Destroyed in 6th century AD by the anti-Buddhist Huna invader Mihirakula (मिहिरकुल). Xuanzang / Hiuen Tsang (ह्वेनसांग) visited Bairath in 634 AD and noted 8 dilapidated Buddhist monasteries.

# 5. Bagor (बागोर - Bhilwara) & Tilwara (तिलवाड़ा - Barmer) — Mesolithic Animal Husbandry

  • Bagor (Bhilwara): Located on the Kothari River (कोठारी नदी); excavated by Dr. V. N. Mishra (डॉ. वी. एन. मिश्र - 1967–70).
    • Significance: Largest Mesolithic (मध्य पाषाण कालीन) site in India. Contains India's earliest conclusive evidence of animal domestication (पशुपालन के प्राचीनतम साक्ष्य) dated 5000 BCE.
    • Discovered thousands of geometric microliths (सूक्ष्म पाषाण उपकरण) and a unique copper needle with an eye (छिद्रित तांबे की सुई). Local mound is called Mahasatiyan (महासतियां).
  • Tilwara (Barmer / Balotra): Mesolithic hunter-pastoral site excavated on the Luni River (लूणी नदी) bed.
⚠️ Examiner Trap: Archaeological Comparisons for RPSC Aspirants
Feature Kalibangan (कालीबंगा) Ahar (आहड़) Ganeshwar (गणेश्वर)
River (नदी) Ghaggar (घग्घर) Berach (बेड़च / बनास) Kantali (कांतली)
Houses (मकान) Sun-baked mud bricks (कच्ची ईंटें) Stone foundations + mud walls Rough stones (पत्थर के मकान - no bricks)
Pottery (मृद्भाण्ड) Red Ware with black lines Black and Red Ware (BRW - लाल-काले) Ochre-Coloured Pottery (OCP - गेरुए रंग के)
Primary Metal (धातु) Bronze & Terracotta (कांस्य) Copper smelting slag (तांबा गलाना) 99% pure refined copper tools (शुद्ध तांबा)

   • Raj Singh (राजसिंह - Raj Prashasti)

# A. Guhil & Sisodia Dynasty of Mewar (मेवाड़ का गुहिल व सिसोदिया राजवंश) 🔥 Core Exam Focus

🎙️ Faculty Lecture: The Pride of the Guhils (गुहिलों का स्वाभिमान)
"Mewar is not just a dynasty; it is a thousand-year symbol of resistance against imperial Delhi. Why? The Guhilots never considered themselves true kings! They ruled as the mere Diwans (दीवान - prime ministers) of their family deity, Lord Eklingji (श्री एकलिंगजी - Shiva). When a Sultan or Mughal Emperor sent gifts and imperial treaties demanding personal submission at the Delhi court, the Maharanas replied: 'The land belongs to Eklingji; we have no right to surrender His sovereignty.' This profound spiritual anchor made Mewar fight to the very last breath."
📖 Terminology Decoder: Warfare & Administrative Terms
  • Saka (साका): The ultimate martial sacrifice consisting of two parts:
    1. Jauhar (जौहर): Rajput noblewomen, led by the Queen, leapt into a blazing fire pit chanting Vedic hymns to preserve their honor.
    2. Kesariko / Saka (केसरिया): Facing inevitable defeat, Rajput warriors wore saffron robes, opened fort gates, and fought to the death without retreating.
  • Haraval (हरावल): The frontline vanguard of an army that absorbs the first enemy assault (In Mewar, the hereditary right to lead Haraval belonged to the Chundawats (चूण्डावत) of Salumbar / सलूम्बर).
  • Chandaval (चंदवाल): The rear guard of the army that protects supplies and the commander's flank.
  • Diwan of Eklingji (एकलिंगजी के दीवान): Mewar rulers never used the title Maharaja in front of God; they called themselves the humble servants/executors (*Diwan*) of Eklingji.

# 1. Bappa Rawal (बप्पा रावल / कालभोज) — The Foundation (734–753 AD)

  • Background & Guru: Reared in Nagda (नागदा); served the sage Harit Rishi (हरित ऋषि). Blessed by the sage, Bappa gained extraordinary military prowess.
  • Capture of Chittor (734 AD): Defeated the Mori King Manmori (मानमोरी) and established Guhil hegemony over Chittorgarh Fort (चित्तौड़गढ़ दुर्ग).
  • Capital & Deity: Made Nagda his capital; constructed the historic Eklingji Temple (एकलिंगजी मन्दिर) at Kailashpuri (कैलाशपुरी).
  • Repelling Arab Incursions: Routed invading Umayyad Arab armies, pursuing them through Sindh to Rawalpindi (Pakistan's city Rawalpindi was named after Bappa Rawal's military outpost).
  • Historian Assessment: Renowned historian C. V. Vaidya (सी. वी. वैद्य) compared Bappa Rawal to Charles Martel (चार्ल्स मार्टेल) (the legendary Frankish commander who halted the Islamic invasion of Western Europe at the Battle of Tours in 732 AD).

# 2. Jaitra Singh (जैत्रसिंह - 1213–1253 AD) & The Battle of Bhutala

  • Battle of Bhutala (भूताला का युद्ध - 1227 AD): Delhi Sultan Shamsuddin Iltutmish (शम्सुद्दीन इल्तुतमिश) invaded Mewar. Jaitra Singh completely crushed the Sultanate army near Nagda.
  • Source: Chronicled in Jaisingh Suri's (जयसिंह सूरि) historical Sanskrit drama Hammir Mardan (हम्मीर मदमर्दन).
  • Strategic Shift: Nagda was devastated during the war; Jaitra Singh wisely shifted the capital to the fortified hill of Chittorgarh (चित्तौड़गढ़).
  • Historian G. H. Ojha's (डॉ. गौरीशंकर हीराचंद ओझा) Remark: Declared Jaitra Singh's reign as the golden zenith of early Mewar military prowess.

# 3. Rawal Ratan Singh (रावल रतनसिंह) & The 1st Saka of Chittor (1303 AD) 🔥 Asked 6+ Times

  • Cause of Alauddin Khilji's (अलाउद्दीन खिलजी) Siege:
    • Strategic desire to control the trade and military highway from Delhi to Gujarat and Malwa.
    • Imperialist expansionism (Khilji wanted to become Sikandar-i-Sani / सिकंदर-ए-सानी / Second Alexander).
    • Romanticized in Malik Muhammad Jayasi’s (मलिक मुहम्मद जायसी) epic Padmavat (पद्मावत) (composed in 1540 AD) around Queen Padmini (रानी पद्मिनी).
  • The 1st Saka of Chittor (चित्तौड़ का प्रथम साका - 1303 AD):
    • Valiant generals Gora (गोरा) and Badal (बादल) led the sortie and fought to the last drop of blood.
    • Queen Padmini along with 1,600 brave women committed Jauhar (जौहर) in a massive fire pit inside Chittor Fort.
    • Khilji conquered the fort on 26 August 1303, slaughtered 30,000 civilians, handed governance to his son Khizr Khan (खिज्र खां), and renamed Chittor Khijrabad (खिज्राबाद).
    • Ratan Singh was the last ruler of the Rawal (रावल) branch of Guhils.

# 4. Rana Hammir (राणा हम्मीर - 1326–1364 AD) — The Sisodia Revival

  • Captured Chittor back from Maldeo Muchhala (मालदेव मूछाला - Songara Chauhan) in 1326 AD.
  • Founded the Sisodia Dynasty (सिसोदिया राजवंश) (named after their ancestral estate Sisoda / सिसोदा).
  • Titles & Honors:
    • "Uddharak of Mewar" (मेवाड़ का उद्धारक) (Liberator/Savior of Mewar).
    • "Vishamghati Panchanan" (विषमघाटी पंचानन) (A roaring lion in the face of daunting adversity) — inscribed in the Kirtistambha Prashasti (कीर्ति स्तम्भ प्रशस्ति).
  • Battle of Singoli (सिंगोली का युद्ध): Crushed the imperial army of Sultan Muhammad bin Tughlaq (मुहम्मद बिन तुगलक) and took his general captive. Built the Annapurna Mata (अन्नपूर्णा माता) temple in Chittor.

# 5. Maharana Kumbha (महाराणा कुम्भा - 1433–1468 AD) — The Renaissance King 🔥 Highest RPSC Frequency

🎙️ Faculty Narrative: Kumbha — Pen, Sword, and Chisel Combined (कलम, कटार और कला के धनी)
"In Indian history, you find kings who were great conquerors (like Samudragupta), kings who were great scholars (like Bhoja Paramara), and kings who were great architects (like Shah Jahan). But Maharana Kumbha was all three simultaneously! He fought 56 battles and never lost one; he built 32 hill forts; he was a master Veena (वीणा) player and composed foundational treatises on classical Indian music; and he patronized scholars who codified town planning and temple architecture."
  • Military Triumphs:
    1. Battle of Sarangpur (सारंगपुर का युद्ध - 1437 AD): Kumbha attacked Sultan Mahmud Khilji I of Malwa (महमूद खिलजी प्रथम) to punish the assassins of his father Rana Mokal (राणा मोकल). Khilji was routed, captured, kept prisoner in Chittor for 6 months, and magnanimously released without ransom. To commemorate this glorious victory, Kumbha erected the 9-story Vijay Stambh (विजय स्तम्भ - Victory Pillar).
    2. Neutralizing the Treaty of Champaner (चम्पानेर की संधि - 1456 AD): Sultan Qutbuddin of Gujarat and Mahmud Khilji of Malwa signed a secret joint military pact to divide Mewar between them. Kumbha defeated both sultanates simultaneously through brilliant flanking maneuvers.
  • Architectural Legacy:
    • Out of 84 major defensive forts protecting Mewar, Kumbha constructed 32 forts (documented by Kaviraj Shyamaldas / कविराजा श्यामलदास in Veer Vinod / वीर विनोद), including Kumbhalgarh (कुम्भलगढ़), Achalgarh (अचलगढ़), and Basantgarh (बसंतगढ़).
    • Chief Royal Architect: Mandan (मण्डन) (authored Devamurti Prakarana, Prasada Mandana, Rupa Mandana, Vastu Mandana).
  • Literary & Musical Genius:
    • Master player of the Veena (वीणा); held the royal title Abhinav Bhartacharya (अभिनव भरताचार्य) and Hindu Suratan (हिंदू सुरताण).
    • Masterworks on Music: Sangitaraj (संगीतराज) (encyclopedic work in 5 parts: Pathya, Geeta, Nritya, Vadya, Rasa / पाठ्य, गीत, नृत्य, वाद्य, रस रत्नकोष), Sangita Mimamsa (संगीत मीमांसा), Sudaprabandha (सूडप्रबंध), and Rasika Priya (रसिकप्रिया) (commentary on Jayadeva’s Gita Govinda).
    • Court Scholars: Kanha Vyas (कान्ह व्यास) (composed Eklinga Mahatmya / एकलिंग महात्म्य), Atri and Mahesh (अत्रि व महेश) (composed Kirtistambha Prashasti).
  • Tragic Death: In 1468 AD, while praying by the Kund at Kumbhalgarh, he was murdered by his own ambitious elder son, Uda (उदा / उदयसिंह प्रथम), the first patricide (पितृहन्ता) of Mewar.

# 6. Rana Sanga (राणा सांगा / संग्राम सिंह प्रथम - 1509–1528 AD) — The "Hindu-Pat" Titan 🔥 Repeated PYQ

  • Expansion & Hegemony: Sanga united all Rajput houses under one umbrella via the ancient tradition of Pati Perawan (पाती पेरवण) (inviting all rulers to fight under a common banner).
  • Victories Over Delhi & Malwa:
    • Battle of Khatoli (खातोली का युद्ध - 1517 AD): Defeated Sultan Ibrahim Lodhi of Delhi (Sanga lost an arm and was crippled in one leg).
    • Battle of Badi / Dholpur (बाड़ी का युद्ध - 1518 AD): Defeated Ibrahim Lodhi's royal generals Mian Makhan and Mian Husain.
    • Battle of Gagron (गागरोन का युद्ध - 1519 AD): Defeated Sultan Mahmud Khilji II of Malwa and captured him.
    • Battle of Bayana (बयाना का युद्ध - February 1527 AD): Sanga's vanguard smashed Babur's Mughal force under Abdul Aziz.
  • Battle of Khanwa (खानवा का युद्ध - 17 March 1527 AD):
    • Fought in Roopwas (रूपवास - Bharatpur) between Rana Sanga and Zahiruddin Muhammad Babur (बाबर).
    • Babur declared the war a holy Jihad (जिहाद), smashed his wine vessels, and repealed the Tamga (तमगा) transit tax on Muslims.
    • Why Did Sanga Lose? Babur’s combination of Ottoman-style artillery (Topkhana / तोपखाना commanded by Mustafa Rumi & Ustad Ali) and Tulghuma (तुलगुमा) flanking cavalry charges, coupled with the eleventh-hour treachery of Silhadi Tanwar (सलहदी तंवर) of Raisen.
    • Sanga was wounded by an arrow, carried to safety at Baswa (बांदीकुई, दौसा - Baswa, Dausa), and later poisoned by demoralized chieftains at Kalpi (कालपी). His cenotaph stands at Mandalgarh (माण्डलगढ़ - Bhilwara).
    • Babur described Sanga as having 80 wounds on his body (सैनिक का भग्नावशेष - Sainik ka Bhagnavesh).

# 7. Maharana Pratap (महाराणा प्रताप - 1572–1597 AD) — The Unconquered Sovereign 🔥 Must-Know Core

🗺️ Pratap's Diplomatic & Military Timeline
  • Birth: 9 May 1540 (Jyeshtha Shukla Tritiya, VS 1597) at Kumbhalgarh (Katargarh / कटारगढ़) to Rana Uday Singh & Rani Jaywanta Bai (जयवंता बाई).
  • Coronation (राज्याभिषेक): 28 Feb 1572 at Gogunda (गोगून्दा); formal Darbar held at Kumbhalgarh.
  • Akbar's 4 Diplomatic Missions (1572–73):
    1. Jalal Khan (जलाल खां - Nov 1572) $\rightarrow$ 2. Man Singh (मानसिंह - June 1573) $\rightarrow$ 3. Bhagwan Das (भगवान दास - Oct 1573) $\rightarrow$ 4. Todarmal (टोडरमल - Dec 1573). (Mnemonic: J-M-B-T / ज-मा-भा-टो)
  • Battle of Haldighati (हल्दीघाटी का युद्ध): 18 June 1576 (or 21 June per G.N. Sharma).
  • Battle of Dewair (दिवेर का युद्ध): October 1582 ("Marathon of Mewar").
  • Establishment of Chavand Capital (चावण्ड): 1585 AD (Cultural renaissance until death on 19 Jan 1597).
  • Battle of Haldighati (हल्दीघाटी का युद्ध - 18 June 1576):
    • Commanders: Imperial Mughal forces led by Kunwar Man Singh of Amer (मानसिंह कछवाहा) (assisted by Asaf Khan / आसफ खां); Mewar vanguard led by Hakim Khan Sur (हाकिम खां सूर) (an Afghan) and tribal commander Rana Poonja Bhil (राणा पूंजा भील).
    • The Climactic Charge: Pratap on his valiant stallion Chetak (चेतक) charged directly at Man Singh's elephant Mardanah (मरदाना). Chetak planted his forelegs on the elephant's trunk, allowing Pratap to hurl his spear at Man Singh.
    • Supreme Sacrifices: When Chetak was wounded and Pratap was surrounded, Jhala Bida / Jhala Manna (झाला बीदा / झाला मन्ना) donned Pratap's royal umbrella (Chhatra / छत्र) and insignia, drawing the entire Mughal fury upon himself to allow Pratap to safely retreat. Chetak leaped across the Balicha brook and died in Pratap's arms (memorial at Balicha / बलीचा).
    • Historian Designations:
      • Badayuni (बदायूनी - eyewitness) called it: Battle of Gogunda (गोगून्दा का युद्ध).
      • Abul Fazl (अबुल फजल) called it: Battle of Khamnor (खमनौर का युद्ध).
      • Col. James Tod called it: Thermopylae of Mewar (मेवाड़ की थर्मोपल्ली).
  • Battle of Dewair (दिवेर का युद्ध - October 1582) — The Turning Point:
    • Pratap and Crown Prince Amar Singh (अमरसिंह प्रथम) launched a massive counter-offensive on the Mughal military base at Dewair. Amar Singh impaled Mughal commander Sultan Khan (सुल्तान खां) and his armored horse with a single spear thrust.
    • Col. Tod hailed Dewair as the "Marathon of Mewar" (मेवाड़ का मैराथन). Following Dewair, Pratap liberated 36 Mughal checkposts, recapturing nearly all of Mewar (except Chittorgarh and Mandalgarh).
  • Chavand Capital (चावण्ड - 1585–1597):
    • Pratap defeated Luna Rathore (लूणा राठौड़), built palaces, Chamunda Mata (चामुण्डा माता) temple, and patronized the Chavand School of Painting (master artist Nasiruddin / निसारदीन).

# 8. Maharana Raj Singh (महाराणा राजसिंह - 1652–1680 AD) — The Challenger to Aurangzeb

  • Resistance Against Fundamentalism:
    • Openly opposed and wrote a scathing protest letter against Aurangzeb’s reimposition of the Jizya Tax (जजिया कर - 1679).
    • Protected Hindu culture: Safely installed the holy idols of Shrinathji at Nathdwara (श्रीनाथजी - नाथद्वारा) and Dwarkadhish at Kankroli (द्वारकाधीश - कांकरोली) when Aurangzeb ordered temple demolitions.
    • Defied Aurangzeb by marrying Princess Charumati of Kishangarh (चारुमती - किशनगढ़) whom the Mughal Emperor desired.
  • Architectural Feat — Rajsamand Lake (राजसमंद झील):
    • Constructed the magnificent Rajsamand Lake (damming the Gomti River / गोमती नदी) during a severe famine relief project.
    • Installed the world’s largest stone inscription, Raj Prashasti (राजप्रशस्ति), on 25 black marble slabs at Nau Chowki Ghat (नौ चौकी पाल) (composed in Sanskrit by Ranchhod Bhatt Tailang / रणछोड़ भट्ट तैलंग).

# B. Rathore Dynasty of Marwar & Bikaner (मारवाड़ एवं बीकानेर का राठौड़ राजवंश)

🗺️ Rathore Lineage: Marwar vs Bikaner
  • Rao Siha (राव सीहा - 13th Century): Migrated from Kannauj (कन्नौज) $\rightarrow$ Established early Rathore seat at Khed (खेड़ - Pali/Balotra).
  • Rao Chunda (राव चूण्डा): Captured Mandore (मण्डोर) from Gurjara-Pratiharas via matrimonial alliance $\rightarrow$ Made Mandore capital.
  • Rao Jodha (राव जोधा): Founded Jodhpur (1459) & built Mehrangarh Fort (मेहरानगढ़ दुर्ग).
  • Rao Bika (राव बीका): Migrated north $\rightarrow$ Founded Bikaner (1488) with Karni Mata's (करणी माता) blessing.
  • Rao Maldeo (राव मालदेव): Expanded Marwar to 58 Parganas ("Hashmatwala Raja").
  • Rao Chandrasen (राव चन्द्रसेन): Chose rugged exile over Mughal subjugation ("Pratap of Marwar").

# 1. Rao Maldeo (राव मालदेव - 1531–1562 AD) — The "Hashmatwala Raja"

  • Imperial Splendor: Persian historians (Firishta, Nizamuddin) called him "Hindustan ka Sabse Hashmatwala Raja" (हशमत वाला राजा) (The Most Powerful and Splendid Ruler of India). He won 52 battles and expanded Marwar across 58 Parganas (परगने).
  • Roothi Rani (रूठी रानी - Uma-De / उमादे): Married Princess Uma-De of Jaisalmer. Due to a misunderstanding on their wedding night, she stayed permanently estranged at Taragarh Fort (तारागढ़ दुर्ग - Ajmer), becoming famous as Roothi Rani (later performed Anumarana / सती with Maldeo’s turban).
  • Battle of Giri-Sammel / Jaitaran (गिरी-सुमेल / जैतारण का युद्ध - January 1544 AD):
    • Fought between Rao Maldeo and Afghan Emperor Sher Shah Suri (शेरशाह सूरी).
    • Sher Shah dropped forged letters near Maldeo’s tent, creating suspicion that his loyal generals Jaita (जैता) and Kumpa (कूंपा) had accepted bribes. Maldeo retreated towards Siwana (सिवाणा).
    • To clear their honor, Jaita and Kumpa with only 12,000 Rathores launched a suicidal dawn assault on Sher Shah's 80,000-strong army, almost annihilating the Afghan center.
    • Sher Shah was saved only by the timely arrival of Jalal Khan Jalwani's fresh reserves. Relieved, Sher Shah famously uttered:

      "बोलेया खिलजी शेरशाह, सुन काजी रहमान | खुदा की कसम, मैं मुट्ठी भर बाजरे के लिए सारे हिंदुस्तान की सल्तनत खो देता!" (For a handful of millet, I would have lost the empire of all Hindustan!)

# 2. Rao Chandrasen (राव चन्द्रसेन - 1562–1581 AD) — The "Forgotten Hero" 🔥 RPSC Favorite

  • Nagaur Darbar (नागौर दरबार - 1570 AD): Akbar organized a grand court at Nagaur to demand the submission of Rajput kings. Chandrasen attended, but seeing Akbar openly favoring his elder traitor brothers (Ram & Mota Raja Udai Singh), he walked out in defiance!
  • Guerrilla Warfare: Fought the Mughals for nearly two decades from the mountains of Bhadrajun (भाद्राजून), Siwana (सिवाणा), and Sojat (सोजत). Never submitted to Mughal suzerainty.
  • Historical Epithets:
    • "The Forerunner / Pioneer of Maharana Pratap" (प्रताप का अग्रगामी) (Chandrasen adopted guerrilla tactics before Pratap).
    • "Pratap of Marwar" (मारवाड़ का प्रताप) and "Bhoola Bisra Raja" (भूला-बिसरा राजा) (The Forgotten King).
    • Died in 1581 AD at Sachiyap / Saran hills (सारण की पहाड़ियां - Sojat) at age 39.

# 3. Rao Rai Singh of Bikaner (राव रायसिंह - 1574–1612 AD)

  • Built Junagarh Fort (जूनागढ़ दुर्ग) in Bikaner (architect: Minister Karamchand / कर्मचन्द).
  • During the catastrophic drought of 1577, he established feeding centers and distributed state grain freely, earning the title "Karna of Rajputana" (राजपूताने का कर्ण) from Munshi Devi Prasad (मुंशी देवी प्रसाद).

# C. Chauhan Dynasty of Shakambhari, Ajmer & Ranthambore (शाकम्भरी, अजमेर एवं रणथंभौर के चौहान)

🗺️ Chauhan Branches: From Shakambhari to Branches Across Rajasthan
$$\text{Vasudeva (वासुदेव - 551 AD - Sambhar / Shakambhari)} \longrightarrow \text{Ajayraj (अजयराज - 1113 AD - Founded Ajmer)}$$ $$\downarrow$$ $$\text{Prithviraj III (पृथ्वीराज तृतीय - 1192 Tarain)} \longrightarrow \text{Govindraj (गोविन्दराज - 1194 - Founded Ranthambore Branch)}$$ $$\downarrow$$ $$\text{Kirti Pal (कीर्तिपाल - 1181 - Founded Jalore Sonagara Branch)} \longrightarrow \text{Kanhad Dev (कान्हड़देव - 1311 Jalore Saka)}$$
  • Vasudeva Chauhan (वासुदेव चौहान - 551 AD): Founded the dynasty at Shakambhari (शाकम्भरी) and created the sacred Sambhar Salt Lake (सांभर झील) (documented in the Bijolia Rock Inscription / बिजौलिया शिलालेख 1170 AD).
  • Ajayraj Chauhan (अजयराज चौहान - 1113 AD): Founded the city of Ajaymeru (अजयमेरु - Ajmer) and built the hilltop fortress Taragarh / Garh Beetli (तारागढ़ / गढ़ बीठली). Issued silver/copper coins named Ajaypriya Dramma (अजयप्रिय द्रम्म).
  • Arnoraj (अर्णोराज / आनाजी - 1133–1155 AD): Constructed Anasagar Lake (आनासागर झील) in Ajmer to purify the soil stained with Turko-Muslim blood. Constructed the historic Varaha Temple (वराह मन्दिर) at Pushkar (पुष्कर).
  • Vigrahraj IV / Visaldev (विग्रहराज चतुर्थ / बीसलदेव - 1158–1163 AD):
    • His reign is celebrated as the Golden Age of Ajmer Chauhans (चौहानों का स्वर्णकाल).
    • Conquered Delhi from the Tomara (तोमर) rulers; established the town of Bisalpur (बीसलपुर) and Bisalpur Lake.
    • Great poet and scholar titled Kavibandhava (कविबान्धव); authored the Sanskrit drama Harikeli (हरिकेलि नाटक) (scenes from which are inscribed on stones of Adhai Din Ka Jhonpra / अढ़ाई दिन का झोंपड़ा). Court poet Somadeva (सोमदेव) authored Lalit Vigrahraja (ललित विग्रहराज).
  • Prithviraj Chauhan III / Rai Pithora (पृथ्वीराज चौहान तृतीय / राय पिथौरा - 1177–1192 AD): 🔥 Major Theme
    • Ascended the throne at age 11 under the regency of his mother Karpuri Devi (कर्पूरी देवी) and minister Kadambavasa / Kaimas (कदम्बवास / कैमास).
    • Titles: Rai Pithora (राय पिथौरा) and Dalpangula (दलपंगुल - विश्व विजेता) (World Conqueror).
    • Battle of Mahoba (महोबा का युद्ध / तुमुल युद्ध - 1182 AD): Defeated the Chandela King Paramardidev of Bundelkhand. Legendary warrior brothers Alha (आल्हा) and Udal (ऊदल) died fighting heroically.
    • First Battle of Tarain (तराइन का प्रथम युद्ध - 1191 AD): Completely routed Muhammad Ghori (मोहम्मद गौरी); Ghori was severely wounded by Govindrai of Delhi and fled in panic.
    • Second Battle of Tarain (तराइन का द्वितीय युद्ध - 1192 AD): Ghori returned with 120,000 disciplined cavalry. Using diplomatic deception and dawn raids, Ghori defeated Prithviraj, marking the establishment of the Delhi Sultanate.
    • Court Scholars: Chand Bardai (चन्दबरदाई) (Prithviraj Raso / पृथ्वीराज रासो), Jayanak (जयानक) (Prithviraj Vijaya / पृथ्वीराज विजय), Vidyapati Gaud (विद्यापति गौड़), Janardana (जनार्दन), Vishwarupa (विश्वरूप).
  • Hammir Dev Chauhan of Ranthambore (हम्मीर देव चौहान - 1282–1301 AD):
    • Famous for his stubborn chivalry (Hammir Hath / हम्मीर हठ): "तिरिया तेल हम्मीर हठ, चढ़े न दूजी बार".
    • Sheltered rebel Mongol generals Muhammad Shah (मीर मुहम्मद शाह) & Khebru (खेबरू), refusing to surrender them to Alauddin Khilji.
    • 1st Saka of Rajasthan (राजस्थान का प्रथम साका - 1301 AD): Chieftain Ranmal (रणमल) and Rati Pal (रतिपाल) betrayed Hammir; Queen Rangdevi (रानी रंगदेवी) led the first recorded water/fire Jauhar (जल/अग्नि जौहर); Hammir died in battle. Historian Amir Khusro (अमीर खुसरो) noted: "The fortress of infidelity became the home of Islam (कुफ्र का गढ़ इस्लाम का घर हो गया)."

# 📝 Chapter 1: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

Practice Q1 🎯 Model Mock Series / Hindi Granth Academy Standard
Consider the following statements regarding the prehistoric archaeological sites of Rajasthan:
1. Ganeshwar (Sikar) is situated on the bank of Kantli river and is known as the "Mother of Copper Age Civilizations".
2. Balathal (Udaipur) excavations revealed a 5-room complex and evidence of woven cotton cloth.
3. Gilund (Rajsamand) is an Ahar culture extension site where baked bricks were used along with stone structures.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(A) 1 and 2 only
(B) 2 and 3 only
(C) 1, 2 and 3 (All correct)
(D) 1 and 3 only
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) 1, 2 and 3 (All correct)
Explanation: All three statements are accurate:
• Ganeshwar (excavated by R.C. Agrawala & Vijay Kumar) yielded 99% pure copper tools and supplied copper to Harappa.
• Balathal (excavated by Dr. V.N. Mishra, 1993) yielded an 11-room fortified structure, a skeleton with leprosy signs (~2000 BCE), and spun cotton cloth.
• Gilund (excavated by B.B. Lal, 1957-58) is unique in Ahar-Banas culture for using kiln-baked bricks.
Examiner Trap: Ahar mostly used stone foundations with unbaked mud bricks, whereas Gilund used baked bricks.
Practice Q2 🎯 Springboard / Rajveer Sir Classroom Standard
Which of the following rulers of Mewar first made Chittorgarh his capital after defeating the Delhi Sultan Iltutmish in the Battle of Bhutala (1227 CE)?
(A) Rawal Samant Singh
(B) Rawal Jaitra Singh
(C) Rawal Samar Singh
(D) Rawal Tej Singh
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Rawal Jaitra Singh
Explanation: In the Battle of Bhutala (1227 CE), Rawal Jaitra Singh defeated Iltutmish's invading army. As the retreating Delhi soldiers ravaged the ancient capital Nagda, Jaitra Singh shifted the capital of Mewar to the safe and fortified hill fort of Chittorgarh. Historian Dr. G.H. Ojha praised Jaitra Singh's era as the golden age of early Mewar.
Practice Q3 🎯 Expected RPSC Statement Pattern
Regarding the Mughal-Mewar Treaty signed in February 1615 between Maharana Amar Singh I and Emperor Jahangir, which condition was NOT part of the treaty?
(A) The Guhils of Mewar agreed to enter into matrimonial alliances with the Mughal imperial dynasty.
(B) The Maharana of Mewar was exempt from personally attending the Mughal imperial court.
(C) Chittorgarh Fort was restored to Mewar on the condition that its fortifications would not be repaired.
(D) Crown Prince Karan Singh would attend the Mughal court with a contingent of 1,000 horsemen.
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) The Guhils of Mewar agreed to enter into matrimonial alliances with the Mughal imperial dynasty.
Explanation: Mewar strictly refused matrimonial alliances with the Mughals, and Jahangir accepted this exemption. Amar Singh I never attended court in person; his son Prince Karan Singh represented Mewar. Chittor was returned with the non-repair fortification clause.
Examiner Trap: Matrimonial alliance was a standard requirement for other Rajput states (Amer, Marwar, Bikaner), but Mewar was permanently exempted in the 1615 treaty.
Practice Q4 🎯 Match The Following / RPSC Advanced Standard
Match List-I (Literary Work on Hammir Chauhan) with List-II (Author):
List-I (Work)
(A) Hammir Raso (हम्मीर रासो)
(B) Hammir Mahakavya (हम्मीर महाकाव्य)
(C) Hammir Hath (हम्मीर हठ)
(D) Hammir Madmardan (हम्मीर मदमर्दन)
List-II (Author)
(i) Jaisingh Suri
(ii) Nayanchandra Suri
(iii) Chandrashekhar
(iv) Jodharaj / Sarangdhar
(A) (A)-(iv), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(i)
(B) (A)-(i), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)
(C) (A)-(iv), (B)-(iii), (C)-(ii), (D)-(i)
(D) (A)-(ii), (B)-(iv), (C)-(i), (D)-(iii)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) (A)-(iv), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(i)
Explanation:
• Hammir Raso = Jodharaj (18th century) & Sarangdhar (14th century).
• Hammir Mahakavya = Jain scholar Nayanchandra Suri.
• Hammir Hath = Chandrashekhar.
• Hammir Madmardan = Jaisingh Suri (chronicles Iltutmish's defeat by Rawal Jaitra Singh, where Hammir refers to Amir-i-Shikar/Sultan).
Practice Q5 🎯 Freedom Struggle & 1857 Special
During the 1857 uprising in Rajasthan, who was the rebel commander who defeated the joint forces of British Captain Heathcote and Jodhpur State Army under Takht Singh in the famous Battle of Bithoda (8 September 1857)?
(A) Jaidayal Bhatnagar
(B) Thakur Kushal Singh Champawat of Auwa
(C) Mehraab Khan Risaldar
(D) Rawat Keshri Singh of Salumber
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Thakur Kushal Singh Champawat of Auwa
Explanation: Thakur Kushal Singh defeated the Jodhpur-British army at Bithoda (Pali) on 8 Sept 1857 where Jodhpur commander Onar Singh was killed. Ten days later (18 Sept 1857), in the Battle of Chelawas (known as the 'Battle of Black and Whites' / गोरों-कालों का युद्ध), Kushal Singh defeated AGG George Lawrence and Political Agent Mock Mason was beheaded. Sugali Mata was the tutelary goddess of Auwa rebels.
Practice Q6 🎯 Medieval Rajput Politics / Marwar Special
After which bloody battle in January 1544 did Afghan Sultan Sher Shah Suri exclaim in relief: "For a handful of millet (Bajra), I had almost lost the empire of Hindustan!" (मैंने मुट्ठी भर बाजरे के लिए हिंदुस्तान की सल्तनत खो दी होती)?
(A) Battle of Pahooba / Saheba (बीकानेर)
(B) Battle of Dharmat (धरमत)
(C) Battle of Merta (मेड़ता)
(D) Battle of Sammel / Giri-Sumel (जैतारण, पाली)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Battle of Sammel / Giri-Sumel (जैतारण, पाली)
Explanation: In the Battle of Giri-Sumel (5 Jan 1544, Jaitaran Pali), Rao Maldev retreated due to Sher Shah's forged letters, but his brave commanders Jaita and Kumpa fought with unparalleled ferocity and almost wiped out Sher Shah's massive army before reserve Afghan troops under Jalal Khan Jalwani arrived.
Practice Q7 🎯 Prajamandal & Freedom Awakening
Match List-I (Prajamandal) with List-II (Key Founder / Leader):
List-I (Prajamandal)
(A) Jaipur Prajamandal (1931/1938)
(B) Mewar Prajamandal (1938)
(C) Bikaner Prajamandal (1936)
(D) Kota Prajamandal (1939)
List-II (Founder)
(i) Magharam Vaidya (कलकत्ता में स्थापित)
(ii) Pt. Nayanuram Sharma & Abhinna Hari
(iii) Kapoori Chand Patni & Jamnalal Bajaj
(iv) Manikya Lal Verma & Balwant Singh Mehta
(A) (A)-(i), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)
(B) (A)-(iv), (B)-(iii), (C)-(i), (D)-(ii)
(C) (A)-(iii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(ii)
(D) (A)-(iii), (B)-(iv), (C)-(i), (D)-(ii)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) (A)-(iii), (B)-(iv), (C)-(i), (D)-(ii)
Explanation:
• Jaipur Prajamandal: Founded 1931 by Kapoori Chand Patni; reorganized 1938 by Jamnalal Bajaj & Hiralal Shastri.
• Mewar Prajamandal: Founded 24 April 1938 by Manikya Lal Verma (Balwant Singh Mehta as President).
• Bikaner Prajamandal: Established in Calcutta in 1936 by Magharam Vaidya.
• Kota Prajamandal: Founded 1939 by Pt. Nayanuram Sharma and Abhinna Hari.
Practice Q8 🎯 Rajasthan Integration Stages
During the 5th stage of Rajasthan's integration on 15 May 1949 (United Greater Rajasthan / संयुक्त वृहत् राजस्थान), which territory was merged with Greater Rajasthan on the recommendation of the Dr. Shankarrao Deo Committee?
(A) Sunel Tappa
(B) Ajmer-Merwara
(C) Matsya Union (Alwar, Bharatpur, Dholpur, Karauli)
(D) Sirohi (except Abu-Delwara)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Matsya Union (Alwar, Bharatpur, Dholpur, Karauli)
Explanation: Dr. Shankarrao Deo Committee (members: Dr. Shankarrao Deo, R.K. Sidhwa, Prabhu Dayal Himmat Singka) ascertained public opinion in Bharatpur and Dholpur, recommending merger of Matsya Union into Greater Rajasthan on 15 May 1949. Shobharam Kumawat was inducted into the cabinet.

# 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)

🎯 Inscriptions — Bijolia Rock Inscription 1170 AD — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & College Lecturer
The Bijolia Rock Inscription (1170 AD), composed by Gunabhadra during the reign of Someshwara Chauhan, provides which crucial historical information about the Chauhans?
(A) It traces the origin of Chauhans to Vatsagotra Brahmins (वत्सगोत्रीय ब्राह्मण) and mentions ancient city names like Jabalipur, Shakambhari, and Shrimal
(B) It describes the naval expeditions of Chauhans in Gujarat
(C) It records the first use of gunpowder in Rajputana
(D) It mentions the construction of the Vijay Stambha
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) It traces the origin of Chauhans to Vatsagotra Brahmins (वत्सगोत्रीय ब्राह्मण) and mentions ancient city names like Jabalipur, Shakambhari, and Shrimal
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The Bijolia inscription (1170 AD, engraved on a rock near Parshvanath temple in Bijolia, Bhilwara) describes the Shakambhari Chauhans as *Vatsagotra Brahmins* and names Vasudeva Chauhan as the founder who created Sambhar Lake in 551 AD.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B)? Chauhans were land-based rulers in Ajmer, Sambhar, and Ranthambore.
  • Why not (C)? Gunpowder was introduced in North India during Babur's era (1526).
  • Why not (D)? Vijay Stambha was built by Maharana Kumbha in 1448 AD.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Chauhans as Vatsagotra Brahmins + Sambhar Lake created by Vasudeva (551 AD) = Bijolia Inscription (1170 AD).
🎯 Inscriptions — Chirwa Inscription 1273 AD — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade
The Chirwa Inscription (चीरवा का शिलालेख - 1273 AD) found near Udaipur provides authentic information regarding which Guhil rulers of Mewar?
(A) Bappa Rawal and Allat
(B) Padmasimha, Jaitra Singh, Tej Singh, and Samar Singh (पद्मसिंह, जैत्रसिंह, तेजसिंह एवं समरसिंह)
(C) Rana Kumbha and Rana Sanga
(D) Maharana Pratap and Amar Singh
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Padmasimha, Jaitra Singh, Tej Singh, and Samar Singh (पद्मसिंह, जैत्रसिंह, तेजसिंह एवं समरसिंह)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Composed by Ratnaprabha Suri and carved by Kelhana in 1273 AD, the Chirwa inscription chronicles the lineage of Mewar Guhils from Padmasimha to Samar Singh, praising Jaitra Singh's victory over the rulers of Malwa, Gujarat, and the Delhi Sultanate (Iltutmish).

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Bappa Rawal and Allat are mentioned in the *Atpur / Ahar Inscription (977 AD)*.
  • Why not (C)? Kumbha is detailed in the *Kumbhalgarh Prashasti (1460 AD)*.
  • Why not (D)? Pratap and Amar Singh are detailed in the *Raj Prashasti (1676 AD)*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Guhil rulers Jaitra Singh, Tej Singh, Samar Singh = Chirwa Inscription (1273 AD); Allat & 977 AD = Atpur Inscription.
🎯 Civilizations — Balathal (Udaipur) — Deep Fact Check RPSC Assistant Professor & 1st Grade
Which Chalcolithic archaeological site in Vallabhnagar (Udaipur), excavated by Dr. V.N. Misra in 1993, yielded an 11-room fort-like mud-brick structure, evidence of woven cotton cloth, and a 4,000-year-old human skeleton showing the earliest evidence of Leprosy (कुष्ठ रोग)?
(A) Gilund (गिलूण्ड - Rajsamand)
(B) Ojhiyana (ओझियाना - Bhilwara)
(C) Balathal (बालाथल, उदयपुर)
(D) Bagor (बागोर - Bhilwara)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Balathal (बालाथल, उदयपुर)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Excavations at **Balathal** revealed an extensive Ahar-Banas culture settlement with a fortified 11-room complex, copper axes, chert blades, a piece of woven cloth, and human skeletal remains proving the existence of leprosy in India by 2000 BC.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Gilund? Gilund on the Banas River yielded burnt brick walls and terracotta animal figurines.
  • Why not (B) Ojhiyana? Ojhiyana in Bhilwara is known for white-painted terracotta bull figurines.
  • Why not (D) Bagor? Bagor on the Kothari River yielded the earliest microliths and animal husbandry evidence.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: 11-room fort structure + Woven cloth + Leprosy skeleton = Balathal; Earliest animal husbandry = Bagor.
🎯 Civilizations — Sunari & Jodhpura (Iron Smelting) — Deep Fact Check RPSC EO/RO & 2nd Grade
At which two ancient archaeological sites in Rajasthan were ancient iron-smelting blast furnaces (लोहा गलाने की प्राचीनतम भट्ठियां) discovered?
(A) Kalibangan and Ahar
(B) Bagor and Tilwara
(C) Ganeshwar and Bairath
(D) Sunari (Jhunjhunu) and Jodhpura (Jaipur / Kotputli)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Sunari (Jhunjhunu) and Jodhpura (Jaipur / Kotputli)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Sunari (Jhunjhunu)** on the Kantali River yielded India's oldest iron-smelting blast furnaces with tuyeres, along with iron arrowheads and bowls. **Jodhpura (on Sabi River, Jaipur)** also yielded furnaces, iron weapons, and elephant tusk artifacts from the Black-and-Red Ware to PGW periods.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Kalibangan was Bronze Age; Ahar was Chalcolithic (copper smelting).
  • Why not (B)? Bagor and Tilwara were Mesolithic microlithic sites.
  • Why not (C)? Ganeshwar was copper-centric (99% pure copper).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Iron-smelting blast furnaces = Sunari (Jhunjhunu) & Jodhpura (Jaipur); Copper smelting furnaces = Ahar (Udaipur).
🎯 Civilizations — Bayana (Gupta Gold Coin Hoard) — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
Where was the largest hoard of Gupta-period gold coins (गुप्तकालीन स्वर्ण सिक्कों का विशाल भण्डार - 1,821 coins) in Rajasthan discovered in 1946 by the Maharaja of Bharatpur?
(A) Bayana / Nagla Chhela (बयाना, भरतपुर)
(B) Rairh (रेढ़ - Tonk)
(C) Naliasar (नलियासर - Sambhar)
(D) Nagar (नगर - Uniyara)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Bayana / Nagla Chhela (बयाना, भरतपुर)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Bayana Hoard (1,821 Gupta gold coins)** discovered at Nagla Chhela near Bayana (Bharatpur) is the largest hoard of Gupta gold coins in Indian history, representing issues from Chandragupta I, Samudragupta, Chandragupta II (Vikramaditya), to Skandagupta.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Rairh? Rairh (Tonk) yielded 3,075 silver punch-marked coins ('Tata Nagar of Ancient India').
  • Why not (C) Naliasar? Naliasar (Sambhar) yielded Kushan and Gupta bronze/copper coins.
  • Why not (D) Nagar? Nagar (Tonk) yielded Malwa coins.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Gupta gold coin hoard = Bayana (Bharatpur); Silver punch-marked hoard (3,075 coins) = Rairh (Tonk); Indo-Greek Menander coins = Bairath (Jaipur).
🎯 Civilizations — Kurada (Town of Metal Implements) — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & SI
Which archaeological site in Didwana-Kuchaman (Nagaur), excavated in 1934, is famously known in Indian archaeology as the 'Town of Metal Implements' (धातु नगरी / ताम्र उपकरणों का नगर) for yielding 103 copper artifacts including spouted bronze vessels?
(A) Tilwara (तिलवाड़ा - Barmer)
(B) Kurada (कुराड़ा, नागौर)
(C) Ojhiyana (ओझियाना - Bhilwara)
(D) Gilund (गिलूण्ड - Rajsamand)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Kurada (कुराड़ा, नागौर)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Kurada (Didwana/Nagaur)** was excavated in 1934 and unearthed 103 copper artifacts, including unique Mediterranean/Roman-influenced spouted vessels (*Pan-patra*), earning it the title *Town of Metal Implements*.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Tilwara? Tilwara on the Luni River is a Mesolithic animal husbandry site.
  • Why not (C) Ojhiyana? Ojhiyana is an Ahar-Banas culture site in Bhilwara.
  • Why not (D) Gilund? Gilund is in Rajsamand on the Banas River.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Kurada (Nagaur) = Town of Metal Implements / Spouted bowls; Rairh (Tonk) = Tata Nagar; Ganeshwar (Neem Ka Thana) = Mother of Copper Cultures.
🎯 Amer Dynasty — Raja Man Singh I (1589–1614 AD) — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & RAS Pre
Which famous idol of Shila Devi (शीला देवी), installed in the Amer Fort temple by Raja Man Singh I, was brought after defeating King Kedar of Jasore in which province?
(A) Kabul / Afghanistan
(B) Bihar
(C) Eastern Bengal (पूर्वी बंगाल - Jessore / जसोर)
(D) Orissa
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Eastern Bengal (पूर्वी बंगाल - Jessore / जसोर)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: During his governorship of Bengal, Raja Man Singh I defeated **King Kedar of Jessore (East Bengal)** and brought the black stone image of **Shila Devi** to Amer in 1604 AD, consecrating it as the Kuldevi of the Kachhwahas. Man Singh also founded Mansinghpur in Bengal and Vaikunthpur in Bihar.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Kabul? From Kabul, Man Singh brought the craft of **Blue Pottery** and subdued the 5 Afghan tribes (Pachranga flag).
  • Why not (B) Bihar? Man Singh made Rohtasgarh his administrative center in Bihar.
  • Why not (D) Orissa? Man Singh conquered Orissa in 1592 and secured the Jagannath Temple at Puri.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Shila Devi idol = Brought from Bengal (King Kedar of Jessore) by Man Singh I; Jamway Mata = Original Kuldevi of Kachhwahas.
🎯 Amer Dynasty — Mirza Raja Jai Singh & Treaty of Purandar 1665 — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & 2nd Grade
On 11 June 1665, Mirza Raja Jai Singh of Amer compelled Maratha ruler Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj to sign the historic Treaty of Purandar (पुरन्दर की संधि). How many forts did Shivaji surrender to the Mughals under this treaty?
(A) 12 forts out of 35
(B) 8 forts
(C) 35 forts completely
(D) 23 forts out of 35 (having an annual revenue of 4 lakh Huns)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) 23 forts out of 35 (having an annual revenue of 4 lakh Huns)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Under the **Treaty of Purandar (11 June 1665)** negotiated by Mirza Raja Jai Singh on behalf of Aurangzeb, Shivaji surrendered **23 out of his 35 forts** (with annual yield of 400,000 Huns) and agreed to send his son Sambhaji to the Mughal court with a Mansab of 5,000.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? 12 forts were retained by Shivaji Maharaj.
  • Why not (B)? 8 was not the agreed term.
  • Why not (C)? Shivaji kept 12 strategic forts for himself.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Treaty of Purandar (1665) = Mirza Raja Jai Singh & Shivaji Maharaj; Forts surrendered = 23; Forts kept = 12.
🎯 Amer Dynasty — Sawai Jai Singh II & 5 Observatories (Jantar Mantar) — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & EO/RO
Between 1724 and 1734, Sawai Jai Singh II constructed 5 stone astronomical observatories (Jantar Mantar) across India. Which of the following cities was NOT among these 5 locations?
(A) Agra (आगरा)
(B) Delhi and Jaipur
(C) Ujjain and Varanasi (Banaras)
(D) Mathura
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Agra (आगरा)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Sawai Jai Singh II built 5 Jantar Mantar observatories: **1. Delhi (1724 - oldest), 2. Jaipur (1728 - largest / UNESCO 2010), 3. Ujjain, 4. Varanasi, and 5. Mathura**. No observatory was built in **Agra**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B)? Delhi was the first and Jaipur was the largest observatory.
  • Why not (C)? Ujjain and Varanasi both contain Sawai Jai Singh's observatories.
  • Why not (D)? Mathura had an observatory built inside the old fort (now ruined).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: 5 Observatories = Delhi, Jaipur, Ujjain, Varanasi, Mathura. (Agra, Jodhpur, and Udaipur are TRAPS!).
🎯 Amer Dynasty — Sawai Pratap Singh & Hawa Mahal 1799 — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & 2nd Grade
The 5-storeyed iconic Hawa Mahal of Jaipur, built in 1799 by Sawai Pratap Singh and designed by architect Lal Chand Usta, has how many intricately carved jharokhas/windows, and what is its top 5th floor named?
(A) 365 windows; top floor: Anand Mandir
(B) 953 jharokhas/windows; top floor: Hawa Mandir (हवा मंदिर)
(C) 500 windows; top floor: Surya Mandir
(D) 1,444 windows; top floor: Prakash Mandir
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) 953 jharokhas/windows; top floor: Hawa Mandir (हवा मंदिर)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Built in 1799 resembling Lord Krishna's crown (*Mukut*), Hawa Mahal has **953 casements / Jharokhas**. Its 5 storeys from bottom to top are: **1. Sharad / Pratap Mandir, 2. Ratan Mandir, 3. Vichitra Mandir, 4. Prakash Mandir, and 5. Hawa Mandir**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? 365 is the days in a solar year.
  • Why not (C)? Surya Mandir is a temple in Galta/Jhalrapatan.
  • Why not (D)? 1,444 is the number of pillars at Ranakpur Jain Temple.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Hawa Mahal (1799) = 5 storeys + 953 Jharokhas + Architect Lal Chand Usta; 5 floors = Sharad, Ratan, Vichitra, Prakash, Hawa.
🎯 Bikaner Dynasty — Maharaja Rai Singh & Junagarh Fort — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & 1st Grade
Which ruler of Bikaner, titled 'Karan of the Kaliyug' (कलयुग का कर्ण) by Munshi Devi Prasad and author of 'Rai Singh Prashasti', constructed the grand Junagarh Fort ('Zameen ka Zewar') between 1589 and 1594?
(A) Rao Bika (1488 AD)
(B) Maharaja Anup Singh
(C) Maharaja Rai Singh (महाराजा रायसिंह - 1571–1612 AD)
(D) Maharaja Ganga Singh
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Maharaja Rai Singh (महाराजा रायसिंह - 1571–1612 AD)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Maharaja Rai Singh** built Junagarh Fort under the supervision of his prime minister **Karam Chand**. Munshi Devi Prasad praised Rai Singh's boundless charity by conferring the title **'Rajputane ka Karan' (राजपूताने का कर्ण)**. In 1594, he installed the *Rai Singh Prashasti* composed by Jain monk Jaita.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Rao Bika? Rao Bika founded Bikaner city on Akshaya Tritiya in 1488 AD.
  • Why not (B) Anup Singh? Maharaja Anup Singh established the *Anup Sanskrit Library* and was titled *Mahi Maratib* by Aurangzeb.
  • Why not (D) Ganga Singh? Maharaja Ganga Singh built the Gang Canal (1927) and Lalgarh Palace.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Rajputane ka Karan = Rai Singh (by Munshi Devi Prasad); Kaliyug ka Karan = Rao Lunkaran (by Jaysom in Karmachand Vamshotkirtankam).
🎯 Bikaner Dynasty — Maharaja Anup Singh & Mahi Maratib — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & EO/RO
Mughal Emperor Aurangzeb conferred the supreme military title 'Mahi Maratib' (माही मरातिब) upon which scholar-king of Bikaner who rescued thousands of ancient Sanskrit manuscripts during the Deccan campaigns and founded the Anup Sanskrit Library?
(A) Rao Kalyanmal
(B) Maharaja Ratan Singh
(C) Maharaja Surat Singh
(D) Maharaja Anup Singh (महाराजा अनूप सिंह - 1669–1698 AD)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Maharaja Anup Singh (महाराजा अनूप सिंह - 1669–1698 AD)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Maharaja Anup Singh** was a scholar and warrior who fought in the Deccan wars (Golconda and Bijapur). Aurangzeb awarded him the emblem **'Mahi Maratib'**. Anup Singh authored *Anupodaya, Kam Prabodh, Shradh Prayog Chintamani* and patronized court scholar Bhavabhatta.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Kalyanmal? Rao Kalyanmal accepted Akbar's suzerainty at the 1570 Nagaur Darbar.
  • Why not (B) Ratan Singh? Ratan Singh signed the 1818 treaty with the British.
  • Why not (C) Surat Singh? Surat Singh conquered Bhatner on a Tuesday in 1805 and renamed it Hanumangarh.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Mahi Maratib + Anup Sanskrit Library = Maharaja Anup Singh; 1570 Nagaur Darbar attendee = Kalyanmal.
🎯 Ancient Inscriptions — Vasantgarh Inscription 625 AD — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & College Lecturer
Which is the earliest epigraphic inscription in Rajasthan that records the term 'Rajasthaniyaditya' (राजस्थानीयादित्य), dated to 625 AD (Vikram Samvat 682) during the reign of King Varmalat?
(A) Ghosundi Stone Inscription (घोसुण्डी शिलालेख - 2nd Century BC)
(B) Bijolia Rock Inscription (बिजोलिया शिलालेख - 1170 AD)
(C) Vasantgarh Inscription of Sirohi (बसंतगढ़ शिलालेख, सिरोही - 625 AD)
(D) Chirwa Inscription (चीरवा शिलालेख - 1273 AD)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Vasantgarh Inscription of Sirohi (बसंतगढ़ शिलालेख, सिरोही - 625 AD)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Vasantgarh Inscription (625 AD / VS 682)** engraved in the Khimel Mata / Khemkari Mata temple at Vasantgarh (Sirohi) during the reign of King Varmalat of Chawda dynasty contains the earliest known epigraphic occurrence of the word *'Rajasthaniyaditya'*.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Ghosundi? The Ghosundi Inscription (Nagari, Chittorgarh - 2nd Century BC in Brahmi script) is the earliest epigraphic record in Rajasthan mentioning the **Bhagavata / Vaishnava cult** and Ashvamedha sacrifice by King Gajayana Sarvatata.
  • Why not (B) Bijolia? The Bijolia Inscription (1170 AD, Gunabhadra composer under Someshwara Chauhan) provides the genealogy of Chauhans declaring them *Vatsagotra Brahmins* and names ancient cities (Jabalipur, Shakambhari, Shrimal).
  • Why not (D) Chirwa? The Chirwa Inscription (1273 AD) gives details about the Guhil rulers of Mewar (Padmasimha, Jaitra Singh, Tej Singh, Samar Singh).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Earliest word 'Rajasthaniyaditya' = Vasantgarh (625 AD); Earliest Vaishnava proof = Ghosundi (2nd C. BC); Chauhan genealogy = Bijolia (1170 AD).
🎯 Ancient Inscriptions — Ghosundi Inscription (Vaishnava Cult) — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade
The Ghosundi Inscription (घोसुण्डी शिलालेख, चित्तौड़गढ़), discovered by Dr. D.R. Bhandarkar and inscribed in Brahmi script and Sanskrit language in the 2nd century BC, provides the earliest epigraphic evidence in Rajasthan for which religious faith?
(A) Jain Digambara Sect
(B) Pashupata Shaivite Sect
(C) Mahayana Buddhism
(D) Vaishnava / Bhagavata Cult and Ashvamedha Yajna (भागवत/वैष्णव धर्म एवं अश्वमेध यज्ञ)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Vaishnava / Bhagavata Cult and Ashvamedha Yajna (भागवत/वैष्णव धर्म एवं अश्वमेध यज्ञ)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The Ghosundi inscription records the construction of a stone enclosure (*Pujashila Prakara*) for the worship of deities **Sankarshana (Balarama) and Vasudeva (Krishna)** by King Sarvatata of the Gajayana lineage, marking the earliest epigraph for Vaishnavism in Rajasthan.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Jainism? Jain inscriptions abound at Ranakpur (1439 AD) and Mount Abu Dilwara.
  • Why not (B) Pashupata Shaivism? Earliest Pashupata Shaiva evidence is recorded in the Eklingji / Harshnath inscriptions.
  • Why not (C) Buddhism? Earliest Buddhist evidence is found at Bairath (Bhabru Edict of Ashoka).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Ghosundi Inscription = 2nd Century BC + Brahmi Script + Sankarshana & Vasudeva (Vaishnavism) + King Sarvatata.
🎯 Ahar Civilization — Alternate Names & River — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & EO/RO
The proto-historic site of Ahar (आहड़, उदयपुर) is situated on the banks of which river, and by what ancient name was it mentioned in 10th–11th century epigraphs and local folklore?
(A) Berach / Ahar River; ancient name: Aghatpur (आघाटपुर) / Tamravati Nagari, locally 'Dhoolkot' (धूलकोट)
(B) Kantali River; ancient name: Matsyapuri
(C) Kothari River; ancient name: Madhyamika
(D) Ghaggar River; ancient name: Saraswati
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Berach / Ahar River; ancient name: Aghatpur (आघाटपुर) / Tamravati Nagari, locally 'Dhoolkot' (धूलकोट)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Ahar is located on the **Ahar / Berach River** (tributary of Banas). Ancient inscriptions call it **Aghatpur (आघाटपुर)** or **Tamravati Nagari (ताम्रवती नगरी)** due to profuse copper wealth, and local villagers call the 500-meter-long mound **'Dhoolkot' (धूलकोट - Mound of Dust)**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B)? The Kantali river in Sikar/Neem Ka Thana flows by **Ganeshwar**.
  • Why not (C)? The Kothari river in Bhilwara flows by **Bagor**.
  • Why not (D)? The Ghaggar river in Hanumangarh flows by **Kalibangan**.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Ahar = Berach river + Dhoolkot + Aghatpur + Tamravati Nagari; Ganeshwar = Kantali river + Tamrasanchayi Sanskriti.
🎯 Ahar Civilization — Copper Smelting & Banasian Bull — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
Which of the following artifact discoveries specifically belongs to the Chalcolithic site of Ahar (आहड़)?
(A) Wooden drainage pipes and ploughed agricultural field
(B) Copper smelting furnaces (तांबा गलाने की भट्ठियां) and terracotta humped bull figurines named 'Banasian Bull' (बनासियन बुल)
(C) Ashokan Bhabru rock edict and Indo-Greek coins of Menander
(D) Pure copper fish-hooks (मछली पकड़ने का कांटा)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Copper smelting furnaces (तांबा गलाने की भट्ठियां) and terracotta humped bull figurines named 'Banasian Bull' (बनासियन बुल)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Excavations at Ahar revealed **cylindrical copper smelting furnaces**, stone anvils, crucibles, 6 copper coins (one depicting Greek Apollo holding arrows), and terracotta figurines of humped bulls styled by archaeologists as the **'Banasian Bull'**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Wooden drains and ploughed fields belong to **Kalibangan**.
  • Why not (C)? Bhabru edict and Menander coins belong to **Bairath (Jaipur)**.
  • Why not (D)? Copper fish-hooks belong to **Ganeshwar (Neem Ka Thana)**.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Copper smelting furnaces & Banasian Bull = Ahar; Copper fish-hooks = Ganeshwar; Craniotomy child skull = Kalibangan; Bhabru Edict = Bairath.
🎯 Ganeshwar Civilization — Mother of Copper Cultures — Deep Fact Check RPSC 2nd Grade & SI
Why is the prehistoric site of Ganeshwar (गणेश्वर, नीम का थाना) on the Kantali River celebrated as the 'Mother of Copper Cultures' (ताम्रयुगीन संस्कृतियों की जननी)?
(A) Because it had the earliest iron smelting blast furnace in Asia
(B) Because it was the capital of Emperor Ashoka
(C) Because its copper artifacts (fishhooks, arrowheads, spears, celts) possess an astonishing 99% pure copper purity and supplied raw copper/tools to Indus sites like Harappa & Mohenjodaro
(D) Because it yielded 100 gold coins of Chandragupta II
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Because its copper artifacts (fishhooks, arrowheads, spears, celts) possess an astonishing 99% pure copper purity and supplied raw copper/tools to Indus sites like Harappa & Mohenjodaro
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Excavated in 1977 by **Ratan Chandra Agrawala (R.C. Agrawala)** and Vijay Kumar, Ganeshwar dates back to c. 2800 BC. The tools found here contain **99% pure copper** mined from the nearby Khetri copper belt, acting as the primary metallurgic supplier to mature Harappan centers.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Ancient iron-smelting blast furnaces were found at **Sunari (Jhunjhunu)** and **Jodhpura (Jaipur)**.
  • Why not (B)? Ashoka's capital was Pataliputra; Bairath was a regional Buddhist center.
  • Why not (D)? The largest hoard of Gupta gold coins in Rajasthan was discovered at **Bayana (Bharatpur)**.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: 99% Pure Copper + Fishhook + R.C. Agrawala 1977 = Ganeshwar; Copper Smelting Furnaces = Ahar; Gupta Gold Coin Hoard = Bayana.
🎯 Bairath Civilization — Bhabru Inscription & Captain Burt — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & College Lecturer
In 1837, British army officer Captain Burt (कैप्टन बर्ट) discovered the famous minor rock edict of Emperor Ashoka at Bijak ki Pahadi (Bairath), which explicitly proclaimed Ashoka's faith in the Triratna (Buddha, Dhamma, Sangha). What is this edict named?
(A) Rumminidei Pillar Edict
(B) Girnar Rock Edict
(C) Maski Rock Inscription
(D) Bhabru Rock Edict (भाब्रू शिलालेख - currently preserved in Asiatic Society Museum, Kolkata)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Bhabru Rock Edict (भाब्रू शिलालेख - currently preserved in Asiatic Society Museum, Kolkata)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Captain Burt discovered the **Bhabru Rock Inscription (भाब्रू शिलालेख)** in 1837 on Bijak-ki-Pahadi at Bairath (Jaipur). It was chiseled out and transported to the **Asiatic Society of Bengal Museum in Kolkata** in 1840. In this inscription, Ashoka addresses the Sangha and expresses supreme reverence for the *Buddha, Dhamma, and Sangha*.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Rumminidei? Rumminidei Pillar (Nepal) records Ashoka's tax reduction in Lumbini (birthplace of Buddha).
  • Why not (B) Girnar? Girnar (Gujarat) contains Major Rock Edicts and Rudradaman's Junagadh inscription.
  • Why not (C) Maski? Maski Inscription (Karnataka) is famous for naming Ashoka directly as *Devanampiya Ashoka*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Bhabru Edict = Discovered 1837 by Capt. Burt at Bijak ki Pahadi (Bairath) -> Kept at Kolkata Museum.
🎯 Bairath Civilization — Greek King Menander & Mihirakula — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & EO/RO
Out of the 36 ancient silver punch-marked coins found wrapped in a hand-spun cotton cloth at Bairath, how many coins belonged specifically to the Indo-Greek ruler Menander (मिलिंद), and which Huna invader subsequently destroyed the Buddhist monasteries here?
(A) 16 coins; invader: Huna king Mihirakula (हूण शासक मिहिरकुल)
(B) 8 coins; invader: Toramana
(C) 28 coins; invader: Mahmud Ghaznavi
(D) 36 coins; invader: Bakhtiyar Khilji
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) 16 coins; invader: Huna king Mihirakula (हूण शासक मिहिरकुल)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Excavations at Bairath yielded 36 silver coins in a clay pot, of which 8 were Indian punch-marked coins and 28 were Indo-Greek kings (with **16 coins belonging to King Menander** alone). In the 6th century AD, fanatic **Huna ruler Mihirakula** invaded and destroyed all the Buddhist monasteries and circular Chaityas at Bairath (as corroborated by Chinese pilgrim Xuanzang in 634 AD).

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B)? Toramana was Mihirakula's father who fought Bhanugupta (Eran inscription).
  • Why not (C)? Mahmud Ghaznavi destroyed temples in the 11th century (Gogaji/Somnath).
  • Why not (D)? Bakhtiyar Khilji destroyed Nalanda and Vikramashila universities in Bihar.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Menander Coins = 16 coins at Bairath; Buddhist Monasteries destroyed by = Huna king Mihirakula; Chinese traveler = Xuanzang (Hiuen Tsang).
🎯 Prehistoric Sites — Bagor & Tilwara (Earliest Animal Husbandry) — Deep Fact Check RPSC 2nd Grade & Assistant Professor
Which Mesolithic site on the banks of the Kothari River in Bhilwara, excavated by Dr. V.N. Misra at the Mahasathiyan Mound (महासतियों का टीला), has yielded the earliest archaeological evidence of Animal Husbandry (पशुपालन के प्राचीनतम साक्ष्य) in India alongside Adamgarh (MP)?
(A) Gilund (गिलूण्ड - Rajsamand)
(B) Bagor (बागोर, भीलवाड़ा)
(C) Balathal (बालाथल - Udaipur)
(D) Ojhiyana (ओझियाना - Bhilwara)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Bagor (बागोर, भीलवाड़ा)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Bagor (बागोर)** on the Kothari River in Bhilwara (excavated 1967–70 by V.N. Misra and L.S. Leshnik) is India's largest Mesolithic micro-lithic site. It provided the **earliest scientific evidence of cattle and sheep domestication/animal husbandry** dating back to 5000–4000 BC.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Gilund? Gilund on the Banas river in Rajsamand is an Ahar culture site famous for burnt brick structures and terracotta toys.
  • Why not (C) Balathal? Balathal (Udaipur) yielded an 11-room fort structure, copper tools, and a 4000-year-old skeleton showing earliest leprosy affliction.
  • Why not (D) Ojhiyana? Ojhiyana (Bhilwara) is an Ahar-affiliated hill-slope site yielding distinctive white-painted terracotta bull figurines.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Earliest Animal Husbandry = Bagor (Bhilwara) & Tilwara (Barmer); 11-room fort-like structure = Balathal; White painted bull = Ojhiyana.
🎯 Ancient Metallurgy — Rairh 'Tata Nagar of Ancient India' — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
Why is the ancient archaeological site of Rairh (रेढ़, निवाई - टोंक) on the Dheel River famously celebrated as the 'Tata Nagar of Ancient India' (प्राचीन भारत का टाटा नगर)?
(A) Because Jamshedji Tata built India's first steel plant here in 1907
(B) Because it produced railway tracks for British India
(C) Because Dr. K.N. Puri's excavations unearthed Asia's largest single hoard of ancient Punch-marked / Silver Karshapana coins (3,075 coins) along with massive iron tools and smelters
(D) Because it was the royal treasury of Emperor Harsha
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Because Dr. K.N. Puri's excavations unearthed Asia's largest single hoard of ancient Punch-marked / Silver Karshapana coins (3,075 coins) along with massive iron tools and smelters
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Excavated by **Dr. K.N. Puri (1938–1940)**, Rairh yielded **3,075 silver punch-marked coins (आहत/धरण/कार्षापण सिक्के)** and extensive iron slag, furnace tuyeres, iron weapons, and metallurgical workshops, earning the title **'Tata Nagar of Ancient India'**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Jamshedpur (Tatanagar) in Jharkhand was established in 1907 by Jamshedji Tata.
  • Why not (B)? Rairh is a 2000-year-old ancient historical site.
  • Why not (D)? Harsha's capital was Kannauj in UP.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Tata Nagar of Ancient India = Rairh (Tonk) + 3,075 Punch-marked coins + Dr. K.N. Puri; Iron Blast Furnace = Sunari (Jhunjhunu).
🎯 Mewar Dynasty — Bappa Rawal (734 AD & Eklingji) — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre
According to historical traditions, from which ruler of the Mori Rajput dynasty did Bappa Rawal (कालभोज / बप्पा रावल) capture Chittorgarh Fort in 734 AD under the blessings of his spiritual guru Harit Rishi (हारीत ऋषि)?
(A) Raja Dahir
(B) Pulakeshin II
(C) Yashovarman
(D) Man Mori (मान मोरी - Mori king of Chittor)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Man Mori (मान मोरी - Mori king of Chittor)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Bappa Rawal (real name *Khalbhoj*) tended cows for ascetic sage **Harit Rishi**. With the sage's blessings, Bappa defeated **King Man Mori in 734 AD**, conquered Chittor, and established **Eklingji (Lord Shiva)** as the supreme sovereign deity of Mewar (with Mewar Maharanas ruling as his *Diwans*). Bappa issued a gold coin weighing 115 grains.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Raja Dahir? Raja Dahir was the ruler of Sindh who fought Muhammad bin Qasim in 712 AD.
  • Why not (B) Pulakeshin II? Pulakeshin II was the Chalukya ruler who defeated Emperor Harsha on the Narmada.
  • Why not (C) Yashovarman? Yashovarman was the king of Kannauj who patronized poet Bhavabhuti.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Bappa Rawal captured Chittor in 734 AD from = Man Mori; Guru = Harit Rishi; Supreme Deity = Eklingji; Gold coin = 115 grains.
🎯 Mewar Dynasty — Jaitra Singh & Battle of Bhutala 1227 AD — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
In which battle in 1227 AD did Rawal Jaitra Singh of Mewar decisively defeat the invading army of Delhi Sultan Iltutmish, though Nagda was burnt, compelling Jaitra Singh to make Chittorgarh the new capital of Mewar?
(A) Battle of Bhutala (भूताला का युद्ध, नागदा के पास - 1227 AD)
(B) Battle of Tarain (तराइन का युद्ध)
(C) Battle of Singoli (सिंगोली का युद्ध)
(D) Battle of Gagron (गागरोन का युद्ध)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Battle of Bhutala (भूताला का युद्ध, नागदा के पास - 1227 AD)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: In the **Battle of Bhutala (1227 AD)**, Rawal Jaitra Singh's brave generals **Balak and Madan** routed the forces of Sultan Iltutmish. This victory is vividly chronicled in **Jaysingh Suri's text 'Hammir Mada Mardana' (हम्मीर मद मर्दन)**. Because retreating Delhi troops burned Nagda, Jaitra Singh shifted Mewar's capital to the fortified hill-top of Chittorgarh.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Tarain? Battle of Tarain (1191/1192) was fought between Prithviraj Chauhan III and Muhammad Ghori in Karnal (Haryana).
  • Why not (C) Singoli? Battle of Singoli (1336 AD) was fought by Rana Hammir against Muhammad bin Tughlaq.
  • Why not (D) Gagron? Battle of Gagron (1519 AD) was fought by Rana Sanga against Mahmud Khilji II of Malwa.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Battle of Bhutala (1227 AD) = Jaitra Singh vs Iltutmish; Generals = Balak & Madan; Book = Hammir Mada Mardana (Jaysingh Suri); New Capital = Chittorgarh.
🎯 Mewar Dynasty — 1st Saka of Chittorgarh 1303 AD — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade
During the 1st Saka of Chittorgarh (1303 AD) by Sultan Alauddin Khilji, who were the two legendary uncle-nephew warriors who commanded the Rajput defense against the imperial army?
(A) Jaimal and Fatta (जयमल एवं फत्ता - 1568 3rd Saka)
(B) Gora and Badal (गोरा एवं बादल - 1303 1st Saka)
(C) Jaita and Kumpa (जैता एवं कूंपा - 1544 Giri Sumel)
(D) Alha and Udal (आल्हा एवं ऊदल - 1182 Mahoba)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Gora and Badal (गोरा एवं बादल - 1303 1st Saka)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: During the 1303 siege by Alauddin Khilji, Rawal Ratan Singh's commanders **Gora and Badal** fought heroically to the death (*Kesarriya*). **Rani Padmini** led 1,600 brave Rajput women into the historic fire *Jauhar* on 26 August 1303. Alauddin renamed Chittor to **'Khizrabad'** after his son Khizr Khan.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Jaimal & Fatta? Jaimal Rathore and Patta Chundawat were the heroes of the **3rd Saka of Chittor (1568 AD)** against Mughal Emperor Akbar.
  • Why not (C) Jaita & Kumpa? Jaita and Kumpa were the commanders of Rao Maldeo of Marwar in the **Battle of Giri-Sumel (1544 AD)** against Sher Shah Suri.
  • Why not (D) Alha & Udal? Alha and Udal were Chandel generals of King Paramardideva in the **Battle of Mahoba (1182 AD)** against Prithviraj Chauhan III.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: 1303 Chittor heroes = Gora & Badal; 1568 Chittor heroes = Jaimal & Fatta; 1544 Giri Sumel heroes = Jaita & Kumpa; 1182 Mahoba heroes = Alha & Udal.
🎯 Mewar Dynasty — Maharana Kumbha & Battle of Sarangpur 1437 — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & Assistant Professor
To commemorate his decisive victory over Sultan Mahmud Khilji I of Malwa in the Battle of Sarangpur (1437 AD), Maharana Kumbha constructed which 9-storeyed architectural monument in Chittorgarh Fort?
(A) Kirti Stambha (कीर्ति स्तम्भ - 7 storeys built by Jain merchant Jijak)
(B) Kumbhalgarh Katargarh
