# 📝 Chapter 3: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

Practice Q1 🎯 Tithi & Calendar Precision Standard
On which specific lunar tithi is the sacred festival of Karwa Chauth (करवा चौथ) observed by married Hindu women in Rajasthan?
(A) Kartik Shukla Chaturthi
(B) Kartik Krishna Chaturthi
(C) Bhadrapada Krishna Chaturthi
(D) Ashwin Krishna Chaturthi
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Kartik Krishna Chaturthi
Explanation: Karwa Chauth falls on Kartik Krishna Chaturthi. Married women observe a strict nirjala fast praying for the longevity and health of their husbands, breaking the fast upon sighting the moon through a sieve.
Examiner Trap: Most festivals around Diwali (Dhanteras, Roop Chaudas, Diwali) fall in the Krishna Paksha of Kartik month, NOT Shukla Paksha.
Practice Q2 🎯 Fairs & Tribal Geography
The famous Sitabari Fair (सीताबारी मेला), celebrated on Jyeshtha Amavasya and considered the "Kumbh of the Sahariya Tribe" (सहरिया जनजाति का कुंभ), is organized at which location?
(A) Kelwara (Baran)
(B) Gogunda (Udaipur)
(C) Kushalgarh (Banswara)
(D) Shahbad (Baran)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Kelwara (Baran)
Explanation: Sitabari Fair is held at Kelwara in Baran district on Jyeshtha Amavasya. It features the Sita Kund, Laxman Kund, and Valmiki Ashram. It is the premier socio-cultural congregation of the Sahariya community.
Practice Q3 🎯 Comparative Festivals Analysis
Consider the following pairs of Teej festivals and their respective celebration tithis:
1. Haryali Teej (Chhoti Teej, Jaipur) — Shravan Shukla Tritiya
2. Kajli Teej (Badi Teej / Satoodi Teej, Bundi) — Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya
3. Akshaya Tritiya (Akha Teej) — Vaishakh Shukla Tritiya
Which of the pairs given above are correctly matched?
(A) 1 and 3 only
(B) 1, 2 and 3 (All correctly matched)
(C) 2 and 3 only
(D) 1 and 2 only
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) 1, 2 and 3 (All correctly matched)
Explanation: All three are perfectly matched:
• Chhoti Teej (Jaipur procession) marks the arrival of monsoons on Shravan Shukla Tritiya.
• Kajli Teej of Bundi is famous for its grand royal procession on Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya.
• Akha Teej (Vaishakh Shukla Tritiya) is the most auspicious day for child marriages (Abujh Muhurat) and marks the foundation day of Bikaner (1488 CE).
Practice Q4 🎯 Fairs & Religious Traditions
At which sacred lake fair organized on Kartik Purnima do members of the Charan caste (चारण जाति) traditionally NOT bathe or participate due to the legendary drowning of Karni Mata's adopted son Lakhan?
(A) Pushkar Lake (Ajmer)
(B) Jaisamand Lake (Udaipur)
(C) Kolayat Lake (Bikaner)
(D) Nakki Lake (Mount Abu)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Kolayat Lake (Bikaner)
Explanation: Kolayat Lake (Bikaner) hosts the ashram of sage Kapil Muni (founder of Sankhya philosophy). On Kartik Purnima, a massive Deepdaan fair is held with 52 ghats. Charans strictly avoid this lake because Lakhan (son of Karni Mata's sister Gulab Bai) drowned here on Kolayat fair day.

# 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)

🎯 Festivals — Regional Variations of Holi in Rajasthan — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
Match the unique regional varieties of Holi Celebrations with their specific locations in Rajasthan:

Which of the following pairings is INCORRECT?
(A) Lathmar Holi (लट्ठमार होली) — Shri Mahavirji (Karauli)
(B) Kodamar Holi (कोड़ामार होली) — Bhinai (Ajmer / Kekri)
(C) Patthar-mar Holi (पत्थरमार होली) — Barmer (बाड़मेर)
(D) Angaron ki Holi (अंगारों की होली) — Jodhpur (जोधपुर)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Angaron ki Holi (अंगारों की होली) — Jodhpur (जोधपुर)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Angaron ki Holi (अंगारों की होली)** is celebrated in **Kekri (Ajmer)** and **Lalsot (Dausa)** (NOT Jodhpur), where revellers walk over glowing red-hot coals. Jodhpur is famous for *Gair Holi* and *Dhinga Gavar*.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Lathmar Holi? *Lathmar Holi* is celebrated at **Shri Mahavirji (Karauli)**.
  • Why not (B) Kodamar Holi? *Kodamar Holi* is celebrated at **Bhinai (Kekri/Ajmer)**.
  • Why not (C) Patthar-mar Holi? *Patthar-mar Holi* is celebrated in **Barmer** (along with the *Iloji procession*).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Lathmar = Mahavirji; Kodamar = Bhinai; Patthar-mar = Barmer; Angaron ki = Kekri & Lalsot; Devar-Bhabhi = Beawar; Doli-Chhint = Pushkar.
🎯 Festivals — Dhinga Gavar / Betmar Mela of Jodhpur — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & College Lecturer
On Vaishakha Krishna Tritiya, the unique carnival of Dhinga Gavar (धींगा गवर / बेंतमार मेला) is celebrated with women roaming the streets at night in various fancy disguises and playfully striking men with decorated wooden canes (*Bênt*) in which historic city of Rajasthan?
(A) Jodhpur (जोधपुर - Started under Maharana Raj Singh of Mewar & adopted by Marwar)
(B) Jaipur
(C) Bikaner
(D) Kota
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Jodhpur (जोधपुर - Started under Maharana Raj Singh of Mewar & adopted by Marwar)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Dhinga Gavar (धींगा गवर)** festival on **Vaishakh Krishna 3** is celebrated with immense fervor in **Jodhpur and Udaipur**. Started initially in Udaipur by Maharana Raj Singh I for his queen, it became a massive urban spectacle in Jodhpur where women take out midnight processions (*Teejnis*) wielding wooden canes (*Bênt*) to mock-strike men, bestowing good marriage luck.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Jaipur? Jaipur is famous for the Chhoti Teej and Gangaur royal processions.
  • Why not (C) Bikaner? Bikaner is famous for *Dolchi-mar Holi* (water buckets of Harsh & Vyas communities).
  • Why not (D) Kota? Kota is famous for its royal *Dussehra Fair*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Dhinga Gavar / Betmar Mela (Vaishakh Krishna 3) = Jodhpur & Udaipur (Only fast observed by widows too!).
🎯 Fairs — Matrikundiya Fair 'Haridwar of Rajasthan' — Deep Fact Check RPSC 2nd Grade & SI
The sacred Matrikundiya Fair (मातृकुण्डिया मेला), held annually on Vaishakha Purnima on the banks of the Banas River at Rashmi (Chittorgarh) where Lord Parashurama was absolved of the sin of matricide, is celebrated as the:
(A) Prayag of Rajasthan
(B) Haridwar of Rajasthan (राजस्थान का हरिद्वार / मेवाड़ का हरिद्वार)
(C) Kumbh of Hadoti
(D) Pushkar of Vagad
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Haridwar of Rajasthan (राजस्थान का हरिद्वार / मेवाड़ का हरिद्वार)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Matrikundiya (Rashmi, Chittorgarh)** is revered as the **'Haridwar of Rajasthan' / 'Haridwar of Mewar'**. Devotees immerse the ashes of departed ancestors into the sacred *Kund*. It is also the historic launching ground of Motilal Tejawat's **Eki Peasant Movement (1921)**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Prayag? Beneshwar Dham (Som-Mahi-Jakham) is called the *Prayag of the Tribals*.
  • Why not (C) Kumbh of Hadoti? Sitabari Fair (Baran) is the Kumbh of Sahariyas.
  • Why not (D) Pushkar of Vagad? Beneshwar is the Pushkar of Vagad.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Haridwar of Rajasthan / Mewar = Matrikundiya (Rashmi, Chittorgarh); Starting point of Eki Movement (1921) = Matrikundiya.
🎯 Festivals — Kajali Teej of Bundi — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
On which lunar tithi is the world-renowned Kajali Teej / Badi Teej / Satudi Teej (कजली तीज / सातूड़ी तीज) celebrated with a magnificent royal elephant procession in the historic city of Bundi (बूंदी)?
(A) Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya (भाद्रपद कृष्ण तृतीया - Kajali Teej of Bundi)
(B) Shravan Shukla Tritiya (श्रावण शुक्ल तृतीया - Chhoti Teej of Jaipur)
(C) Bhadrapada Shukla Tritiya (भाद्रपद शुक्ल तृतीया - Haritalika Teej)
(D) Chaitra Shukla Tritiya (चैत्र शुक्ल तृतीया - Gangaur)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya (भाद्रपद कृष्ण तृतीया - Kajali Teej of Bundi)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Kajali Teej (कजली तीज)** is celebrated on **Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya** in **Bundi**. Women observe rigorous fast, prepare traditional *Sattu* sweets, worship the Neem tree, and witness the centuries-old royal chariot procession carrying Goddess Teej atop caparisoned elephants.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Shravan Shukla 3? Shravan Shukla Tritiya is **Chhoti Teej (छोटी तीज)** celebrated in **Jaipur**, marking the onset of the monsoon and the festival calendar (*'तीज त्योहारां बावड़ी, ले डूबी गणगौर'*).
  • Why not (C) Bhadrapad Shukla 3? Bhadrapad Shukla Tritiya is **Haritalika Teej** when women fast to commemorate Goddess Parvati's penance to win Lord Shiva.
  • Why not (D) Chaitra Shukla 3? Chaitra Shukla Tritiya is the main festival of **Gangaur (गणगौर)**.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Chhoti Teej = Jaipur (Shravan Shukla 3); Kajali / Badi Teej = Bundi (Bhadrapad Krishna 3); Haritalika Teej = Bhadrapad Shukla 3; Gangaur = Chaitra Shukla 3.
🎯 Fairs — Sitabari Fair (Kumbh of the Sahariyas) — Deep Fact Check RPSC 2nd Grade & RAS Pre
The grand fair of Sitabari (सीताबड़ी का मेला, केलवाड़ा - बारां), held on Jyeshtha Amavasya and celebrated as the 'Kumbh of the Sahariyas' (सहरिया जनजाति का कुम्भ), is dedicated to which epic personalities?
(A) Lord Shiva and Parvati
(B) Mata Sita and Lakshmanji (माता सीता एवं लक्ष्मण जी - with sacred Sita Kund & Suraj Kund)
(C) Lord Krishna and Radha
(D) Sant Jambhoji and Amrita Devi
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Mata Sita and Lakshmanji (माता सीता एवं लक्ष्मण जी - with sacred Sita Kund & Suraj Kund)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Sitabari Fair (Kelwara, Baran)** is held on **Jyeshtha Amavasya** in the Hadoti region. Revered as the **'Kumbh of the Sahariya Tribe'**, it marks the sacred site where Maharishi Valmiki's ashram was located and where Mata Sita gave birth to Luv and Kush. Sahariyas perform sacred immersion of ancestor ashes in the cold natural springs (*Sita Kund, Lakshman Kund, Suraj Kund*).

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Shiva-Parvati fairs are held at Matrikundiya and Maha Shivratri.
  • Why not (C)? Krishna fairs are held at Nathdwara and Khatu Shyamji.
  • Why not (D)? Jambhoji fairs are held at Mukam (Talwa, Nokha) and Khejarli.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Kumbh of Sahariyas = Sitabari (Kelwara, Baran on Jyeshtha Amavasya); Kumbh of Vagad/Tribals = Beneshwar (Dungarpur on Magh Purnima).
🎯 Fairs — Pushkar Fair & 52 Ghats Deepdan — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & College Lecturer
The international Pushkar Fair (पुष्कर मेला, अजमेर), renowned as the largest camel/livestock fair in the world and celebrated with grand evening Deepdan (दीपदान) at Pushkar Lake, culminates on which sacred tithi?
(A) Ashvin Purnima (Sharad Purnima)
(B) Magha Purnima (माघ पूर्णिमा)
(C) Kartik Purnima (कार्तिक पूर्णिमा - Full moon of Kartik)
(D) Vaishakha Purnima (बुद्ध पूर्णिमा)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Kartik Purnima (कार्तिक पूर्णिमा - Full moon of Kartik)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The Pushkar Fair begins on **Kartik Shukla Ekadashi** and reaches its grand spiritual climax on **Kartik Purnima (कार्तिक पूर्णिमा)**. Pilgrims take holy dips across the **52 Ghats** (including *Brahma Ghat* and *Varaha Ghat*) of the crescent-shaped sacred Pushkar Lake and offer lamps (*Deepdan*). The only ancient world temple of Lord Brahma with Savitri temple on Ratnagiri hill is situated here.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Sharad Purnima? Sharad Purnima (Ashvin Purnima) is famous for the Raas Lila and Moon rays bathing *Kheer*.
  • Why not (B) Magha Purnima? Magha Purnima is the culmination tithi of the **Beneshwar Tribal Fair** in Dungarpur.
  • Why not (D) Vaishakha Purnima? Vaishakha Purnima is Buddha Purnima / Matrikundiya Fair.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Pushkar Fair & Kolayat Fair = Kartik Purnima; Beneshwar Fair = Magha Purnima; Sitabari Fair = Jyeshtha Amavasya.
🎯 Festivals — Gangaur Varieties (Jaisalmer, Nathdwara, Jodhpur) — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre
In which princely state of Rajasthan is the royal procession of Gangaur taken out exclusively WITHOUT Lord Isar (बिना ईसर की गवर) on Chaitra Shukla Chaturthi, following a historical raid by the rulers of Bikaner?
(A) Nathdwara (नाथद्वारा - Pink Gangaur on Chaitra Shukla 5)
(B) Jaipur (जयपुर - Traditional Royal Gangaur on Chaitra Shukla 3)
(C) Jodhpur (जोधपुर - Dhinga Gavar on Vaishakha Krishna 3)
(D) Jaisalmer (जैसलमेर - Gangaur without Isar on Chaitra Shukla 4)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Jaisalmer (जैसलमेर - Gangaur without Isar on Chaitra Shukla 4)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: In **Jaisalmer**, the Gangaur procession features only Goddess Gauri (Gavar) without Lord Isar (Shiva) on **Chaitra Shukla Chaturthi**. Historically, the Bikaner army snatched the idol of Isar during the festival, so only Gavar has been worshipped since.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Nathdwara? Nathdwara is famous for **Pink Gangaur (Gulabi Gangaur)** celebrated on **Chaitra Shukla Panchami**.
  • Why not (B) Jaipur? Jaipur celebrates the standard state royal Gangaur procession with both Isar and Gauri on **Chaitra Shukla Tritiya**.
  • Why not (C) Jodhpur? Jodhpur is celebrated for **Dhinga Gavar / Bentmar Gangaur** started by Maharaja Ajit Singh on **Vaishakha Krishna Tritiya**.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Gangaur without Isar = Jaisalmer (Chaitra Shukla 4); Pink Gangaur = Nathdwara (Chaitra Shukla 5); Dhinga Gavar = Jodhpur (Vaishakh Krishna 3); Royal Gangaur = Jaipur (Chaitra Shukla 3).
🎯 Regional Holis — 7 Unique Varieties in Rajasthan — Deep Fact Check RPSC EO/RO & 2nd Grade
Match the traditional regional forms of Holi celebration in Rajasthan with their authentic geographic locations:

Which of the following pairings is INCORRECT?
(A) Gobar ke Kande ki Holi (गोबर के कण्डों की होली) — Beawar (ब्यावर)
(B) Kodamar Holi (कोड़ामार होली) — Bhinai (Ajmer / Kekri)
(C) Patharmar Holi (पत्थरमार होली) — Barmer (बाड़मेर)
(D) Lathmar Holi (लट्ठमार होली) — Shri Mahavirji (Karauli)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Gobar ke Kande ki Holi (गोबर के कण्डों की होली) — Beawar (ब्यावर)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Gobar ke Kande ki Holi (गोबर के कण्डों की होली) is celebrated in **Galiakot (Dungarpur)**. Beawar is famous for **Devar-Bhabhi ki Holi (देवर-भाभी की होली)** and the *Badshah ki Sawari*.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Kodamar Holi? Kodamar Holi is played at **Bhinai (Kekri/Ajmer)** where two teams strike each other with wet cloth whips (*Koda*).
  • Why not (C) Patharmar Holi? Patharmar Holi is the stone-pelting Holi tradition of **Barmer** (where the procession of *Iloji* is also taken out).
  • Why not (D) Lathmar Holi? Lathmar Holi is played at **Shri Mahavirji (Karauli)** where women strike men with decorated wooden sticks.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Lathmar = Mahavirji; Kodamar = Bhinai; Patharmar = Barmer; Gobar Kande = Galiakot (Dungarpur); Angaron ki Holi = Kekri & Lalsot; Devar-Bhabhi = Beawar.
🎯 Fairs & Devotion — Kailadevi & Languriya — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
At the grand fair of Goddess Kailadevi (कैलादेवी, करौली) held on Chaitra Shukla Ashtami on the slopes of the Trikut Mountain, which devotional dance and folk songs are performed by Meena and Gurjar devotees?
(A) Agni Dance (अग्नि नृत्य)
(B) Languriya Dance and Songs (लांगुरिया नृत्य एवं गीत - dedicated to Hanumanji)
(C) Terah Taali Dance (तेरहताली नृत्य)
(D) Gavari Dance (गवरी नृत्य)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Languriya Dance and Songs (लांगुरिया नृत्य एवं गीत - dedicated to Hanumanji)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Devotees of Goddess Kailadevi (regarded as the incarnation of Goddess Anjana / Yogmaya) perform the lively **Languriya Dance (लांगुरिया नृत्य)** and sing humorous-devotional Languriya songs dedicated to Lord Hanuman (*Langur*). Devotees also perform the rigorous penance of *Kanak-Dandavat* along the Kalisil river.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Agni Dance? Agni dance is performed by Jasnathi Siddhs at Katariasar (Bikaner).
  • Why not (C) Terah Taali? Terah Taali is performed by Kamad women at Ramdevra.
  • Why not (D) Gavari? Gavari is a 40-day Bhil tribal dance-drama in Mewar.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Kailadevi = Chaitra Shukla 8 + Trikut Hill + Languriya songs + Bohra ki Chhatri opposite temple.
🎯 Traditional Festivals — Teej Traditions — Deep Concept Check RPSC 2nd Grade & EO/RO
The world-famous royal procession of Kajali Teej / Badi Teej / Satudi Teej (कजली तीज / बड़ी तीज) is celebrated with magnificent elephant and camel pageantry on Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya in which historical city?
(A) Jaipur (जयपुर - Chhoti Teej)
(B) Bundi (बूंदी - Kajali Teej)
(C) Jodhpur (जोधपुर - Dhinga Gavar)
(D) Nathdwara (नाथद्वारा - Pink Gangaur)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Bundi (बूंदी - Kajali Teej)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Kajali Teej (also known as Badi Teej, Satudi Teej, or Boorhi Teej) is celebrated on Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya with an iconic royal procession featuring decorated elephants, camels, and folk performers exclusively in **Bundi**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Jaipur? Jaipur is world-renowned for the royal procession of **Chhoti Teej / Hariyali Teej** on Shravana Shukla Tritiya (where Ghewar is the signature sweet).
  • Why not (C) Jodhpur? Jodhpur is celebrated for **Dhinga Gavar / Bentmar Gangaur** held on Vaishakha Krishna Tritiya.
  • Why not (D) Nathdwara? Nathdwara in Rajsamand is famous for celebrating **Pink Gangaur (Gulabi Gangaur)** on Chaitra Shukla Panchami.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Chhoti Teej = Shravan Shukla 3 (Jaipur); Badi/Kajali Teej = Bhadrapad Krishna 3 (Bundi); Gangaur = Chaitra Shukla 3 (Jaipur/Udaipur).
🎯 Fairs & Tribal Culture — Beneshwar Dham — Deep Concept Check RPSC RAS Pre & Assistant Professor
The Beneshwar Fair (बेणेश्वर मेला), hailed as the 'Kumbh of the Tribals' (आदिवासियों का महाकुम्भ) and 'Pushkar of Vagad', is held on Magha Purnima (माघ पूर्णिमा) at the confluence of which three rivers?
(A) Chambal, Banas, and Seep (चम्बल, बनास एवं सीप - Rameshwaram)
(B) Som, Mahi, and Jakham (सोम, माही एवं जाखम - Dungarpur)
(C) Banas, Berach, and Menal (बनास, बेड़च एवं मेनाल - Bigod, Bhilwara)
(D) Ahu, Kali Sindh, and Parwan (आहू, कालीसिंध एवं परवन)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Som, Mahi, and Jakham (सोम, माही एवं जाखम - Dungarpur)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Beneshwar Dham at Nawata-para (Sabla, Dungarpur) is situated at the sacred **Triveni Sangam of Som, Mahi, and Jakham rivers**. Established by Sant Mavji, it is the only place in the world where a **broken/fragmented Shivling (खण्डित शिवलिंग)** is worshipped during the Magha Purnima fair.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Chambal, Banas, Seep? Chambal, Banas, and Seep form the sacred confluence at **Rameshwaram Ghat (Sawai Madhopur)**, revered as the pilgrimage center for Meena devotees.
  • Why not (C) Banas, Berach, Menal? These three rivers meet at **Bigod (Bhilwara)** forming the Bigod Triveni Sangam.
  • Why not (D) Ahu & Kali Sindh? Ahu and Kali Sindh meet at Gagron Fort in Jhalawar (Jal Durg).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Beneshwar Triveni = Som + Mahi + Jakham (Dungarpur); Rameshwaram Triveni = Chambal + Banas + Seep; Bigod Triveni = Banas + Berach + Menal.

# 🎯 Chapter 3: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

Q1 RPSC RAS Pre 2016 / 2nd Grade 2022 / EO/RO 2022
'Kajli Teej' (कजली तीज / सातुड़ी तीज / बड़ी तीज) is celebrated with a world-famous grand royal procession in which city of Rajasthan?
(A) Udaipur
(B) Bundi
(C) Jaipur
(D) Jodhpur
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Bundi
Explanation: While Haryali/Chhoti Teej (Shravan Shukla Tritiya) is celebrated in Jaipur, Kajli/Badi Teej is celebrated on Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya with immense royal splendor in Bundi.
Q2 RPSC SI 2021 / 1st Grade 2020
On which Tithi does the famous 18-day 'Gangaur' (गणगौर) festival culminate?
(A) Shravan Krishna Tritiya
(B) Vaishakh Shukla Tritiya
(C) Bhadrapada Shukla Tritiya
(D) Chaitra Shukla Tritiya
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Chaitra Shukla Tritiya
Explanation: Gangaur worship starts the day after Holika Dahan (Chaitra Krishna Pratipada) and continues for 18 days, culminating on Chaitra Shukla Tritiya with elaborate processions of Gauri (Isar-Gauri). (Note: Dhinga Gangaur is celebrated in Udaipur/Jodhpur on Vaishakh Krishna Tritiya!).
Q3 RPSC RAS Pre 2021
'Sheetlashtami' (शीतलाष्टमी / बासोड़ा) is celebrated on which date of the Hindu calendar?
(A) Vaishakh Krishna Ashtami
(B) Chaitra Krishna Ashtami
(C) Ashwin Shukla Ashtami
(D) Chaitra Shukla Ashtami
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Chaitra Krishna Ashtami
Explanation: Celebrated on Chaitra Krishna Ashtami. Stale food (*Basoda*) prepared on Saptami night is offered to Sheetla Mata (Seel ki Dungri, Chaksu, Jaipur) to ward off pox and fevers. No fresh fires are lit in households on this day.
Q4 RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / EO/RO 2022
The famous 'Beneshwar Fair' (बेणेश्वर मेला), known as the "Kumbh of Tribals", is organized at the confluence of which rivers on Magh Purnima?
(A) Luni, Bandi and Sukri
(B) Kali Sindh, Ahu and Parwan
(C) Chambal, Banas and Morel
(D) Som, Mahi and Jakham
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Som, Mahi and Jakham
Explanation: Located at Sabla/Nawapara in Dungarpur at the Triveni Sangam of Som, Mahi, and Jakham. Devotees venerate the fractured Shiva Lingam (*Khandit Shivling*) and immerse mortal remains (*Asthi Visarjan*).
Q5 RPSC SI 2021 / Patwari 2021
The internationally renowned Pushkar Fair in Ajmer is held during which lunar period?
(A) Kartik Shukla Ekadashi to Kartik Purnima
(B) Magh Shukla Ekadashi to Purnima
(C) Ashwin Shukla Ekadashi to Purnima
(D) Chaitra Shukla Pratipada to Navami
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Kartik Shukla Ekadashi to Kartik Purnima
Explanation: The fair spans Kartik Shukla Ekadashi to Kartik Purnima (October/November), featuring sacred lake bathing, Maha Aarti at Brahma temple, deepdan, and the world's largest camel and horse trading congregation.
Q6 RPSC 2nd Grade 2022 / RAS Pre 2013
In the fair of Kailadevi (कैलादेवी - Karauli), which devotional folk songs and dances are performed by the devotees?
(A) Ghoomar
(B) Chari
(C) Terah Taali
(D) Languriya (लांगुरिया)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Languriya (लांगुरिया)
Explanation: Kailadevi is worshipped as the incarnation of Anjana Mata (mother of Lord Hanuman / Languriya). Her Lakhi Mela is celebrated on Chaitra Shukla Ashtami on the banks of the Kalisil river in Karauli.
Q7 RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / SI 2021
Which fair in Rajasthan is revered as the "Kumbh of the Sahariya Tribe" (सहरिया जनजाति का कुम्भ)?
(A) Gautameshwar Fair (Pratapgarh)
(B) Beneshwar Fair (Dungarpur)
(C) Sitabari Fair (Kelwara, Baran)
(D) Ghotia Amba Fair (Banswara)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Sitabari Fair (Kelwara, Baran)
Explanation: Held at Kelwara (Shahbad, Baran) on Jyeshtha Amavasya. Sahariya tribal members gather in huge numbers at the sacred ponds (Lakshman Kund, Sita Kund, Suraj Kund) for rituals and community settlement.
Q8 RPSC REET 2022 / Constable 2020
On which Tithi does the famous Khatu Shyamji Fair in Sikar take place?
(A) Bhadrapada Dashami
(B) Kartik Purnima
(C) Chaitra Shukla Navami
(D) Phalgun Shukla Ekadashi & Dwadashi
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Phalgun Shukla Ekadashi & Dwadashi
Explanation: Barbarik (grandson of Bhima) is worshipped as Shyam Baba ("Haare Ka Sahara"). Devotees carry colorful Nishan (flags) from Ringas to Khatu during Phalgun Shukla Ekadashi-Dwadashi.

CHAPTER 4
Chapter 4: Costumes and Ornaments of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के वस्त्र एवं आभूषण)

🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Language of Turbans & Jewels (पगड़ी और आभूषणों की भाषा)
"In ancient Rajasthan, you didn’t need to ask a man his caste, region, or marital status—you only had to look at his head! The way a turban (Pagri / Safa - पगड़ी / साफा) was tied revealed whether he came from the hills of Mewar (compact flat pagri) or the deserts of Marwar (tall flowing safa). A saffron turban meant he was marching to war (केसरिया बाणा); a mothara turban meant he was off to a wedding; and a lahariya turban meant the monsoon rains had arrived. Similarly, traditional Rajasthani jewelry was an intricate science of acupressure and social status."

# 1. Traditional Male & Female Costumes (पारंपरिक वेशभूषा)

# A. Male Costumes (पुरुष वेशभूषा)

  • Turbans (Pagri / Safa / Paag - पगड़ी / साफा / पाग / फेंटा):
    • Mewari Pagri (मेवाड़ी पगड़ी): Flat, compact, tied low on the head (e.g., Chundawatshahi / चूण्डावतशाही, Amarshahi / अमरशाही, Shambhushahi / शंभूशाही).
    • Marwari Safa (मारवाड़ी साफा): High, broad, long tail (Chhogadar / छोगों वाला).
    • Jaipuri Pagri (जयपुरी पगड़ी): Khirkiya Pagri (खिड़कीदार पगड़ी), Man-shahi (मानशाही).
  • Occasion-Specific Turbans (RPSC Match Table):
    • Lahariya Pagri (लहरिया पगड़ी): Shravana / Monsoon season (सावन माह).
    • Mothara Pagri (मोठड़ा पगड़ी): Wedding ceremonies (विवाह अवसर - criss-cross check dyes).
    • Madil Pagri (मदील पगड़ी): Dussehra / विजयादशमी (interwoven gold and silver Zari threads).
    • Phaganiya Pagri (फागणिया पगड़ी): Holi / Spring festival (होली व फाल्गुन).
    • Kasumal (कसूम्बल - Bright Red): Auspicious festive gatherings.
    • Khakha / White (खाखा / सफेद): Mourning attire (शोक के समय).
  • Upper & Lower Body: Angarakhi (अंगरखी) (inner coat, also called Bugatari / बुगतरी, Mirzai / मिरजई, Bandi / बांडी), Choga (चोगा) (embroidered royal robe over Angarakhi), Jama (जामा) (flared gown), Dhoti / Pajama (धोती / पाजामा).

# B. Female Costumes (महिला वेशभूषा)

  • Traditional Ensemble: Ghaghra (घाघरा) (flared skirt with 80 kalis/panels), Kanchli (कांचली) (back-tied fitted inner bodice), Kurti (कुर्ती) (sleeveless tunic over Kanchli), and Odhani (ओढ़नी) (drape / veil).
  • Types of Odhani / Veils (ओढ़नी के प्रकार):
    • Pila Pomcha (पीला पोमचा - Yellow): Lotus motif drape worn by a mother on the birth of a baby boy (पुत्र जन्म).
    • Gulabi Pomcha (गुलाबी पोमचा - Pink): Worn on the birth of a baby girl (पुत्री जन्म).
    • Tara-Bhaat ki Odhani (तारा-भांत की ओढ़नी) / Kareel ki Odhani / Jawar-Bhaat (ज्वार-भांत): Celebrated traditional drapes worn by tribal (Adivasi / आदिवासी) women.

# 2. Comprehensive Head-to-Toe Ornaments Decoder (सिर से पैर तक आभूषण) 🔥 Direct Exam Match Questions

📖 Complete Head-to-Toe Identification Guide (आभूषण शब्दावली)
Body Part (अंग) Traditional Ornaments (पारंपरिक आभूषण) Key Identifying Features (विशेष पहचान)
Head & Forehead (सिर व मस्तक) Rakhdi (रखड़ी), Borla (बोरला), Sheeshphool (शीशफूल), Tikka (टीका), Memand (मेमंद), Damini (दामिनी), Tiddi-Balko (टीडी-भलको), Sankali (सांकली) Borla (spherical bell on crown), Memand (forehead crest), Tiddi-Balko (hanging along hairline).
Nose (नाक) Nath (नथ), Long (लौंग), Besar (बेसर), Chop (चोप), Chuni (चुन्नी), Fini (फीणी), Bhanwariya (भंवरिया) Besar (featured in Bani-Thani painting), Chop (golden clove in nose wing).
Teeth (दांत) Chhup / Chumpa (चूंपा), Rakhankha (रखन) Gold/silver pins or plates embedded inside front incisors.
Ears (कान) Jhumka (झुमका), Kundal (कुण्डल), Bali (बाली), Jhela (झेला), Oganiya (ओगण्या), Toti (टोटी), Karnaphool (कर्णफूल), Peepal-Patra (पीपल-पत्रा), Surliya (सुरलिया) Oganiya & Peepal-Patra (leaf-shaped clips on top ear cartilage), Toti (round plug).
Neck & Chest (गला व छाती) Timaniya (तिमणिया), Aad (आड़), Hansli (हंसली), Thussi (ठुस्सी), Tulsi (तुलसी), Kanthi (कंठी), Champakali (चम्पाकली), Mandliya (मांदलिया), Galpatta (गलपट्टा), Har (हार) Aad (heavy rectangular royal Rajput necklace), Timaniya (triangular plate), Champakali (jasmine buds).
Arms & Wrists (बाजू व कलाई) Bhujbandh (भुजबन्ध), Kada (कड़ा), Bangadi (बंगड़ी), Hathphool (हथफूल), Nogri (नौगरी), Gokhru (गोखरू), Poonchi (पूंछी), Chudi (चूड़ी), Anant (अनंत) Bangadi (gold bangles with round beads), Gokhru (spiked bangle), Nogri (pearl band).
Fingers & Thumbs (उंगलियां व अंगूठा) Damna (दामणा), Arsi (आरसी), Bitholi (बींठी), Mundri (मुंदरी) Damna (ring joining two fingers), Arsi (large thumb ring fitted with a mirror).
Waist (कमर) Kandora (कंदोरा), Kardhani (करधनी), Tagdi (तगड़ी), Kanakti (कणकती), Satka (सटका), Zanjeer (जंजीर) Tagdi / Kandora (jingling silver/gold chain belts).
Ankles & Feet (पैर व उंगलियां) Payal (पायल), Payjeb (पायजेब), Kada (कड़ा), Nupur (नूपुर), Jhanjhar (झांझर), Ramjhol (रमझोल), Todar (तोड़ा), Nevri (नेवरी), Bichhiya (बिछिया), Angoota (अंगूठा) Nevri (worn with pajeb), Ramjhol (heavy bells on cloth), Bichhiya (married toe ring).
⚠️ Examiner Trap: The #1 Most Confused Question in RPSC (चोप बनाम चूंपा)
Chop (चोप) is worn in the NOSE (नाक)!
Chhup / Chumpa (चूंपा) is worn in the TEETH (दांत)!
(Examiners intentionally put both in options to trap hurried candidates!)

# ⚡ Chapter 4: 1-Minute Quick Cramming Table

Ornament / Dress Location / Body Part Critical Exam Key
Borla / Memand (बोरला / मेमंद) Head / Forehead (सिर व मस्तक) Round spherical ornament / Forehead line
Chop vs Chhup (चोप बनाम चूंपा) Chop = Nose | Chhup = Teeth High-frequency elimination trap
Oganiya / Peepal-Patra (ओगण्या / पीपल-पत्रा) Ears (कान) Leaf-shaped top helix ornaments
Aad / Timaniya (आड़ / तिमणिया) Neck (गला) Royal rectangular necklace / Triangular piece
Gokhru / Bangadi (गोखरू / बंगड़ी) Wrist / Forearm (कलाई) Spiked gold bangle & beaded bracelet
Damna (दामणा) Fingers (उंगलियां) Single ornament bridging two fingers
Arsi (आरसी) Thumb (अंगूठा) Thumb ring with small mirror for self-viewing
Tagdi / Kandora (तगड़ी / कंदोरा) Waist (कमर) Jingling waist chain
Ramjhol / Nevri (रमझोल / नेवरी) Ankle / Feet (पैर) Jingling ankle band

# 📝 Chapter 4: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

Practice Q1 🎯 Anatomical Identification / Ornaments
Which of the following groups consists EXCLUSIVELY of traditional Rajasthani ornaments worn around the Neck / Throat (गले के आभूषण)?
(A) Borla, Shishphool, Tikda, Tiddi Bhalco, Memand
(B) Oganiya, Toti, Surliya, Peepalpatra, Jhumka
(C) Timaniya, Champakali, Hansli, Tussi, Mandaliya
(D) Kandhora, Kardhani, Tagdi, Kanakti, Satka
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Timaniya, Champakali, Hansli, Tussi, Mandaliya
Explanation:
• Option (A) = All Neck/Throat ornaments.
• Option (B) = Head and Forehead ornaments.
• Option (C) = Ear ornaments.
• Option (D) = Waist ornaments.
Practice Q2 🎯 Textiles & Traditional Attire
The traditional textile "Pabla / Pompcha" (पोमचा) with a yellow background (Peela) is presented to a woman by her maternal family (Pihar) on which occasion?
(A) Upon the birth of a male child (Son)
(B) During Teej festival celebrations
(C) Upon the birth of a female child (Daughter)
(D) Upon marriage engagement (Sagai)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Upon the birth of a male child (Son)
Explanation: Peela Pompcha (yellow) is gifted on the birth of a son during the Jalkwa / Kua Pujan ceremony. Gulabi Pompcha (pink) is gifted on the birth of a daughter.
Practice Q3 🎯 Teeth & Ear Ornaments Special
What is the key difference between the traditional teeth ornaments "Chump" (चूँप) and "Rakhn" (रखन) in Rajasthan?
(A) Chump is worn in the ear, while Rakhn is worn in the nose.
(B) Chump is worn by men only, while Rakhn is worn by unmarried girls.
(C) Chump is made of ivory, while Rakhn is made of copper.
(D) Chump is a gold pin studded into a tooth, while Rakhn is a gold/silver cap/plate covering the tooth.
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Chump is a gold pin studded into a tooth, while Rakhn is a gold/silver cap/plate covering the tooth.
Explanation:
• चूँप (Chump): A tiny gold pin/nail embedded by drilling a small pit in the front upper teeth.
• रखन (Rakhn): A gold or silver plate or cap fitted over the teeth.
Examiner Trap: Do not confuse चूँप (Chump - Tooth) with चोंप (Chomp - Nose)!
Practice Q4 🎯 Tribal Odhanis & Motif Design
The famous "Tara Bhaat ki Odhani" (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी) with star motifs on a reddish-brown ground is the quintessential traditional veil worn by women of which community?
(A) Bishnoi women
(B) Maheshwari women
(C) Adivasi / Tribal women (आदिवासी महिलाएं)
(D) Rajput royal women
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Adivasi / Tribal women (आदिवासी महिलाएं)
Explanation: Traditional tribal women odhanis include Tara Bhaat (star print), Jwar Bhaat (jowar grain print), Ker Bhaat, and Lehar Bhaat. Kachhaboo (घाघरा) is the traditional skirt worn by Bhil women.

# 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)

🎯 Costumes — Male Body Garments (Angarakhi Varieties) — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & College Lecturer
What are 'Bugatari', 'Mirzai', 'Tanzeeb', 'Gadar', and 'Dootai' in traditional Rajasthani attire?
(A) Varieties of Rajputi daggers and shields
(B) Types of silk turbans worn in Marwar
(C) Regional synonyms and seasonal tailored varieties of the traditional male upper-body vest / shirt called 'Angarakhi' (अंगरखी के विभिन्न प्रकार)
(D) Traditional designs of silver chokers
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Regional synonyms and seasonal tailored varieties of the traditional male upper-body vest / shirt called 'Angarakhi' (अंगरखी के विभिन्न प्रकार)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Angarakhi (अंगरखी)** (a close-fitting collarless shirt tied with cords across the chest) is known in various regions as **Bugatari (बुगतरी), Mirzai (मिर्जई), Tanzeeb (तनजीब), Gadar, Dootai, and Kasan**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Daggers include *Katar*, *Chhura*, and *Khanjar*.
  • Why not (B)? Turbans include *Amarshahi, Jaswantshahi, Vijayashahi*.
  • Why not (D)? Chokers include *Thussi, Timaniya, Hansli*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Bugatari, Mirzai, Tanzeeb, Gadar, Dootai = Types of Angarakhi (अंगरखी); Choga = Overcoat worn over Angarakhi.
🎯 Ornaments — Head & Forehead (Tidi-Bhalako & Memand) — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade
Which of the following specific jewelry items is an authentic Forehead / Head ornament (माथा / सिर का आभूषण) worn by married women in Rajasthan?
(A) Oganiya and Pippalpatra (Ears)
(B) Tagdi and Kardhani (Waist)
(C) Gokhru and Pachheli (Wrists)
(D) Tidi-Bhalako (टीडी-भलको) and Memand (मेमंद)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Tidi-Bhalako (टीडी-भलको) and Memand (मेमंद)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Tidi-Bhalako (टीडी-भलको) (a delicate pendant hanging right beneath the hairline in the center of the forehead) and Memand (मेमंद) (a central hair-parting jewel) are prominent **Head & Forehead ornaments**, alongside *Rakhdi, Borla, Shishphool, Bindi, Tikka, and Sankli*.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? *Oganiya* and *Pippalpatra* are worn in the upper **Ears (कान)**.
  • Why not (B)? *Tagdi* and *Kardhani* are worn around the **Waist (कमर)**.
  • Why not (C)? *Gokhru* and *Pachheli* are worn on the **Wrists / Hands (कलाई/हाथ)**.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Tidi-Bhalako, Memand, Rakhdi, Shishphool = Head/Forehead; Oganiya, Surliya, Pippalpatra = Ears; Gokhru, Bangdi = Wrist; Tagdi = Waist.
🎯 Traditional Costumes — Rajputi Turbans (Amarshahi, Vijayashahi) — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
What are 'Amarshahi', 'Vijayashahi', 'Jaswantshahi', 'Mansinghshahi', and 'Bhimshahi' in Rajasthani cultural heritage?
(A) Traditional varieties of sword tempering techniques
(B) Traditional folk theatre scripts of Mewar
(C) Forms of silver cattle ornaments
(D) Distinct royal stylistic modes of tying Turbans / Pagris (पगड़ी / साफ़ा बांधने की विभिन्न राजपूती शैलियां)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Distinct royal stylistic modes of tying Turbans / Pagris (पगड़ी / साफ़ा बांधने की विभिन्न राजपूती शैलियां)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: These are famous regional styles of **Pagris / Turbans (पगड़ी / साफे की शैलियां)** named after respective rulers: **Amarshahi & Chundawatshahi** (Mewar), **Vijayashahi, Jaswantshahi & Mansinghshahi** (Jodhpur/Marwar), **Bhimshahi & Madhoshahi** (Jaipur/Kota).

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Famous sword varieties include *Sirohi Talwar* and *Tegh*.
  • Why not (B)? Folk theatre forms are *Khyal*, *Tamasha*, and *Rammat*.
  • Why not (C)? Cattle ornaments include *Gorband* (camel neck) and *Ghungroo*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Amarshahi, Vijayashahi, Jaswantshahi, Mansinghshahi = Turban styles (पगड़ी शैलियां); Pachranga Safa = Jodhpur; Khunchewali Pagri = Mewar.
🎯 Traditional Costumes — Female Textiles (Mothda, Pomcha, Lahariya) — Deep Fact Check RPSC EO/RO & 2nd Grade
In traditional Rajasthani textiles, what is the tie-dye pattern called when the diagonal striped lines of Lahariya (लहरिया) intersect and cross each other in a checkered / grid fashion?
(A) Mothda (मोठड़ा - Cross-striped checkered tie-dye)
(B) Pomcha (पोमचा - Lotus print veil)
(C) Dhabla (धाबला - Woolen rural skirt)
(D) Rejha (रेझा - Coarse tribal cloth)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Mothda (मोठड़ा - Cross-striped checkered tie-dye)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: When the diagonal wavy lines of *Lahariya* intersect each other at right angles to form diamond/checkered grids, the textile is called **Mothda (मोठड़ा)** (celebrated in Jodhpur turbans and odhnis). **Pomcha** is a traditional lotus-roundel veil (yellow Pomcha on birth of a baby boy; pink Pomcha on birth of a baby girl).

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Pomcha? *Pomcha* is a yellow/pink central-circle veil gifted by maternal grandparents (*Peehar*) upon childbirth.
  • Why not (C) Dhabla? *Dhabla* is a thick handspun woolen skirt worn by rural women in Marwar/Bikaner.
  • Why not (D) Rejha? *Rejha* is the thick white cotton costume worn by Sahariya women.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Diagonal parallel waves = Lahariya; Diagonal crossing lines (checkered) = Mothda; Son's birth veil = Yellow Pomcha; Daughter's birth veil = Pink Pomcha.
🎯 Ornaments — Feet & Ankle Jewels (Anwala & Nevari) — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & 2nd Grade
Which pair of heavy solid silver ornaments is worn around the Ankles and Feet (पैर / टखने) of Rajasthani women, where one ring rests immediately on top of the other?
(A) Timaniya and Hansli
(B) Anwala (आंवला) and Nevari (नेवरी)
(C) Gokhru and Bangdi
(D) Oganiya and Pippalpatra
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Anwala (आंवला) and Nevari (नेवरी)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Anwala (आंवला) is a thick ribbed silver anklet. Nevari (नेवरी) is an ornate silver ring worn directly over the Anwala around the ankles, along with *Ramjhol* (musical tinkling bell-pad) and *Hirnamain*.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Timaniya & Hansli? Both are **Neck (गला)** ornaments.
  • Why not (C) Gokhru & Bangdi? Both are **Wrist / Hand (कलाई/हाथ)** ornaments.
  • Why not (D) Oganiya & Pippalpatra? Both are **Ear (कान)** cartilage ornaments.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Anwala & Nevari = Feet/Ankles; Timaniya & Hansli = Neck; Gokhru & Bangdi = Wrist; Oganiya & Surliya = Ear; Chump = Teeth; Chop = Nose.
🎯 Ornaments — Head to Toe (Teeth Ornaments: Chump vs Chop) — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
In Rajasthani culture, what is the critical anatomical difference between the ornaments 'Chump' (चूंम्प) and 'Chop' (चोप)?
(A) Chump is worn on the neck; Chop is worn on the ankle
(B) Chump is worn on the forehead; Chop is worn on the wrist
(C) Chump (चूंम्प) is a gold pin studded inside the teeth; Chop (चोप) is an ornament worn on the nose
(D) Both are identical types of Rajasthani silver earrings
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Chump (चूंम्प) is a gold pin studded inside the teeth; Chop (चोप) is an ornament worn on the nose
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Chump (चूंम्प) and Rakkan (रखन) are ornaments of the **Teeth (दांत)** (Chump is a gold stud embedded in the tooth; Rakkan is a gold/silver cap). In contrast, Chop (चोप), **Long**, **Bari**, and **Besari** are ornaments worn on the **Nose (नाक)**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Neck ornaments include *Timaniya*, *Hansli*, and *Madaliya*; ankle ornaments include *Nevari* and *Anwala*.
  • Why not (B)? Forehead ornaments include *Memand* and *Borla*; wrist ornaments include *Gokhru* and *Bangdi*.
  • Why not (D)? Ear ornaments include *Oganiya*, *Pippalpatra*, and *Surliya*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Chump (चूंम्प) & Rakkan = Teeth (दांत); Chop (चोप), Besari & Bari = Nose (नाक); Memand = Forehead (माथा); Oganiya = Ear (कान).
🎯 Ornaments — Upper Ear & Finger Jewels (Oganiya & Damna) — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & SI
Which of the following pairs of traditional Rajasthani ornaments is correctly matched with the respective body parts on which they are worn?
(A) Bari — Waist & Kandhora — Ear
(B) Memand — Ankle & Timaniya — Nose
(C) Gokhru — Forehead & Nevari — Neck
(D) Oganiya (ओगण्या) — Ear cartilage (कान के ऊपरी हिस्से में) & Damna (दामणा) — Fingers of the hand (हाथ की अंगुलियों में)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Oganiya (ओगण्या) — Ear cartilage (कान के ऊपरी हिस्से में) & Damna (दामणा) — Fingers of the hand (हाथ की अंगुलियों में)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Oganiya (ओगण्या) (along with *Pippalpatra* and *Surliya*) is worn on the upper ear rim/cartilage. Damna (दामणा) is a double-ring ornament linking two fingers of the hand together simultaneously.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? *Bari* is worn on the nose; *Kandhora* is worn around the waist.
  • Why not (B)? *Memand* is worn on the forehead hair; *Timaniya* is a triangular necklace on the neck.
  • Why not (C)? *Gokhru* is worn on the wrist; *Nevari* is worn around the ankles.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Oganiya & Pippalpatra = Ear; Damna = Two fingers; Gokhru = Wrist; Nevari & Anwala = Feet/Ankle; Kandhora & Tagdi = Waist.
🎯 Traditional Headgear — Turbans of Rajasthan — Deep Concept Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
Match the princely royal turban (पगड़ी / साफा) styles of Rajasthan with their respective royal court kingdoms:

Which of the following pairs is INCORRECTLY matched?
(A) Amarshahi Turban (अमरशाही पगड़ी) — Mewar (मेवाड़)
(B) Vijayashahi & Mansinghshahi (विजयशाही व मानसिंहशाही साफा) — Jodhpur / Marwar (जोधपुर)
(C) Bhimshahi Turban (भीमशाही पगड़ी) — Mewar (मेवाड़)
(D) Chundawatshahi Turban (चूंडावतशाही पगड़ी) — Bikaner (बीकानेर)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Chundawatshahi Turban (चूंडावतशाही पगड़ी) — Bikaner (बीकानेर)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The Chundawatshahi Turban (चूंडावतशाही पगड़ी) belongs to the Salumbar (सलूम्बर) thikana of **Mewar**, NOT Bikaner. Bikaner was famous for its distinctive high, light-weight Bikaneri Safa and Khunchewali Pagri.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Amarshahi? Amarshahi Pagri was introduced by Maharana Amar Singh II of Mewar and is one of the classic royal turbans of Udaipur.
  • Why not (B) Vijayashahi & Mansinghshahi? Vijayashahi (introduced by Maharaja Vijay Singh) and Mansinghshahi (by Maharaja Man Singh) are famous Marwar/Jodhpur royal safas.
  • Why not (C) Bhimshahi? Bhimshahi Pagri was introduced by Maharana Bhim Singh of Mewar (featuring a broader base and prominent crown).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Amarshahi, Bhimshahi, Udayashahi, Chundawatshahi, Nathdwara Pagri = Mewar; Vijayashahi, Mansinghshahi, Jaswantshahi = Jodhpur.
🎯 Traditional Attire — Odhnis & Motifs — Deep Concept Check RPSC Senior Teacher & SI
Which of the following traditional Rajasthani printed Odhnis is predominantly worn by tribal and rural women featuring angular cross-check wave patterns (छपाई)?
(A) Lahariya (लहरिया - Diagonal stripes worn during Shravan/Teej)
(B) Tara Bhat ki Odhni (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी - Tribal women)
(C) Mothda (मोठड़ा - Two-way criss-cross grid pattern)
(D) Pila Pomcha (पीला पोमचा - Gifted on birth of male child)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Tara Bhat ki Odhni (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी - Tribal women)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Tara Bhat ki Odhni (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी) features star-shaped motifs with red-brown borders on a dark background, worn with great pride by tribal and rural women in Rajasthan (along with Jwar Bhat and Keri Bhat).

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Lahariya? Lahariya features one-directional diagonal wave-like colored stripes, traditionally worn during the monsoon month of Shravana on Teej festival.
  • Why not (C) Mothda? Mothda is created when diagonal stripes cross each other in two opposite directions forming small checked squares (Mothda turbans are worn during wedding ceremonies).
  • Why not (D) Pila Pomcha? Pomcha is an auspicious lotus-print veil with a yellow center (Pila) sent by parents upon the birth of a baby boy, while a pink Pomcha is sent for a girl.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Tara Bhat, Keri Bhat, Jwar Bhat = Tribal Odhnis; Single diagonal wave = Lahariya; Cross diagonal grid = Mothda; Birth of boy = Pila Pomcha.

# 🎯 Chapter 4: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

Q1 RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / SI 2021
'Timaniya' (तिमणिया) is a traditional ornament worn on which part of the body by Rajasthani women?
(A) Neck / Throat (गला)
(B) Hand / Wrist (हाथ)
(C) Head / Forehead (सिर)
(D) Waist (कमर)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Neck / Throat (गला)
Explanation: Timaniya is a gold necklace studded with gems and small golden beads. Other neck ornaments include Aad, Hansli, Champakali, Mandliya, Mangtika, Galpatta, and Tulsi.
Q2 RPSC RAS Pre 2021 / College Lecturer 2021
'Chump' (चूंप) is a traditional ornament worn in the:
(A) Nose (नाक)
(B) Tooth (दांत)
(C) Ear (कान)
(D) Finger (अंगुली)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Tooth (दांत)
Explanation: Chump (चूंप) and Rakkhan (रखन) are gold/silver inserts in teeth.
Examiner Trap: Chop (चॉप) is worn in the nose, whereas Chump (चूंप) is in the tooth!
Q3 RPSC 1st Grade 2020 / EO/RO 2022
'Memand' (मेमंद) and 'Rakhdi' (रखड़ी) are traditional ornaments of:
(A) Ankles and Feet
(B) Waist and Hips
(C) Nose and Ears
(D) Head and Forehead (सिर व मस्तक)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Head and Forehead (सिर व मस्तक)
Explanation: Memand, Rakhdi, Borla, Shishphool, Teeka, and Tikda are all worn across the head, hairline (*Maang*), and forehead.
Q4 RPSC 2nd Grade 2022
'Kandhora' (कंदोरा) and 'Kankati' (कणकती) are worn around which body part?
(A) Neck (गला)
(B) Waist (कमर)
(C) Arms (बाजू)
(D) Feet (पैर)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Waist (कमर)
Explanation: Kandhora, Kankati, Tagdi, Kardhani, and Zanjir are metallic belt ornaments clasped tightly around the waist.
Q5 RPSC RAS Pre 2013, 2018 / Patwari 2021
'Tara Bhat ri Odhni' (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी) is a signature traditional attire worn by:
(A) Rajput Women
(B) Brahmin Women
(C) Tribal / Adivasi Women
(D) Jat Women
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Tribal / Adivasi Women
Explanation: Tribal women in Rajasthan wear tie-dye patterned Odhanis: *Tara Bhat* (Star motif on red-brown ground), *Kairi Bhat* (Mango/Paisley motif), and *Jawar Bhat* (Millet seed motif).
Q6 RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / SI 2021
'Amarshahi', 'Vijayashahi', 'Mansinghshahi', and 'Bhimshahi' are regional styles of:
(A) Gold coins
(B) Sword fighting styles
(C) Royal palanquins
(D) Traditional Turbans / Pagris (पगड़ियां)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Traditional Turbans / Pagris (पगड़ियां)
Explanation: Turbans represent regional honor and royal lineage: Amarshahi (Mewar), Vijayashahi & Mansinghshahi (Marwar/Jodhpur), Jaswantshahi, Shahjahanshahi, and Khangshedi.
Q7 RPSC 2nd Grade 2019 / REET
'Peela / Pomcha' (पीला / पोमचा) is a traditional ceremonial veil presented to a mother:
(A) During wedding engagement only
(B) During Teej dance only
(C) During mourning ceremony
(D) On the birth of a newborn child (Matritva ritual)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) On the birth of a newborn child (Matritva ritual)
Explanation: Yellow Pomcha (*Peela*) is gifted by maternal grandparents on the birth of a baby boy, while Pink Pomcha (*Gulabi Pomcha*) is given upon the birth of a baby girl during the *Kuan Pujan / Jalwa Pujan* ceremony.
Q8 RPSC Patwari 2021 / Constable 2020
'Bari' (बारी), 'Bhogli' (भोगली), and 'Chop' (चॉप) are worn in:
(A) Nose (नाक)
(B) Fingers (अंगुलियां)
(C) Ears (कान)
(D) Ankles (पैर)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Nose (नाक)
Explanation: Bari, Bhogli, Nath, Kaanta, Long, Fini, and Chop are all traditional nose ornaments worn by Rajasthani women.

CHAPTER 5
Chapter 5: Painting Styles and Folk Arts of Rajasthan (राजस्थान की चित्रकला शैलियां एवं लोक कलाएं)

🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: How Did Rajasthani Painting Evolve? (चित्रकला का उद्भव)
"Before the 16th century, Indian painting was dominated by the rigid, flat lines of the Western Indian (Apabhramsa / अपभ्रंश / Jain) style. But when Mughal imperial stability united the country, Rajput rulers visited Agra and Lahore, bringing Persian miniaturists back to their desert courts. The fusion that resulted was magical: the precision, fine brushwork, and realism of Mughal court art merged with the raw devotion, bright indigenous colors, and natural landscapes of Rajasthan, giving birth to the Four Great Schools of Rajasthani Painting."

# 1. The Four Major Painting Schools (राजस्थानी चित्रकला की 4 शैलियां) 

                              4 SCHOOLS OF RAJASTHAN PAINTING (चित्रकला शैलियां)
                                                      │
         ┌───────────────────────────┬────────────────┴───────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┐
         │                           │                                            │                           │
    MEWAR SCHOOL                MARWAR SCHOOL                                DHUNDHAR SCHOOL             HADOTI SCHOOL
   (मेवाड़ स्कूल)              (मारवाड़ स्कूल)                                (ढूंढाड़ स्कूल)             (हाड़ौती स्कूल)
         │                           │                                            │                           │
 • Mewar Main (Sahibdin)     • Kishangarh (Bani-Thani - Nihal Chand)      • Amer/Jaipur (आमेर/जयपुर) • Bundi (बूंदी - Bird/Nature)
 • Nathdwara (Pichhwai)      • Jodhpur (Yellow, Clouds, Camels)           • Alwar (अलवर - Courtesans)• Kota (कोटा - Hunting with Queens)
 • Chavand (Nasiruddin)      • Bikaner (Usta & Matheerna Art)             • Shekhawati Frescoes
 • Deogarh (Thikana Style)

# A. Mewar School (मेवाड़ स्कूल) — The Root of Rajput Art

  • Earliest Illustrated Manuscript: Savaga Padhikkamana Sutta Chunni (श्रावक प्रतिक्रमण सूत्र चूर्णी) (painted on palm-leaf in 1260 AD at Ahar during the reign of Rawal Tej Singh / तेजसिंह by artist Kamal Chandra / कमलचन्द्र).
  • Mewar Main Style:
    • Golden Era under Maharana Jagat Singh I (जगतसिंह प्रथम - 1628–1652 AD); established the royal art academy Chiteron ki Ovari (चितेरों की ओवरी) (popularly called Tasveera ro Karkhano / तस्वीरों रो कारखानो).
    • Master Artists: Sahibdin (साहिबदीन) (painted Ragamala, Rasikapriya, Geet Govinda) and Manohar (मनोहर) (painted Bal Kanda of Ramayana).
  • Nathdwara Sub-Style (नाथद्वारा शैली): 🔥 RPSC Favorite
    • Fusion of Mewar and Braj / Vallabh (बल्लभ सम्प्रदाय) sect traditions.
    • Pichhwai (पिछवाई): Large cloth backdrops hung behind the Shrinathji (श्रीनाथजी) idol depicting Krishna’s Baal-Leela, cows, lotus flowers, and Kadamba / banana trees.
    • Dominant Colors: Bright Yellow and Green (पीला व हरा रंग).
    • Notable Female Painters (महिला चित्रकार): Kamala and Ilaichi (कमला एवं इलायची) (frequently asked in RPSC!).
  • Chavand Sub-Style (चावण्ड शैली): Originated under Maharana Pratap (1585 AD); master artist Nasiruddin / Nisaradin (निसारदीन) painted the famous Ragamala (रागमाला) set in 1605 AD under Amar Singh I.
  • Deogarh Sub-Style (देवगढ़ शैली): Discovered and brought to global fame by art critic Dr. Shridhar Andhare (डॉ. श्रीधर अंधारे) (mix of Mewar, Marwar, and Dhundhar).

# B. Marwar School (मारवाड़ स्कूल) — Desert Realism & Ethereal Grace

# 1. Kishangarh Style & Bani-Thani (किशनगढ़ शैली एवं बणी-ठणी) 🔥 Highest Exam Priority

  • Golden Period: Reign of King Sawant Singh (सावंत सिंह) (who adopted the pen-name Nagardas / नागरीदास).
  • Masterpiece: Bani-Thani (बणी-ठणी) painted by court artist Nihal Chand (निहालचन्द).
  • Global Acclaim: Art historians Eric Dickinson (एरिक डिकिंसन) and Dr. Fayaz Ali (डॉ. फैयाज अली) discovered this style and hailed Bani-Thani as the "Mona Lisa of India" (भारत की मोनालिसा). Government of India issued a commemorative postage stamp in 1973.
  • Distinct Features: Long curved lotus eyes (Khonjanayan / खंजन नयन), arched thin eyebrows, sharp pointed nose with Besar (बेसर) ornament, transparent odhani, romantic Krishna-Radha themes around Gundolav Lake (गुंदोलाव झील).

# 2. Jodhpur & Bikaner Styles

  • Jodhpur Style (जोधपुर शैली): Bold lines, bright yellow and red colors, desert dunes, swirling dark clouds, mango trees, and camels. Peak under Maharaja Man Singh (मानसिंह) (Nath sect paintings like Nath Charitra / नाथ चरित्र).
  • Bikaner Style (बीकानेर शैली): Heavy Mughal refinement, soft pastel shades. Famous for:
    • Usta Art (उस्ता कला / मुनब्बती कला): Intricate embossed gold filigree work on cured camel hide (Padma Shri Hisamuddin Usta / हिसामुद्दीन उस्ता).
    • Matheerna Art (मथेरणा कला): Wet lime plaster religious fresco murals painted by traditional Jain Matheerna families.

# C. Dhundhar School (ढूंढाड़ स्कूल)

  • Amer / Jaipur Style (आमेर / जयपुर शैली): Founded by Sawai Jai Singh (established 36 royal workshops including Suratkhana / सूरतखाना); peak under Sawai Pratap Singh (सवाई प्रताप सिंह).
    • Arayash Technique (आलागीला / मोराकसी / फ्रेश्को बुनो): Traditional fresco-buono technique where mineral pigments are applied to wet lime-marble plaster and buffed to a mirror finish.
    • Master Artists: Sahibram (साहिबराम) (pioneered life-size portraits — Aadamkad / आदमकद चित्र of Maharaja Ishwari Singh) and Lalchand (लालचन्द) (master of animal-fight scenes).
  • Alwar Style (अलवर शैली): Illustrated copies of Sheikh Saadi’s Gulistan (गुलिस्तां) (by Baldev & Ghulam Ali under Raja Vinay Singh); unique for portraits of courtesans (Tawaifs / वेश्याओं के चित्र).
  • Shekhawati Frescoes (शेखावाटी के भित्ति चित्र): Havelis of Nawalgarh (नवलगढ़), Mandawa (मंडावा), and Fatehpur (फतेहपुर) decorated with magnificent external wall murals; known globally as the "Open Air Art Gallery" of India.

# D. Hadoti School (हाड़ौती स्कूल)

  • Bundi Style (Bird & Animal Style / पशु-पक्षी शैली): 🔥 Repeated PYQ
    • Characterized by lush forest vegetation, rain clouds, water springs, dancing peacocks, and monkeys.
    • Maharao Ummed Singh (उम्मेद सिंह) constructed the world-famous Chitrashala / Rangmahal (चित्रशाला / रंगमहल) inside Bundi Fort—a celestial gallery of wall paintings.
  • Kota Style (Hunting Style / शिकार शैली):
    • Peak under Maharao Ummed Singh I (उम्मेद सिंह प्रथम).
    • Unique RPSC Question: The only painting school in India where queens and royal ladies are depicted hunting wild lions and tigers (रानियों को शिकार करते हुए दिखाया गया) in dense jungles alongside kings!

# 2. Major Folk Arts & Master Handicrafts of Rajasthan (प्रमुख लोक कलाएं एवं हस्तशिल्प)

  • Phad Painting (फड़ चित्रकला - Shahpura, Bhilwara): 🔥 High-Yield
    • Painted scrolls on coarse Khadi cloth illustrating epics of folk deities (Pabuji, Devnarayanji).
    • Practiced exclusively by the Joshi family of Shahpura (शाहपुरा का जोशी परिवार) (Padma Shri Shree Lal Joshi / श्रीलाल जोशी; Parvati Joshi / पार्वती जोशी was India’s first female Phad painter).
  • Thewa Art (थेवा कला - Pratapgarh): 🔥 Global GI Tag
    • Delicate 23-carat gold filigree and engraving on colored Belgian glass (Kanch / कांच).
    • Practiced exclusively by the Rajsoni family of Pratapgarh (राजसोनी परिवार) (pioneered by Nathu Ji Soni / नाथू जी सोनी; family secrets strictly guarded).
  • Blue Pottery (ब्लू पॉटरी - Jaipur):
    • Ceramic glaze art made without clay (using quartz powder, raw glass, soda, and copper oxide).
    • Revived and globally popularized by Padma Shri Kripal Singh Shekhawat (कृपाल सिंह शेखावत) (developed Kripal Kumbh with 25 distinct color shades).
  • Terracotta Art (टेराकोटा - Molela, Rajsamand / मोलेला, राजसमंद):
    • Hollow relief plaques of folk deities (Devnarayanji, Goraji) molded from Banas clay and donkey dung by Kumhars (Padma Shri Mohan Lal Kumhar / मोहनलाल कुम्हार).
  • Kavad Art (कावड़ - Bassi, Chittorgarh / बस्सी, चित्तौड़गढ़):
    • Portable traveling wooden shrine (चलता-फिरता काष्ठ मन्दिर) with multi-layered folding painted doors narrating mythological epics; made by Suthar artisans (Prabhatji Suthar / प्रभात जी सुथार).

# ⚡ Chapter 5: 1-Minute Quick Cramming Table

Thewa Art (थेवा कला) 23-karat gold on Belgian glass Pratapgarh / Nathu Ji Soni (Rajsoni)
