# 📝 Chapter 7: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

Practice Q1 🎯 Tribal Folk Dances & Performance Styles
Which of the following folk dances of the Garasia tribe (गरासिया जनजाति) is performed in a slow, graceful circular formation without the accompaniment of any musical instrument (बिना किसी वाद्य यंत्र के)?
(A) Gair Dance (गैर नृत्य)
(B) Mandal Dance (मांदल नृत्य)
(C) Loor Dance (लूर नृत्य)
(D) Valar Dance (वालर नृत्य)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Valar Dance (वालर नृत्य)
Explanation: Valar is termed the "Ghoomar of the Garasias". Performed by men and women holding an umbrella or sword, it relies purely on rhythmic clapping and vocal cadence without any percussive musical instrument.
Examiner Trap: Gair is a Bhil dance using wooden sticks, whereas Valar is Garasia.
Practice Q2 🎯 Folk Theatre & Traditional Khyal Styles
In which traditional folk drama of Rajasthan do the two competing akhadas represent Shiva (Turra - saffron flag) and Parvati (Kalangi - green flag) in an open poetic contest without a written script?
(A) Jaipuri Tamasha (तमाशा)
(B) Kuchamani Khyal (कुचामणी ख्याल)
(C) Shekhawati Khyal (शेखावाटी ख्याल)
(D) Turra-Kalangi Khyal (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Turra-Kalangi Khyal (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल)
Explanation: Turra-Kalangi was founded ~400 years ago in Ghosunda/Chittorgarh by Hindu saint Tukangir (Turra - Shiva) and Muslim saint Shah Ali (Kalangi - Shakti/Parvati). The stage setup is called 'Dhangal' and the singing style is 'Ghammat'.
Practice Q3 🎯 UNESCO Intangible Heritage / Kalbelia
In 2010, the sensuous and serpentine Kalbelia Folk Dance of Rajasthan was inscribed on UNESCO's Representative List of the Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity. Which renowned exponent popularized this dance on international stages?
(A) Gulabo Sapera (गुलाबो सपेरा)
(B) Mangi Bai Arya (मांगी बाई आर्य)
(C) Gavari Bai (गवरी बाई)
(D) Allah Jilai Bai (अल्लाह जिलाई बाई)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Gulabo Sapera (गुलाबो सपेरा)
Explanation: Gulabo Sapera (Padma Shri recipient) brought Kalbelia dance to global prominence. The dance is performed to the musical accompaniment of Pungi / Been (पूंगी) and Khanjari (खंजरी). Sub-types include Indoni, Shankariya, Panihari, and Bagariya.
Practice Q4 🎯 Bhil Ritual Drama / Gavari
Consider the following statements regarding the Gavari (गवरी / राई) dance-drama of Mewar:
1. It is a 40-day ritualistic folk drama performed exclusively by Bhil men during the month of Bhadrapada.
2. It is based on the mythological battle between Lord Shiva (Pashupati) and Demon Bhasmasura (Boodiya).
3. The central anchor-clown characters who link the story episodes are called 'Kutkudiya' and 'Pat-Bhomia'.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(A) 1 and 3 only
(B) 1, 2 and 3 (All correct)
(C) 2 and 3 only
(D) 1 and 2 only
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) 1, 2 and 3 (All correct)
Explanation: Gavari is India's oldest surviving tribal dance-drama (often termed 'Meru Natya' / मेरु नाट्य). During the 40 days of Gavari, performers maintain strict celibacy, abstain from alcohol/meat, sleep on the floor, and travel from village to village.

# 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)

🎯 Tribal Folk Dances — Waler Dance of the Garasias — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
Which graceful circle dance of the Garasia tribe of Sirohi, celebrated as the 'Ghoomar of the Garasias', is performed in two concentric semicircles (men on outer, women on inner) by dancers holding swords/umbrellas WITHOUT the accompaniment of ANY musical instrument?
(A) Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य - सिरोही के गरासिया)
(B) Gavari Dance (राई / गवरी नृत्य - Bhils)
(C) Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य - Gujjars)
(D) Chakri Dance (चकरी नृत्य - Kanjar women)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य - सिरोही के गरासिया)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य)** is the iconic folk dance of the **Garasia tribe** (Mount Abu & Pindwara, Sirohi). Initiated by an elder carrying an umbrella (*Chhatri*) or sword, it progresses slowly to rhythmic hand clapping and foot-tapping **without any percussion or wind instruments**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Gavari? Gavari is a 40-day Bhil religious dance drama accompanied by the *Mandal* clay drum and *Thali*.
  • Why not (C) Chari? Chari is a Gujjar dance accompanied by Dhol, Thali, and Bankiya.
  • Why not (D) Chakri? Chakri is a rapid 80-whirl spinning dance of Kanjar girls in Hadoti accompanied by Dholak and Manjira.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Dance WITHOUT ANY musical instrument = Waler (Garasias of Sirohi); 40-day Shiva-Bhasmasura drama = Gavari (Bhils of Mewar).
🎯 Folk Theatre — Charbait Folk Opera of Tonk — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
The Charbait (चारबैत), a distinctive 4-line Urdu/Pashto musical folk theatre performed in traditional military soldier costumes with dramatic rhythms of the Dhaf (डफ), is the unique traditional theatre style of which former princely state of Rajasthan?
(A) Bikaner (Rammat)
(B) Tonk (टोंक का चारबैत - Started under Nawab Faizullah Khan by Khalifa Karim Khan)
(C) Jaipur (Tamasha)
(D) Alwar (Alibakshi Khyal)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Tonk (टोंक का चारबैत - Started under Nawab Faizullah Khan by Khalifa Karim Khan)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Charbait (चारबैत)** originated in Afghanistan and was introduced to the Nawabi princely state of **Tonk** during the reign of Nawab Faizullah Khan by Afghan soldier-artists **Abdul Karim Khan and Khalifa Karim Khan Nihang**. Performers recite four-line verses (*Char-bait*) in high-pitched voices while striking large handheld *Dhaf* tambourines.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Bikaner? Bikaner is the hub of *Rammat* folk drama on wooden platforms (*Pata Sanskriti*).
  • Why not (C) Jaipur? Jaipur is the home of *Tamasha* folk theatre (Banshidhar Bhatt).
  • Why not (D) Alwar? Alwar/Mundawar is the home of *Alibakshi Khyal*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Charbait (Pashto/Urdu folk opera with Dhaf) = Tonk; Rammat = Bikaner & Jaisalmer; Tamasha = Jaipur; Alibakshi = Alwar.
🎯 Folk Dramas — Turra-Kalangi Khyal (Chittorgarh) — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
In the 400-year-old popular Turra-Kalangi Khyal (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल) of Ghosunda and Chittorgarh, what do 'Turra' and 'Kalangi' respectively represent?
(A) Turra represents Lord Shiva (symbolized by Saffron Flag) and Kalangi represents Goddess Parvati (symbolized by Green Flag)
(B) Turra represents Lord Rama and Kalangi represents Lord Krishna
(C) Turra represents the Sun and Kalangi represents the Moon
(D) Turra represents Emperor Akbar and Kalangi represents Maharana Pratap
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Turra represents Lord Shiva (symbolized by Saffron Flag) and Kalangi represents Goddess Parvati (symbolized by Green Flag)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Turra-Kalangi Khyal was founded by two saint-poets: Hindu saint **Tukangir** (who worshipped Lord Shiva and wore the royal crest *Turra* with saffron flag) and Muslim saint **Shah Ali** (who worshipped Goddess Shakti/Parvati and wore the crest *Kalangi* with green flag). In this folk opera, two opposing parties engage in spontaneous poetic musical duels (*Dangal*) atop an open wooden stage without written scripts.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B)? Rama-Krishna folk operas are *Ram Lila* and *Ras Lila*.
  • Why not (C)? Sun-Moon symbolism is used in Hathayoga (*Ha-Tha*).
  • Why not (D)? Akbar-Pratap debates are modern historical enactments.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Turra = Shiva + Tukangir + Saffron flag; Kalangi = Parvati + Shah Ali + Green flag; Center = Ghosunda & Chittorgarh.
🎯 Folk Dances — Bhavai Dance & Physical Stunts — Deep Fact Check RPSC 2nd Grade & EO/RO
Which thrilling professional folk dance of Rajasthan, founded by Bagaji of Kekri (Ajmer), features dancers balancing 7 to 11 earthen/brass pots on their heads while balancing barefoot on the edges of naked swords and broken glass shards?
(A) Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य - Gujjar women)
(B) Bhavai Dance (भवाई नृत्य - Bagaji of Kekri)
(C) Kacchi Ghodi Dance (कच्छी घोड़ी नृत्य - Shekhawati)
(D) Gair Dance (गैर नृत्य - Bhils)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Bhavai Dance (भवाई नृत्य - Bagaji of Kekri)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Bhavai Dance (भवाई नृत्य)** (pioneered by Bagaji and Kankaji of Kekri) is Rajasthan's supreme dance of physical balance and daring acrobatics. Dancers balance towering columns of 8–11 brass pitchers (*Matkas*), pick handkerchiefs with their eyelids from the floor, and dance atop sword blades, brass *Thali* rims, and crushed glass shards. Famous artists: **Roop Singh Shekhawat, Dayaram, and Shrestha Soni**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Chari Dance? Chari dance is performed with brass pots containing burning cottonseeds on the head (*Falgu Bai*).
  • Why not (C) Kacchi Ghodi? Kacchi Ghodi is a dummy horse mock-battle dance from Shekhawati.
  • Why not (D) Gair Dance? Gair is a community circle dance of Bhil men using wooden sticks (*Khanda*).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Dancing on sword edge / 8-11 pots = Bhavai (Bagaji of Kekri); Flaming pots = Chari (Falgu Bai); Dummy horse = Kacchi Ghodi.
🎯 Folk Dances — Tribal Waler Dance (Garasia of Sirohi) — Deep Fact Check RPSC EO/RO & 2nd Grade
Which graceful folk dance of the Garasia tribe of Mount Abu / Sirohi is famously known as the 'Ghoomar of the Garasias' and is performed strictly WITHOUT the accompaniment of any musical instrument (बिना किसी वाद्य यंत्र के)?
(A) Gair Dance (गैर नृत्य - Bhils with wooden sticks)
(B) Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य - Garasias of Sirohi)
(C) Agni Dance (अग्नि नृत्य - Jasnathi Siddhs)
(D) Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य - Gujjar women)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य - Garasias of Sirohi)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य)** is performed by Garasia men and women in two concentric semicircles. A male leader begins the rhythm with an open umbrella (*Chhata*) or sword. Uniquely in Rajasthan folk dance, **no drums, flutes, or musical instruments are used**—the rhythm is kept entirely by vocal clapping and coordinated footwork.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Gair Dance? Gair is performed with heavy wooden sticks (*Khanda*) accompanied by loud *Dhol*, *Bankiya*, and *Thali*.
  • Why not (C) Agni Dance? Agni dance is performed to the loud beating of the *Nagada* drum at Katariasar.
  • Why not (D) Chari Dance? Chari dance is performed with brass pots accompanied by the *Dhol*, *Bankiya*, and *Thali*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Dance WITHOUT any musical instrument = Waler Dance (Garasia tribe, Sirohi); Dummy horse dance = Kacchi Ghodi; 40-day harvest dance = Gavari.
🎯 Folk Theatre — Rammat of Bikaner & Jaisalmer (Tez Kavi) — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre
In 1943 during the Quit India Movement, which revolutionary folk poet authored the anti-British Rammat play 'Swatantra Bawani' (स्वतंत्र बावनी) and boldly gifted a copy directly to Mahatma Gandhi?
(A) Banshidhar Bhatt (बंशीधर भट्ट - Jaipur Tamasha)
(B) Lachhiram (लच्छीराम - Kuchamani Khyal)
(C) Tez Kavi of Jaisalmer (तेज कवि जैसलमेरी)
(D) Janakilal Bhand (जानकीलाल भांड - Behrupia artist)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Tez Kavi of Jaisalmer (तेज कवि जैसलमेरी)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Folk playwright **Tez Kavi (तेज कवि)** of Jaisalmer founded the *Shri Krishna Company* and penned *'Swatantra Bawani'* in 1943, presenting it to Mahatma Gandhi. When British police came to arrest him, he famously challenged the commissioner outside the police station: 'कमिश्नर खोल दरवाजा, हमें भी जेल जाना है। हिंद परदेसियों का है, न तेरे बाप का खाना है॥'.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Banshidhar Bhatt? Banshidhar Bhatt was the pioneer of the **Tamasha** musical theatre of Jaipur.
  • Why not (B) Lachhiram? Lachhiram was the pioneer who codified the **Kuchamani Khyal** (author of *Chandmal*, *Rao Ridmal*).
  • Why not (D) Janakilal Bhand? Janakilal Bhand of Bhilwara ('Monkey Man') is the living master of the **Behrupia (स्वांग)** masquerade art.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Swatantra Bawani (1943) gifted to Gandhi = Tez Kavi (Rammat, Jaisalmer); Kuchamani Khyal = Lachhiram; Tamasha = Banshidhar Bhatt; Behrupia = Janakilal Bhand.
🎯 Folk Dances — Kalbelia & UNESCO World Heritage — Deep Concept Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
Which world-renowned female exponent from Rajasthan propelled the serpent-mimicking Kalbelia Dance (कालबेलिया नृत्य) onto the global stage, leading to its inscription in UNESCO's Representative List of Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity in 2010?
(A) Falgu Bai of Kishangarh (फलकू बाई - Chari Dance)
(B) Gulabo Sapera (गुलाबो सपेरा - Kalbelia Dance)
(C) Gavari Bai of Dungarpur (गवरी बाई - Bhakti Singer)
(D) Shreshtha Soni of Udaipur (श्रेष्ठा सोनी - Bhavai Dance)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Gulabo Sapera (गुलाबो सपेरा - Kalbelia Dance)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Padma Shri **Gulabo Sapera** from Ajmer brought the nomadic Kalbelia snake charmer dance to international fame. The dance features black swirling skirts, agile acrobatic flexibility, and is accompanied by the *Pungi (Been)* and *Khanjari*. In 2010, UNESCO officially recognized it as Intangible Cultural Heritage.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Falgu Bai? Falgu Bai of Kishangarh is the master dancer of the **Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य)** of the Gujjar community, balancing brass pots with burning cottonseeds.
  • Why not (C) Gavari Bai? Gavari Bai of Dungarpur is a saintly 18th-century devotional singer revered as the *'Meera of Vagad' (वागड़ की मीरा)*.
  • Why not (D) Shreshtha Soni? Shreshtha Soni (along with Dayaram and Tara Sharma) is a famous exponent of the **Bhavai Dance (भवाई नृत्य)** who set world records balancing brass pots on her head.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Kalbelia & UNESCO (2010) = Gulabo Sapera; Chari Dance = Falgu Bai; Meera of Vagad = Gavari Bai; Bhavai = Shreshtha Soni / Dayaram.
🎯 Folk Theatre — Turra Kalangi Tradition — Deep Concept Check RPSC Assistant Professor & EO/RO
In the 400-year-old Turra-Kalangi Khyal (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल) folk opera tradition of Ghosunda and Chittorgarh, the 'Turra' (तुर्रा) and 'Kalangi' (कलंगी) emblems symbolize which two divine cosmic principles?
(A) Turra = Lord Rama, Kalangi = Lord Krishna
(B) Turra = Lord Shiva (Hindu saint Tukangir), Kalangi = Goddess Shakti (Muslim saint Shah Ali)
(C) Turra = Lord Vishnu, Kalangi = Goddess Lakshmi
(D) Turra = Surya Dev, Kalangi = Chandra Dev
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Turra = Lord Shiva (Hindu saint Tukangir), Kalangi = Goddess Shakti (Muslim saint Shah Ali)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Turra-Kalangi was created in Chanderi when Hindu saint **Tukangir** (who worshipped Shiva - Turra crest) and Sufi saint **Shah Ali** (who worshipped Shakti - Kalangi crest) engaged in friendly poetic duels. The ruler of Chanderi awarded them royal crowns with Turra and Kalangi. In Rajasthan, Ghosunda, Nimbahera, and Chittorgarh are its primary centers, performed as an unscripted poetic competition (*Dangal*) using the Chang instrument.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A)? Vaishnava legends of Rama and Krishna are the primary themes of *Rammat* and *Rasleela*, not Turra-Kalangi.
  • Why not (C)? Lakshmi-Narayan themes predominate in classical Temple Dhrupad music.
  • Why not (D)? Solar and lunar motifs are dynastic symbols, not the theological basis of Turra-Kalangi.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Turra = Shiva (Tukangir); Kalangi = Shakti (Shah Ali); Main stage = Dangal; Key instrument = Chang.

# 🎯 Chapter 7: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

Q1 RPSC RAS Pre 2016, 2021 / SI 2021
The sensational 'Kalbeliya Dance' (कालबेलिया नृत्य) was inscribed on UNESCO's Representative List of Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity in which year?
(A) 2008
(B) 2010
(C) 2012
(D) 2015
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) 2010
Explanation: Performed by women of the snake-charmer community dressed in black embroidered skirts (*Ghaghra*) replicating serpentine movements to the tune of Pungi and Khanjari. Gulabo Sapera brought it international acclaim.
Q2 RPSC 2nd Grade 2022 / EO/RO 2022
'Terah Taali Dance' (तेरह ताली नृत्य) is traditionally performed by women of the Kamada community in devotion to which folk deity?
(A) Pabuji
(B) Gogaji
(C) Tejaji
(D) Baba Ramdevji
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Baba Ramdevji
Explanation: Dancers tie 13 cymbals (*Manjiras*) across their body (9 on the right leg, 2 on elbows, 2 in hands) and strike them with incredible dexterity while sitting and balancing pots/swords. Famous dancer: Mangi Bai.
Q3 RPSC Patwari 2021 / Constable 2020
Which dance is celebrated as the "State Dance of Rajasthan" (राज्य नृत्य) and the "Sirmaur of all folk dances" (नृत्यों का सिरमौर)?
(A) Gair
(B) Kachhi Ghodi
(C) Ghoomar (घूमर)
(D) Chari
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Ghoomar (घूमर)
Explanation: Performed on auspicious occasions like Gangaur and Teej with 8 rhythmic footsteps called *Sawai* (सवाई) and graceful swirling movements (*Ghoom*).
Q4 RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / RAS Pre 2021
The traditional folk theatre 'Turra-Kalangi Khyal' (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल) features poetic dialogues between:
(A) Shiva (Turra) and Shakti/Parvati (Kalangi)
(B) Krishna and Radha
(C) Rama and Ravana
(D) King and Minister
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Shiva (Turra) and Shakti/Parvati (Kalangi)
Explanation: Originated ~400 years ago when the ruler of Chanderi honored Tukangir (Hindu saint representing Shiva / Saffron flag) with *Turra* and Shah Ali (Muslim saint representing Shakti / Green flag) with *Kalangi*. Popular in Ghosunda and Chittor.
Q5 RPSC Assistant Professor 2021 / SI 2021
'Tamasha' (तमाशा) folk theatre of Jaipur was patronized during the reign of which Kachwaha ruler?
(A) Sawai Man Singh II
(B) Sawai Jai Singh II
(C) Sawai Pratap Singh
(D) Sawai Ram Singh II
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Sawai Pratap Singh
Explanation: Introduced to Jaipur from Maharashtra by the Bhatt family (Banshidhar Bhatt and Gopiji Bhatt) during the reign of Sawai Pratap Singh (1778–1803 AD). It is performed on an open stage called *Akhada*.
Q6 RPSC 2nd Grade 2019 / Patwari 2021
The thrilling acrobatic folk dance 'Bhavai' (भवाई नृत्य) was pioneered by which community leader?
(A) Pundarik Vitthal
(B) Ali Baksh
(C) Lachhiram
(D) Bagaji Jat of Kekri
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Bagaji Jat of Kekri
Explanation: Involves balancing 7 to 9 brass/earthen pitchers on the head while dancing on edge of swords, brass plates, and glass shards. Renowned dancers: Shreshtha Soni, Roop Singh Shekhawat, Tara Sharma.
Q7 RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / 1st Grade 2022
Who was the founder and promoter of the 'Kuchamani Khyal' (कुचामणी ख्याल) style of folk opera?
(A) Lachhiram (लच्छीराम)
(B) Nanuram
(C) Ugamraj
(D) Duliyaran
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Lachhiram (लच्छीराम)
Explanation: Lachhiram created poetic folk operas such as *Chand Malagiri*, *Rao Ridmal*, and *Meera Mangal*. Performed with forceful operatic singing accompanied by Shehnai and Dholak.
Q8 RPSC REET 2022 / 2nd Grade 2022
'Gair Dance' (गैर नृत्य) is performed on the occasion of Holi primarily by the men of which community?
(A) Kalbeliyas of Jaisalmer
(B) Bhil and Rajput men of Mewar & Barmer
(C) Meenas of Jaipur
(D) Sahariyas of Baran
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Bhil and Rajput men of Mewar & Barmer
Explanation: Dancers strike wooden sticks called *Khanda* in concentric circles (*Gher*).

CHAPTER 8
Chapter 8: Folk Musical Instruments of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के लोक वाद्य यंत्र)

🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The 4-Way Classification (वाद्य यंत्रों का वर्गीकरण)
"In ancient Indian musicology (Bharata Muni’s Natyashastra - भरत मुनि का नाट्यशास्त्र), musical instruments are classified into four scientific families based on acoustic vibration. In RPSC exams, 90% of questions are simple identification tests: 'Which of the following is a Tat / Sushir / Avanaddha / Ghan instrument?' Memorize the four categories and you will never lose a mark here."

# The 4 Scientific Instrument Families (वाद्य यंत्रों के 4 प्रकार) 

                           4 INSTRUMENT FAMILIES OF RAJASTHAN (लोक वाद्य)
                                                 │
         ┌───────────────────────┬───────────────┴───────────────┬───────────────────────┐
         │                       │                               │                       │
     TAT VADYA              SUSHIR VADYA                    AVANADDHA VADYA          GHAN VADYA
 (तत वाद्य - Stringed)   (सुषिर वाद्य - Wind/Air)        (अवनद्ध वाद्य - Membrane) (घन वाद्य - Metallic)
         │                       │                               │                       │
 • Ravanhattha (रावणहत्था)• Algoza (अलगोजा - State Inst.) • Mridang / Pakhawaj     • Khartal (खड़ताल - Siddique Khan)
 • Kamayacha (कामायचा)   • Shehnai (शहनाई)               • Mandal (मांदल - Molela) • Manjira (मंजीरा - 13 in Terah Taali)
 • Jantar (जन्तर)        • Pungi / Been (पूंजी/बीन)      • Chang & Dhaf (चंग व डफ) • Jhanjh (झांझ - Kachhi Ghodi)
 • Sarangi (सारंगी)      • Morchang (मोरचंग - Jew's harp) • Nagada & Naubat (नगाड़ा)• Chimta & Thali (चिमटा व थाली)
 • Bhapang (भपंग)        • Bankiya & Nagphani (बांकिया)  • Tasha (ताशा)

# Detailed Category Profiles

  1. Tat Vadya (तत वाद्य - Chordophones / Stringed):
    • Ravanhattha (रावणहत्था): Half-cut coconut shell covered with goat hide, bamboo shaft, 9 strings. Played with horsehair bow (Gaj / गज) by Bhopas narrating Pabuji ki Phad (पाबूजी की फड़).
    • Kamayacha (कामायचा): Single block of mango wood, 16–17 strings, wide parchment belly. Soul of Manganiyar music (Padma Shri Sakar Khan / साकर खां मांगणियार).
    • Jantar (जन्तर): Two round gourds, 5–6 strings. Hung around neck and played standing by Gurjar Bhopas narrating Devnarayanji ki Phad (देवनारायण जी की फड़).
    • Sarangi (सारंगी): Most melodious bowed instrument with 27–29 gut strings; played by Langa musicians (लांगा गायक).
    • Bhapang (भपंग): Single string plucked gourd instrument of Mewat (Zahoor Khan Mewati / जहूर खां मेवाती).
  2. Sushir Vadya (सुषिर वाद्य - Aerophones / Wind):
    • Algoza (अलगोजा): 🔥 State Musical Instrument Pair of matching beaked flutes played simultaneously in mouth with continuous circular breathing (राजस्थान का राज्य वाद्य यंत्र).
    • Morchang (मोरचंग): Smallest iron instrument (Jew's harp); held in teeth while blowing and plucking the iron tongue.
    • Shehnai (शहनाई), Pungi (पूंजी/बीन), Bankiya (बांकिया), Satara (सतारा), Nagphani (नागफणी), Mashak (मशक - Bagpipe), Shankh (शंख).
  3. Avanaddha Vadya (अवनद्ध वाद्य - Membranophones / Percussion Drums):
    • Mridang / Pakhawaj (मृदंग / पखावज): Barrel drum (Padma Shri Pt. Purushottam Das / पं. पुरुषोत्तम दास).
    • Mandal (मांदल): Earthen drum hand-molded in Molela (मोलेला - Rajsamand); played in tribal dances.
    • Chang & Dhaf (चंग व डफ): Circular wooden frame covered with sheepskin played during Shekhawati Holi.
    • Nagada & Naubat (नगाड़ा व नौबत): Massive kettle drums at fort/temple gates (Ramkishan Solanki / रामकिशन सोलंकी).
  4. Ghan Vadya (घन वाद्य - Idiophones / Metallic Resonance):
    • Khartal (खड़ताल): Pair of polished Sheesham clappers (Siddique Khan Manganiyar / सिद्दीक खां मांगणियार - Khartal Wizard).
    • Manjira (मंजीरा): 13 brass cymbals used in Terah Taali.
    • Jhanjh (झांझ): Large bronze cymbals clapped in Kachhi Ghodi dance.

# ⚡ Chapter 8: 1-Minute Quick Cramming Table

Category Instruments Included Key Identifying Feature
Tat (तत - String) Ravanhattha, Kamayacha, Jantar, Sarangi, Bhapang Ravanhattha = Pabuji Phad | Jantar = Devnarayanji Phad
Sushir (सुषिर - Wind) Algoza, Shehnai, Pungi, Morchang, Bankiya, Mashak Algoza = State Instrument (twin flutes) | Morchang = Jew's harp
Avanaddha (अवनद्ध - Drum) Mridang, Mandal, Chang, Nagada, Naubat, Tasha Mandal = Molela earthen drum | Chang = Shekhawati Holi
Ghan (घन - Metal) Khartal, Manjira, Jhanjh, Chimta, Thali Khartal = Siddique Khan | Manjira = 13 in Terah Taali

# 📝 Chapter 8: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

Practice Q1 🎯 Classification of Rajasthani Musical Instruments
Which of the following is the state musical instrument of Rajasthan (राजस्थान का राज्य वाद्य यंत्र) that belongs to the Sushir (Wind / फूँक वाद्य) category and consists of a pair of identical flutes played simultaneously?
(A) Morchang (मोरचंग)
(B) Algoza (अलगोजा)
(C) Shehnai (शहनाई)
(D) Satara (सतारा)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Algoza (अलगोजा)
Explanation: Algoza is the official state instrument of Rajasthan. It features two beak-flutes blown together (one provides tonic drone while the other plays the melody). Renowned players include Ramnath Chaudhary (who famously plays Algoza using his nostrils).
Practice Q2 🎯 Tat & Avanaddh Categorization
Consider the following musical instruments:
1. Kamayacha (कामायचा)
2. Ravanhattha (रावणहत्था)
3. Bhapang (भपंग)
4. Khartal (खड़ताल)
Which of the instruments given above belong to the Tat Vadya (Stringed / तार वाद्य) category?
(A) 1 and 2 only
(B) 2, 3 and 4 only
(C) 1, 2 and 3 only
(D) 1, 2, 3 and 4
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) 1, 2 and 3 only
Explanation: Kamayacha (bowed string with 17 strings), Ravanhattha (half coconut shell with 9 strings), and Bhapang (plucked single-string drum-tumbi) are all Tat Vadya. Khartal is a Ghan Vadya (Idiophone / wooden clapper) whose legendary virtuoso was Siddique Khan Manganiyar.
Practice Q3 🎯 Folk Instrument Virtuosos
Match List-I (Folk Instrument) with List-II (Renowned Maestro):
List-I (Instrument)
(A) Kamayacha (कामायचा)
(B) Bhapang (भपंग)
(C) Khartal (खड़ताल)
(D) Pakhawaj / Mridang (पखावज)
List-II (Maestro)
(i) Zahoor Khan Mewati & Umar Farooq Mewati
(ii) Sakar Khan Manganiyar (पद्मश्री)
(iii) Pandit Purushottam Das of Nathdwara
(iv) Siddique Khan Manganiyar
(A) (A)-(i), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)
(B) (A)-(ii), (B)-(iii), (C)-(iv), (D)-(i)
(C) (A)-(iv), (B)-(i), (C)-(ii), (D)-(iii)
(D) (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(iii)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(iii)
Explanation:
• Kamayacha = Sakar Khan (Padma Shri 2012).
• Bhapang = Zahoor Khan Mewati ("Jadugar of Bhapang").
• Khartal = Siddique Khan Manganiyar ("Jadugar of Khartal").
• Pakhawaj = Pandit Purushottam Das of Nathdwara (Padma Shri).
Practice Q4 🎯 Membranophones / Avanaddh Vadya
Which of the following is an Avanaddh (Membranophone / Percussion) instrument made of baked clay (मिट्टी का मांदल) by the potters of Molela (Rajsamand) and played exclusively by Bhil and Garasia tribes during folk rituals?
(A) Dhonsa (धौंसा)
(B) Daph (डफ)
(C) Mandal (मांदल)
(D) Chang (चंग)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Mandal (मांदल)
Explanation: Mandal is an ancient cylindrical percussion drum made of baked clay crafted at Molela. The Garasias perform the spirited Mandal dance to its beats.

# 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)

🎯 Folk Instruments — Bhapang & Zahoor Khan Mewati — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre
The Bhapang (भपंग), an ancient Tat (stringed) percussion instrument shaped like a hollow cylindrical tumba/gourd with a single gut string plucked while varying tension under the armpit, was popularized globally by which legendary master of the Mewat region (Alwar)?
(A) Padma Shri Sakar Khan (Kamayacha maestro)
(B) Ramnath Chaudhary (Algoza player from nose)
(C) Zahoor Khan Mewati & Umar Farooq Mewati (जहूर खां मेवाती एवं उमर फारूक मेवाती - Bhapang maestros)
(D) Padma Shri Siddique Khan (Khartal maestro)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Zahoor Khan Mewati & Umar Farooq Mewati (जहूर खां मेवाती एवं उमर फारूक मेवाती - Bhapang maestros)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Zahoor Khan Mewati is immortalized as the 'Magician of the Bhapang' (भपंग का जादूगर), along with his illustrious successor **Umar Farooq Mewati**. The Bhapang is played extensively by Jogi singers in Alwar/Mewat while narrating the epics of Raja Gopichand and Raja Bharthari.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Sakar Khan? Sakar Khan was the Padma Shri maestro of the **Kamayacha** in Jaisalmer.
  • Why not (B) Ramnath Chaudhary? Ramnath Chaudhary of Jaipur is famous for playing the **Algoza through his nostrils**.
  • Why not (D) Siddique Khan? Siddique Khan was the *'Magician of Khartal'*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Bhapang maestro = Zahoor Khan Mewati (Alwar); Kamayacha maestro = Sakar Khan (Jaisalmer); Khartal maestro = Siddique Khan; Nose Algoza = Ramnath Chaudhary.
🎯 Folk Instruments — Kamayacha & Padma Shri Sakar Khan — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & College Lecturer
The Kamayacha (कमायचा), a majestic 27-stringed bowed instrument carved out of a single solid piece of mango wood with a goatskin-covered resonator and regarded as the 'King of Bowed Instruments', is the hallmark instrument of which traditional Muslim folk musician community of the Thar Desert?
(A) Langa Community (लांगा समुदाय - Sarangi masters)
(B) Kalbelia Community (कालबेलिया - Pungi masters)
(C) Manganiyar Community (मांगणियार समुदाय - Master: Padma Shri Sakar Khan)
(D) Bhopas of Pabuji (भोपा - Ravanhattha masters)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Manganiyar Community (मांगणियार समुदाय - Master: Padma Shri Sakar Khan)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Kamayacha** is played exclusively by the **Manganiyar community** of Jaisalmer and Barmer. Bowed with horsehair (*Gaj*), its 27 steel and gut strings produce deep resonant harmonies accompanying desert songs. Legend **Padma Shri Sakar Khan** (awarded 2012) was the greatest maestro of Kamayacha in world history.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Langas? The Muslim Langa community of Jodhpur/Barmer specializes in the **Sindhi Sarangi** and **Gujrati Sarangi**.
  • Why not (B) Kalbelias? Kalbelias play the **Pungi (Been)** and **Khanjari**.
  • Why not (D) Bhopas? Nayak Bhopas play the **Ravanhattha**.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Kamayacha = Manganiyars (Sakar Khan); Sarangi = Langas; Ravanhattha = Pabuji Nayak Bhopas; Jantar = Devnarayanji Gurjar Bhopas.
🎯 Folk Instruments — Percussion Avanaddha (Mandal & Chang) — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade
The Mandal (मांदल), an ancient folk percussion instrument made out of baked terracotta clay from the artisan village of Molela and played by Bhils during the 40-day Gavari dance drama, belongs to which category of Indian musical instruments?
(A) Tat Vadya (Chordophone / String Instrument)
(B) Ghana Vadya (Idiophone / Solid metal-wood Instrument)
(C) Sushir Vadya (Aerophone / Wind Instrument)
(D) Avanaddha Vadya (Membranophone / Leather-covered percussion instrument - अवनद्ध वाद्य)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Avanaddha Vadya (Membranophone / Leather-covered percussion instrument - अवनद्ध वाद्य)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Mandal (मांदल) is an **Avanaddha Vadya (अवनद्ध / ताल वाद्य)**. Its barrel-shaped body is sculpted out of baked red clay by Molela potters and both drumheads are covered with goat parchment skin. It is played with hands during the Bhil *Gavari* and Garasia *Mandal* dances.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Tat? *Tat* instruments use vibrating strings (Ravanhattha, Kamayacha, Jantar, Bhapang).
  • Why not (B) Ghana? *Ghana* instruments are solid metal/wood struck together (Khartal, Manjira, Thali, Chimta).
  • Why not (C) Sushir? *Sushir* instruments are blown with breath (Algoza, Shehnai, Pungi, Bankiya, Morchang).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Clay drum = Mandal (Avanaddha); State Instrument = Algoza (Sushir); 4 wooden clappers = Khartal (Ghana); 9 strings coconut = Ravanhattha (Tat).
🎯 Folk Musical Instruments — Official State Instrument (Algoza) — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
Which instrument is officially designated as the 'State Musical Instrument of Rajasthan' (राजस्थान का राज्य वाद्य यंत्र), consisting of a pair of joined beak flutes blown simultaneously through circular breathing?
(A) Shehnai (शहनाई)
(B) Bankiya (बांकिया)
(C) Pungi / Been (पूंजी)
(D) Algoza (अलगोजा - Double beak flute)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Algoza (अलगोजा - Double beak flute)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Algoza (अलगोजा)** is the **Official State Musical Instrument of Rajasthan**. It comprises two hollow bamboo or Sheesham wood flutes (each with 4 holes) played simultaneously in the mouth. Master musician **Ramnath Chaudhary** of Padampura (Jaipur) gained worldwide fame by playing the Algoza using his nostrils / nose!

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Shehnai? Shehnai is a conical classical aerophone played during auspicious weddings.
  • Why not (B) Bankiya? Bankiya is a curved brass trumpet played by the Sargara community.
  • Why not (C) Pungi? Pungi (Been) is crafted from a dried bottle-gourd used by Kalbelia snake charmers.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: State Musical Instrument = Algoza (Double flute); Nose player = Ramnath Chaudhary; 27-stringed bowed lute = Kamayacha (Sakar Khan).
🎯 Folk Musical Instruments — Bhapang of Mewat — Deep Concept Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
Which single-stringed plucked gourd instrument belonging to the Tat Vadya (Chordophone) category is famously associated with Zahoor Khan Mewati (जहूर खां मेवाती) as the 'King of the Instrument'?
(A) Kamayacha (कमायचा - Played by Manganiyars)
(B) Bhapang (भपंग - Mewati folk instrument)
(C) Ravanhattha (रावणहत्था - Pabuji Phad)
(D) Jantar (जन्तर - Devnarayanji Phad)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Bhapang (भपंग - Mewati folk instrument)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Bhapang (भपंग)** is a plucked stringed instrument crafted from a hollow dry bottle-gourd (*Tumbe*) covered with goat skin and played with a bamboo plectrum under the armpit. **Zahoor Khan Mewati** and **Umar Farooq Mewati** of Alwar are its legendary world masters.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Kamayacha? The Kamayacha is a 27-stringed bowed instrument made of mango wood, played by Manganiyar folk masters like Padma Shri Sakar Khan of Barmer/Jaisalmer.
  • Why not (C) Ravanhattha? The Ravanhattha is a 9-stringed bow-and-coconut instrument played by Nayak Bhopas to recite the Phad of Pabuji.
  • Why not (D) Jantar? The Jantar is an ancient veena-like instrument with two gourds and 5 or 6 strings, played resting on the chest by Gurjar Bhopas during the Phad of Devnarayanji.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Bhapang = Mewat (Zahoor Khan); Kamayacha = Marwar (Sakar Khan); Jantar = Devnarayanji Phad (Gurjars); Ravanhattha = Pabuji Phad.
🎯 Folk Musical Instruments — Percussion & Idiophones — Deep Concept Check RPSC Senior Teacher & SI
Which of the following Rajasthani musical instruments is classified as an Avanaddha Vadya (Membranophone / अवनद्ध वाद्य — Percussion with Leather Skin)?
(A) Khartal (खड़ताल - Wood clapper idiophone)
(B) Morchang (मोरचंग - Iron Jew's harp wind instrument)
(C) Mandal / Madal (मांदल - Terracotta clay drum)
(D) Manjira (मंजीरा - Brass cymbal idiophone)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Mandal / Madal (मांदल - Terracotta clay drum)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Mandal (मांदल)** is a traditional barrel drum made of a baked clay body (crafted by the potters of Molela) and covered with animal skin on both ends, played by Bhil and Garasia tribes (Gavari and Gair dances).

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Khartal? Khartal is a solid idiophone (**Ghana Vadya / घन वाद्य**) made of four sheesham wood clappers.
  • Why not (B) Morchang? Morchang is an iron wind instrument (**Sushir Vadya / सुषिर वाद्य**) held between the teeth and blown with the mouth (Jew's harp).
  • Why not (D) Manjira? Manjira is a pair of brass cymbals classified under **Ghana Vadya (घन वाद्य)**, used in Terah Taali and devotional Bhajans.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Mandal/Chang/Dhol/Dhak = Avanaddha (Skin); Manjira/Khartal/Thali/Chimta = Ghana (Metal/Wood); Morchang/Algoza = Sushir (Wind); Ravanhattha/Bhapang = Tat (String).

# 🎯 Chapter 8: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

Q1 RPSC EO/RO 2022 / RAS Pre 2021
Which of the following is/are Sushir (Aerophone / Wind) musical instrument(s) of Rajasthani folk music? (A) Algoza, (B) Bhapang, (C) Satara, (D) Nad
(A) Only (A)
(B) (A) and (B)
(C) (A), (C) and (D)
(D) (A), (B) and (C)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) (A), (C) and (D)
Explanation: Algoza, Satara, and Nad are Sushir (wind) instruments blown with air. Bhapang is a Tat (stringed / chordophone) instrument played under the armpit!
Q2 RPSC SI 2021 / 2nd Grade 2022
Which instrument is designated as the "Official State Musical Instrument" (राज्य वाद्य यंत्र) of Rajasthan?
(A) Ravanhattha
(B) Khartal
(C) Algoza (अलगोजा)
(D) Shehnai
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Algoza (अलगोजा)
Explanation: Algoza consists of a pair of joined wooden beak-flutes played simultaneously through circular breathing (*continuous airflow*). Famous player: Ramnath Chaudhary.
Q3 RPSC RAS Pre 2016, 2018 / Patwari 2021
'Ravanhattha' (रावणहत्था) — the ancient stringed bow instrument used during the recital of *Pabuji ki Phad* — is constructed using:
(A) Hollow pumpkin shell
(B) Solid Sheesham wood only
(C) Half coconut shell covered with goat skin
(D) Clay pot
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Half coconut shell covered with goat skin
Explanation: It has 9 strings (including horsetail hairs). The bow (*Gaj*) has tiny brass bells (*Ghungroos*) that chime rhythmically as the Bhopa sings.
Q4 RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / SI 2021
'Kamayacha' (कामायचा) — the 27-stringed bowed instrument played by the Manganiyar community — is crafted from which wood?
(A) Neem
(B) Bamboo
(C) Mango (Aam) or Rohida wood
(D) Teak (Sagwan)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Mango (Aam) or Rohida wood
Explanation: It has a large rounded belly covered in goatskin. Legendary master: Padma Shri Sakar Khan Manganiyar (Hamira, Jaisalmer).
Q5 RPSC 1st Grade 2020 / REET 2021
'Bhapang' (भपंग) — the plucked drum made from a hollowed dried gourd — is famously associated with which folk artist from Mewat?
(A) Ramnath Chaudhary
(B) Sakar Khan
(C) Zahoor Khan Mewati
(D) Saddiq Khan
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Zahoor Khan Mewati
Explanation: Zahoor Khan Mewati and Umar Farooq Mewati are the wizards of Bhapang in Alwar/Mewat, reciting folk tales of *Gopichand* and *Bharthari*.
Q6 RPSC 2nd Grade 2019
Pandit Purushottam Das of Nathdwara is a nationally revered maestro of which percussion instrument?
(A) Pakhawaj / Mridang (पखावज)
(B) Nagada
(C) Chang
(D) Tabla
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Pakhawaj / Mridang (पखावज)
Explanation: Pandit Purushottam Das received the Sangeet Natak Akademi award and Padma Shri for elevating the Haveli Sangeet Pakhawaj tradition of Nathdwara.
Q7 RPSC Patwari 2021 / Constable 2020
'Khartal' (खड़ताल) — the pair of wooden clappers that produce sharp metallic beats — belongs to which instrument category?
(A) Avanaddha Vadhya
(B) Tat Vadhya
(C) Sushir Vadhya
(D) Ghana Vadhya (घन वाद्य - Idiophone)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Ghana Vadhya (घन वाद्य - Idiophone)
Explanation: Ghana instruments produce sound through solid percussion without strings or membranes. Master player: Siddiqui Khan Manganiyar ("Wizard of Khartal").
Q8 RPSC Assistant Professor 2021
'Mandal' (मांदल) — the earthen cylindrical drum played by the Garasia tribe — is manufactured at which pottery village?
(A) Baswa (Dausa)
(B) Bagru (Jaipur)
(C) Molela (Rajsamand)
(D) Akola (Chittorgarh)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Molela (Rajsamand)
Explanation: Terracotta artisans of Molela shape the clay bodies of Mandal drums which are then covered with sheepskin.

CHAPTER 9
Chapter 9: Rajasthani Language and Literature (राजस्थानी भाषा और साहित्य)

🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Rich Idiom of the Dunes (मरुवाणी का वैभव)
"Rajasthani is not a broken dialect of Hindi; it is an ancient, independent Indo-Aryan language with a literary heritage older than modern Hindi and Bengali! From the heroic Dingal (डिंगल) war poems chanted before Rajput cavalry charges to the soul-stirring couplets of Kripa Ram Khidiya's (कृपाराम खिड़िया) Rajiya, Rajasthani literature combined fiery courage with deep practical wisdom."

# 1. Linguistic Origin & Dialect Classification (भाषा की उत्पत्ति एवं वर्गीकरण)

  • Evolutionary Lineage: $$\text{Vedic Sanskrit (वैदिक संस्कृत)} \longrightarrow \text{Prakrit (शौरसेनी प्राकृत)} \longrightarrow \text{Maru-Gurjari Apabhramsa (मरु-गुर्जरी अपभ्रंश)} \longrightarrow \text{Rajasthani / Marubhasha (राजस्थानी / मरुभाषा)}$$
  • First Textual Evidence: Udyottan Suri (उद्योतन सूरि - 778 AD / VS 835) mentioned 'Marubhasha' (मरुभाषा) among 18 native languages (Desi Bhashas / देशी भाषाएं) in his classic work Kuvalayamala (कुवलयमाला).
  • Coined by George Abraham Grierson (जॉर्ज अब्राहम ग्रियर्सन - 1912): First linguist to use the formal collective term 'Rajasthani' (राजस्थानी) in his monumental work Linguistic Survey of India (भारतीय भाषा सर्वेक्षण).

# 2. Major Regional Dialects (प्रमुख बोलियां) 🔥 Match Table

  • Western Dialects (Dingal Literary Tradition / डिंगल साहित्य शैली):
    • Marwari (मारवाड़ी): Standard literary language of Western Rajasthan (Jodhpur, Nagaur, Jaisalmer, Pali, Barmer). Sub-dialects: Thali (थली), Godwadi (गोडवाड़ी).
    • Mewari (मेवाड़ी): Spoken in Udaipur, Chittorgarh, Rajsamand, Bhilwara.
    • Wagdi / Bagri (वागड़ी / बागड़ी): Spoken in Dungarpur and Banswara (strong Gujarati influence; Grierson called it "Bhili dialect" / भीली बोली).
  • Eastern Dialects (Pingal Literary Tradition / पिंगल साहित्य शैली):
    • Dhundhari (ढूंढाड़ी): Spoken in Jaipur, Dausa, Tonk; characterized by the auxiliary verb 'Chhe' (छै / छो). Sub-dialects: Torawati (तोरावाटी), Rajawati (राजावाटी).
    • Hadoti (हाड़ौती): Spoken in Kota, Bundi, Jhalawar, Baran. Also uses 'Chhe'; Suryamal Misan composed in Hadoti.
    • Mewati (मेवाती): Spoken in Alwar and Bharatpur (Braj influence); Saint Laldas (लालदास जी) & Charandas (चरणदास जी) preached in Mewati.
    • Ahirwati / Rathi (अहीरवाटी / राठी): Spoken by Yadavs in Behror, Kotkasim, Mundawar (Alwar).

# 3. Four Eras & Specific Literary Genres (साहित्य के काल एवं विधाएं)

  1. Ancient / Viragatha Period (आरंभिक / वीरगाथा काल - 1050–1450 AD): Heroic ballads (Rannmal Chhand / रणमल छंद by Shridhar Vyas / श्रीधर व्यास).
  2. Pre-Medieval / Bhakti Period (पूर्व-मध्य / भक्ति काल - 1450–1650 AD): Veli Kisan Rukmani Ri (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री) by Prithviraj Rathore (पृथ्वीराज राठौड़ / पीथल) (hailed as the "5th Veda & 19th Purana" / पाँचवाँ वेद व 19वाँ पुराण by Durasa Arha / दुरसा आढ़ा); Mirabai Padavali (मीराबाई की पदावली); Hariraso (हरिरस) by Isardas (ईसरदास जी).
  3. Post-Medieval / Niti Period (उत्तर-मध्य / नीति व श्रृंगार काल - 1650–1850 AD): Rajiya ra Sortha (राजिया रा सोरठा) by Kripa Ram Khidiya (कृपाराम खिड़िया) (ethical couplets addressed to his loyal servant Rajiya).
  4. Modern Period (आधुनिक काल - 1850–Present): Kaviraj Bankidas (कविराजा बांकीदास) (Aayo Angrez Mulk Re Upar / आयो अंग्रेज मुल्क रे ऊपर) and Suryamal Misan (सूर्यमल्ल मीसण) (Vansh Bhaskar / वंश भास्कर, Veer Satsai / वीर सतसई).
📖 Terminology Decoder: Literary Forms & Genres (साहित्यिक विधाएं)
  • Khyat (ख्यात): Official historical chronicles of dynasties (*Nainsi ri Khyat / नैणसी री ख्यात, Dayaldas ri Khyat / दयालदास री ख्यात*).
  • Vachnika (वचनिका): Rhyming rhythmic prose-poetry (*Achaldas Khinchi ri Vachnika / अचलदास खींची री वचनिका* by Shivdas Gaadan / शिवदास गाडण).
  • Dawavait (दवावैत): Prose style incorporating Urdu and Persian loan words.
  • Vaat (वात): Traditional short story / folktale (*Pabuji ri Vaat / पाबूजी री वात*).
  • Vigat (विगत): Statistical gazetteer (*Marwar Ra Pargana Ri Vigat / मारवाड़ रा परगना री विगत* by Munhta Nainsi — **"Gazetteer of Rajasthan / राजस्थान का गजेटियर"**).
  • Raso (रासो): Heroic epics (*Prithviraj Raso / पृथ्वीराज रासो* by Chand Bardai, *Bisaldeo Raso / बीसलदेव रासो* by Narpati Nalha).

# 4. Renowned Modern Authors (आधुनिक साहित्यकार)

  • Vijaydan Detha ('Bijji') (विजयदान देथा 'बिज्जी' - 1926–2013): Born in Borunda (बोरून्दा - Jodhpur); co-founded Rupayan Sansthan (रूपायन संस्थान); authored 14-volume Rajasthani folk tale epic Baataan Ri Phulwari (बातां री फुलवारी); short story Duvidha (दुविधा) adapted into films Duvidha & Paheli; Padma Shri (2007) and Rajasthan Ratna (2012).
  • Kanhaiyalal Sethia (कन्हैयालाल सेठिया - 1919–2008): Born in Sujangarh (सुजानगढ़ - Churu); authored patriotic anthems "Dharti Dhoran Ri" (धरती धोरां री) and "Pathal aur Peethal" (पाथल और पीथल); Sahitya Akademi Award for Leeltans (लीलटांस - 1976); Moortidevi Award for Nirgranth (निर्ग्रन्थ); Padma Shri (2004).
  • Lakshmi Kumari Chundawat (लक्ष्मीकुमारी चूण्डावत): Authored Mumal (मूमल), Manjhal Raat (मांझल रात), Gir Uncha Uncha Garhan (गीर ऊंचा ऊंचा गढ़ां).
  • Major Journals (प्रमुख पत्रिकाएं): Jagti Jot (जागती जोत) (Rajasthani Bhasha Academy, Bikaner / राजस्थानी भाषा, साहित्य एवं संस्कृति अकादमी, बीकानेर) and Madhumati (मधुमती) (Rajasthan Sahitya Akademi, Udaipur / राजस्थान साहित्य अकादमी, उदयपुर).

# 📝 Chapter 9: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

Practice Q1 🎯 Classical Rajasthani Literature & Epics
The celebrated poetic treatise "Veli Krishan Rukmani Ri" (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री) was authored in the Dingal style by which royal poet, described by contemporary bard Durasa Adha as the "5th Veda and 19th Purana" (5वां वेद और 19वां पुराण)?
(A) Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner (पीथल)
(B) Kaviraja Shyamaldas (कविराजा श्यामलदास)
(C) Chandbardai (चंदबरदाई)
(D) Suryamalla Misran (सूर्यमल मीसण)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner (पीथल)
Explanation: Prithviraj Rathore (younger brother of Raja Rai Singh of Bikaner) composed Veli Krishan Rukmani Ri while stationed at Gagron Fort under Akbar. Italian linguist Dr. L.P. Tessitori called him the "Hero of Dingal" (डिंगल का हेरोस). In Kanhaiyalal Sethia's poem, he is immortalized as 'Peethal' writing to Maharana Pratap ('Paathal').
Practice Q2 🎯 State Poet & Modern Literature
Which of the following magnum opus works of Rajasthan's State Poet (राज्यकवि) Suryamalla Misran provides the detailed historical genealogy of the Hada Chauhans of Bundi and was completed after his demise by his adopted son Muraridhan?
(A) Vansh Bhaskar (वंश भास्कर)
(B) Veer Satsai (वीर सतसई)
(C) Chhandomayukh (छंदोमयूख)
(D) Balwant Vilas (बलवंत विलास)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Vansh Bhaskar (वंश भास्कर)
Explanation: Vansh Bhaskar is an epic Pingal-Dingal verse chronicle commissioned by Maharao Ramsingh of Bundi. Suryamalla Misran also composed Veer Satsai during the 1857 revolt to awaken Rajput valour against British colonial rule ("इला न देणी आपणी, हालरिये हुलराय...").
Practice Q3 🎯 Modern Literary Giants & Sahitya Akademi
Match List-I (Modern Rajasthani Writer) with List-II (Iconic Literary Creation):
List-I (Author)
(A) Kanhaiyalal Sethia (कन्हैयालाल सेठिया)
(B) Vijaydan Detha 'Bijji' (विजयदान देथा)
(C) Meghraj Mukul (मेघराज मुकुल)
(D) Chandra Singh Birwali (चंद्रसिंह बिरकाली)
List-II (Creation)
(i) Bataan ri Phulwari (बातां री फुलवारी, 14 खण्ड)
(ii) Sainani (सेनाणी — "चूंडावत मांगी सैनाणी, सिर काट दे दियो क्षत्राणी")
(iii) Patal aur Peethal & Leeltans (पातल और पीथल, लीलटांस)
(iv) Badli & Loo (बादली, लू)
(A) (A)-(i), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)
(B) (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(iii)
(C) (A)-(iii), (B)-(i), (C)-(ii), (D)-(iv)
(D) (A)-(iii), (B)-(iv), (C)-(i), (D)-(ii)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) (A)-(iii), (B)-(i), (C)-(ii), (D)-(iv)
Explanation:
• Kanhaiyalal Sethia: Born in Sujangarh (Churu); won Sahitya Akademi for Leeltans (1976), immortalized Dharti Dhoran Ri and Patal aur Peethal.
• Vijaydan Detha 'Bijji': Born in Borunda (Jodhpur); compiled 14 volumes of folklore in Bataan ri Phulwari; his story Duvidha became iconic films.
• Meghraj Mukul: Wrote Sainani describing Hadi Rani Sahal Kanwar's head sacrifice for Rao Ratan Singh Chundawat.
• Chandra Singh Birwali: Wrote Badli (first modern Rajasthani nature poem) and Loo.
Practice Q4 🎯 Linguistic Geography & Dialects
In 1912, which British linguist first coined the term "Rajasthani" for the languages spoken in Rajputana in his monumental work "Linguistic Survey of India" (Vol IX, Part II)?
(A) Col. James Tod
(B) Dr. Suniti Kumar Chatterji
(C) Dr. L.P. Tessitori
(D) Sir George Abraham Grierson
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Sir George Abraham Grierson
Explanation: Sir George Abraham Grierson first formally used the term "Rajasthani" in 1912 in Linguistic Survey of India, classifying Rajasthani dialects into 5 major branches: Western Rajasthani (Marwari), North-Eastern (Mewati/Ahirwati), Middle-Eastern (Dhundhari/Hadoti), South-Eastern (Malvi), and Southern Rajasthani (Nimadi).

# 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)

🎯 Linguistic Pioneers — Dr. L.P. Tessitori & Dingal — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & 1st Grade
The Italian philologist and Indologist Dr. Luigi Pio Tessitori (लुइगी पिओ तेस्सीतोरी), who conducted extensive research on Rajasthani Bardic literature in Bikaner and termed Prithviraj Rathore the 'Dingal ro Heros', has his memorial cenotaph (Chhatri) located in which city?
(A) Jodhpur
(B) Jaipur
(C) Udaipur
(D) Bikaner (बीकानेर - Demised 1919 AD)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Bikaner (बीकानेर - Demised 1919 AD)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Dr. L.P. Tessitori (from Udine, Italy) came to Rajasthan under the Asiatic Society of Bengal. Commissioned by Maharaja Ganga Singh of Bikaner, he catalogued ancient Dingal manuscripts and identified the pre-Harappan antiquities at Kalibangan. He passed away at the young age of 31 in **Bikaner (1919)**, where his commemorative marble cenotaph stands.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Jodhpur? Jodhpur was the center of Munshi Devi Prasad and Pandit Ramkaran Asopa.
  • Why not (B) Jaipur? Jaipur was the seat of Purohit Harinarayan Sharma.
  • Why not (C) Udaipur? Udaipur was the research hub of Col. James Tod and Kaviraja Shyamaldas.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: L.P. Tessitori memorial = Bikaner; Called Prithviraj Rathore 'Dingal ro Heros'; First identified Kalibangan pre-Harappan antiquity.
🎯 Rajasthani Literature — Kanhadade Prabandha (1455 AD) — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
The monumental 15th-century historical romance and chronicle 'Kanhadade Prabandha' (कान्हड़दे प्रबन्ध - 1455 AD), which gives an eyewitness-quality account of the 1311 AD siege of Jalore and the romance of Prince Viramdev with Alauddin Khilji's daughter Firoza, was authored by which court poet?
(A) Padmanabha (पद्मनाभ - Court poet of Akhairaj Songara of Jalore)
(B) Shivdas Gadan (शिवदास गाडण)
(C) Vithu Suja (वीठू सूजा)
(D) Durasa Adha (दुरासा आढ़ा)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Padmanabha (पद्मनाभ - Court poet of Akhairaj Songara of Jalore)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Padmanabha** authored *Kanhadade Prabandha* in 1455 AD under the patronage of Rawal Akhairaj Songara of Jalore. Written in Old Western Rajasthani, it vividly chronicles King Kanhadade's heroic defense, Prince Viramdev's sacrifice, and the 1311 Saka of Jalore Fort.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Shivdas Gadan? Shivdas Gadan authored *Achaldas Khinchi ri Vachanika* (1423 AD Gagron Saka).
  • Why not (C) Vithu Suja? Vithu Suja authored *Rao Jaitsi ro Chhand* (1534 AD).
  • Why not (D) Durasa Adha? Durasa Adha authored *Kirtar Bavani* and *Virud Chhihattari*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Kanhadade Prabandha = Padmanabha (1455 AD); Achaldas Khinchi ri Vachanika = Shivdas Gadan; Rao Jaitsi ro Chhand = Vithu Suja.
🎯 Rajasthani Literature — Patal aur Peethal (Kanhaiyalal Sethia) — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
In the celebrated patriotic poem 'Patal aur Peethal' (पाथल और पीथल) composed by Padma Shri Kanhaiyalal Sethia of Sujangarh, who do 'Patal' and 'Peethal' respectively represent?
(A) Patal represents Maharana Pratap and Peethal represents Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner (डिंगल का हेरोस)
(B) Patal represents Rana Sanga and Peethal represents Babur
(C) Patal represents Rao Jodha and Peethal represents Rao Maldeo
(D) Patal represents Prithviraj Chauhan and Peethal represents Chandbardai
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Patal represents Maharana Pratap and Peethal represents Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner (डिंगल का हेरोस)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: In Kanhaiyalal Sethia's immortal poem, **'Patal' (पाथल)** is **Maharana Pratap** and **'Peethal' (पीथल)** is courtier-poet **Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner**. When a rumored letter of submission from Pratap reaches Akbar's court, Peethal sends inspiring Dingal couplets to Pratap to verify if the lion of Mewar will truly bow down, prompting Pratap's defiant vow to fight to the end.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B)? Rana Sanga and Babur fought the Battle of Khanwa in 1527.
  • Why not (C)? Rao Jodha founded Jodhpur; Rao Maldeo fought Giri-Sumel.
  • Why not (D)? Prithviraj Chauhan and Chandbardai are the subjects of *Prithviraj Raso*.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Patal = Maharana Pratap; Peethal = Prithviraj Rathore; Author = Kanhaiyalal Sethia (who also wrote 'Dharti Dhoran Ri' and 'Leeltans').
🎯 Rajasthani Literature — Vansh Bhaskar & Suryamal Misran — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre
The monumental historical epic 'Vansh Bhaskar' (वंश भास्कर), chronicling the royal history of the Hada Chauhans of Bundi and written in Pingal style by state poet Suryamal Misran (सूर्यमल्ल मीसण), was completed after his demise by whom?
(A) Kaviraja Shyamaldas
(B) His foster son Muraridan (दत्तक पुत्र मुरारीदान)
(C) Chandbardai's son Jalhan
(D) Vijaydan Detha
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) His foster son Muraridan (दत्तक पुत्र मुरारीदान)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **'Vansh Bhaskar'** was commissioned by Maharao Raja Ram Singh of Bundi to state poet **Suryamal Misran** (1815–1868 AD). When Suryamal passed away leaving the vast epic incomplete, it was completed and published by his adopted/foster son **Muraridan (मुरारीदान)**. Suryamal also authored the fiery 1857 patriotic work **'Veer Satsai'** (*'इला न देणी आपणी, हालरियै हुलराय'*).

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Shyamaldas? Kaviraja Shyamaldas of Bhilwara authored *Vir Vinod* under Maharana Sajjan Singh.
  • Why not (C) Jalhan? Jalhan was the son of Chandbardai who completed *Prithviraj Raso*.
  • Why not (D) Vijaydan Detha? Vijaydan Detha ('Bijji') authored *Bataan ri Phulwari* in 14 volumes.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Vansh Bhaskar completed by = Muraridan; Prithviraj Raso completed by = Jalhan; Vir Vinod author = Shyamaldas.
🎯 Literature — Veli Krishan Rukmani ri & 5th Veda — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre
The monumental Dingal romantic-devotional masterpiece 'Veli Krishan Rukmani ri' (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री), composed by Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner at Gagron Fort, was hailed by contemporary poet Durasa Adha as which of the following?
(A) The 5th Veda and the 19th Purana (पांचवां वेद और 19वां पुराण)
(B) The Bible of Rajasthan
(C) The Mahabharata of the Desert
(D) The First Novel of Hindi Literature
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) The 5th Veda and the 19th Purana (पांचवां वेद और 19वां पुराण)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Charan poet **Durasa Adha (दुरसा आढ़ा)** praised *'Veli Krishan Rukmani ri'* as the **'5th Veda and 19th Purana'** for its peerless poetic brilliance. Italian philologist Dr. L.P. Tessitori called Prithviraj Rathore the **'Dingal ro Heros' (डिंगल का हेरोस)**, and Col. James Tod remarked that its verses possessed the vigor of 10,000 horses.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B)? *Bible of Rajasthan* is not a formal literary title.
  • Why not (C)? *Vansh Bhaskar* of Suryamal Misran is often compared to historical epics.
  • Why not (D)? The first novel in Rajasthani is *'Kanak Sundar'* (1903) by Shivchandra Bhartiya.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: 5th Veda & 19th Purana = Veli Krishan Rukmani ri (Durasa Adha's title); Dingal ro Heros = Prithviraj Rathore (Tessitori's title).
🎯 Rajasthani Literature — Mahakavi Suryamal Misran — Deep Concept Check RPSC RAS Pre & College Lecturer
Mahakavi Suryamal Misran of Bundi (1815–1868 AD) composed which fiery patriotic Dingal verse collection during the 1857 Revolt to awaken Rajput rulers against British colonial subjugation, opening with 'इला न देणी आपणी, हालरिया हुलराय...'?
(A) Vansh Bhaskar (वंश भास्कर - Bundi chronicle)
(B) Veer Satsai (वीर सतसई - 1857 patriotic verses)
(C) Chetavni ra Chungtiya (चेतावनि रा चूंघट्या - 13 Sorathas)
(D) Patal aur Peethal (पाथल और पीथल)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Veer Satsai (वीर सतसई - 1857 patriotic verses)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Suryamal Misran (the state poet of Maharao Ramsingh of Bundi) authored the legendary **Veer Satsai (वीर सतसई)** during the 1857 uprising. His opening couplet: 'इला न देणी आपणी, हालरिया हुलराय। पूत सिखावै पालणै, मरण बड़ाई माय॥' (Do not surrender your motherland even for a moment; mothers teach their infants supreme sacrifice in the cradle itself) ignited heroic anti-colonial resistance across Rajasthan.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Vansh Bhaskar? *Vansh Bhaskar* is an epic verse chronicle detailing the genealogy and history of the Hada Chauhans of Bundi, completed by Suryamal's adopted son Muraridan.
  • Why not (C) Chetavni ra Chungtiya? *Chetavni ra Chungtiya* comprises 13 Dingal couplets composed in 1903 by revolutionary poet **Kesari Singh Barhath** to prevent Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar from attending Lord Curzon's Delhi Durbar.
  • Why not (D) Patal aur Peethal? *Patal aur Peethal* is a modern classic Rajasthani poem composed by Padma Shri **Kanhaiyalal Sethia** describing the dialogue between Maharana Pratap (Patal) and Prithviraj Rathore (Peethal).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Veer Satsai (1857) = Suryamal Misran; Chetavni ra Chungtiya (1903) = Kesari Singh Barhath; Patal aur Peethal = Kanhaiyalal Sethia; Bataan ri Phulwari = Vijaydan Detha.
🎯 Rajasthani Dialects & Geography — Deep Concept Check RPSC EO/RO & 2nd Grade
Match the major regional dialects of Rajasthan with their respective geographic speaking regions:

Which of the following pairs is INCORRECTLY matched?
(A) Dhundhari Dialect (ढूंढाड़ी बोली) — Jaipur, Dausa, Tonk (Sant Dadu's literature)
(B) Hadoti Dialect (हाड़ौती बोली) — Kota, Bundi, Baran, Jhalawar (Suryamal Misran)
(C) Mewati Dialect (मेवाती बोली) — Alwar, Bharatpur (Sant Laldas & Charan Das)
(D) Vagadi Dialect (वागड़ी बोली) — Sikar, Jhunjhunu, Churu (Shekhawati tract)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Vagadi Dialect (वागड़ी बोली) — Sikar, Jhunjhunu, Churu (Shekhawati tract)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Vagadi (वागड़ी) is spoken in the southern **Vagad region (Dungarpur and Banswara)**; Sir George Grierson called it 'Bhili' due to strong Gujarati and tribal influence. The dialect spoken in Sikar, Jhunjhunu, and Churu is **Shekhawati (शेखावाटी)**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Dhundhari? Dhundhari is spoken across Jaipur, Dausa, and Tonk; Sant Dadu Dayal composed his spiritual discourses (*Vani*) in Dhundhari.
  • Why not (B) Hadoti? Hadoti is the standard dialect of the southeastern Hadoti plateau (Kota, Bundi, Baran, Jhalawar), used in Mahakavi Suryamal Misran's poetry.
  • Why not (C) Mewati? Mewati is spoken in the northeastern border region of Alwar and Bharatpur; Sant Lal Das, Sant Charan Das, Sahjo Bai, and Daya Bai authored their works in Mewati.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Vagadi = Dungarpur & Banswara (Sant Mavji's Chopada); Shekhawati = Sikar & Jhunjhunu; Ahirwati / Rathi = Kotputli, Behror & Alwar.

# 🎯 Chapter 9: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

Q1 RPSC RAS Pre 2016, 2021 / College Lecturer 2021
Who was the first linguist to undertake a systematic linguistic classification of Rajasthani dialects in his 1912 work *Linguistic Survey of India*?
(A) Dr. L.P. Tessitori
(B) Dr. Suniti Kumar Chatterji
(C) Sir George Abraham Grierson
(D) Col. James Tod
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Sir George Abraham Grierson
Explanation: Grierson classified Rajasthani into 5 sub-groups: Western Rajasthani (Marwari), North-Eastern (Mewati/Ahirwati), Middle-Eastern (Dhundhari/Harauti), Southern (Nimadi), and South-Western (Malvi).
Q2 RPSC 2nd Grade 2022 / SI 2021
'Dingal' (डिंगल) is primarily the literary language of which region of Rajasthan?
(A) Southern Rajasthan (Malvi base)
(B) Western Rajasthan (Marwari base)
(C) Northern Rajasthan (Punjabi base)
(D) Eastern Rajasthan (Braj base)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Western Rajasthan (Marwari base)
Explanation: Dingal is the heroic poetic style of Charan and Bhat bards in Western Rajasthan. Pingal is the Eastern style influenced by Braj Bhasha.
Q3 RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / 1st Grade 2020
Which classic Dingal work composed by Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner was hailed by Durasa Adha as the "Fifth Veda and Nineteenth Purana" (पांचवां वेद व 19वां पुराण)?
(A) *Dhola Maru Ra Duha*
(B) *Rao Jaitsee Ro Chhand*
(C) *Kanhadade Prabandh*
(D) *Veli Krisan Rukmani Ri* (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) *Veli Krisan Rukmani Ri* (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री)
Explanation: Written during Akbar's court in 1580 AD. It contains 305 verses celebrating the divine wedding of Krishna and Rukmini. Italian scholar L.P. Tessitori called Prithviraj Rathore the "Horace of Dingal".
Q4 RPSC SI 2021 / RAS Pre 2021
The monumental 14-volume Rajasthani folk lore compendium *Baataan Ri Phulwari* (बातां री फुलवारी) was authored by:
(A) Vijaydan Detha ("Bijji")
(B) Sitaram Lalas
(C) Suryamal Misran
(D) Kanhaiyalal Sethia
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Vijaydan Detha ("Bijji")
Explanation: Borunda-based Padma Shri author Vijaydan Detha documented Rajasthan's rich oral lore. His stories inspired Bollywood films like *Paheli* and *Duvidha*.
Q5 RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / EO/RO 2022
*Kanhadade Prabandh* (कान्हड़दे प्रबन्ध) — which documents the heroic resistance of Songara Chauhans against Alauddin Khilji in 1311 AD — was composed by:
(A) Jayanak
(B) Chand Bardai
(C) Padmanabha (पद्मनाभ)
(D) Hemchandra
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Padmanabha (पद्मनाभ)
Explanation: Written in 1455 AD in Old Western Rajasthani (Maru-Gurjar), providing vivid accounts of the Siege of Jalore and Siwana.
Q6 RPSC Patwari 2021 / 2nd Grade 2019
Who was the author of the monumental epic *Prithviraj Raso* (पृथ्वीराज रासो)?
(A) Jayanak
(B) Sarangdhar
(C) Narapati Nalha
(D) Chand Bardai (चंदबरदाई)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Chand Bardai (चंदबरदाई)
Explanation: Chand Bardai, court poet and close friend of Prithviraj Chauhan III, composed *Prithviraj Raso* in Pingal. (Note: *Prithviraj Vijaya* was authored in Sanskrit by Jayanak!).
Q7 RPSC Assistant Professor 2021
*Vansh Bhaskar* (वंश भास्कर) and *Veer Satsai* (वीर सतसई) were composed by the state poet of Bundi:
(A) Durasa Adha
(B) Bankidas
(C) Suryamal Misran (सूर्यमल्ल मीसण)
(D) Kaviraja Shyamaldas
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Suryamal Misran (सूर्यमल्ल मीसण)
Explanation: Suryamal Misran was the Mahakavi of Maharao Raja Ram Singh of Bundi. His *Veer Satsai* ignited patriotic fervor during the 1857 revolt: "इला न देणी आपणी, हालरिया हुलराय..."
Q8 RPSC RAS Pre 2016
Who compiled the encyclopedic 4-volume *Rajasthani Sabad Kos* (राजस्थानी सबद कोस - Dictionary of 2 lakh+ words)?
(A) Komal Kothari
(B) Dasharatha Sharma
(C) Kanhaiyalal Sethia
(D) Padma Shri Sitaram Lalas
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Padma Shri Sitaram Lalas
Explanation: Dr. Sitaram Lalas devoted 40 years to compile this monumental dictionary. Dr. Suniti Kumar Chatterji called him the "Encyclopedia Britannica of Rajasthani".

CHAPTER 10
Chapter 10: Prominent Tourist Destinations & Heritage Circuits (प्रमुख पर्यटन स्थल एवं परिपथ)

  • Tourism Milestones: Department established in 1956; declared an Industry in 1989 (Mohammad Yunus Committee / मोहम्मद युनुस समिति); divided into 10 Tourist Circuits (10 पर्यटन परिपथ).

# Key District Landmarks & Circuits

# 1. Ajmer & Pushkar (अजमेर एवं पुष्कर)

  • Adhai Din Ka Jhonpra (अढ़ाई दिन का झोंपड़ा): Built by Vigrahraj IV (विग्रहराज चतुर्थ) as a Sanskrit college (Harikeli drama inscribed); converted to a mosque in 1199 AD by Qutbuddin Aibak (कुतुबुद्दीन ऐबक).
  • Dargah of Khwaja Saheb (ख्वाजा साहब की दरगाह): Nizam Gate (Hyderabad), Shahjahani Gate, Buland Gate (Mahmud Khilji); Badi Deg (बड़ी देग) (4,800 kg - Akbar) & Chhoti Deg (छोटी देग) (2,240 kg - Jahangir).
  • Anasagar Lake (आनासागर झील): Built by Arnoraj (अर्णोराज); Baradaris (सफेद संगमरमर की बारादरी) by Shah Jahan; Daulat Bagh / Subhash Udhyan (दौलत बाग) by Jahangir (where Nur Jahan's mother Asmat Begum / असमत बेगम invented rose perfume / गुलाब का इत्र).
  • Brahma Temple (ब्रह्मा मन्दिर - Pushkar): Four-faced marble statue; Sun God idol wearing boots (घुटनों तक जूते पहने सूर्य प्रतिमा).

# 2. Alwar, Banswara & Bharatpur (अलवर, बांसवाड़ा एवं भरतपुर)

  • Alwar: Sariska Tiger Reserve (सरिस्का - 1978–79); Bhangarh Fort (भानगढ़ दुर्ग - haunted city by Madho Singh); Moosi Maharani 80-pillar chhatri (मूसी महारानी की छतरी); Siliserh Lake Palace (सिलीसेढ़ - Vinay Singh 1845).
  • Banswara (City of 100 Islands / सौ द्वीपों का शहर): Mahi Dam (माही बजाज सागर बांध - 16 gates, 3.1 km); Tripura Sundari / Tartai Mata (त्रिपुरा सुन्दरी / तरताई माता - 18-armed black stone goddess).
  • Mangarh Dham (मानगढ़ धाम - Banswara): 🔥 Jallianwala Bagh of Rajasthan (राजस्थान का जलियांवाला बाग)
    • On 17 November 1913 (Margashirsha Purnima / मार्गशीर्ष पूर्णिमा), British troops fired upon peaceful Bhil tribals assembled under Guru Govind Giri (गुरु गोविन्द गिरि), massacring over 1,500 Bhils.
  • Bharatpur: Keoladeo Ghana National Park (केवलादेव घना राष्ट्रीय उद्यान - UNESCO 1985; winter home of Siberian Cranes / साइबेरियन सारस); Bandh Baretha Dam (बांध बारैठा).

# 3. Jaipur (UNESCO World Heritage City 2019 / विश्व धरोहर नगर)

  • Hawa Mahal (हवा महल - 1799 AD): Built by Sawai Pratap Singh (सवाई प्रताप सिंह) (Architect: Lal Chand Ustad / लालचंद उस्ताद); 5 stories (Sharad, Ratan, Vichitra, Prakash, Hawa Mandir / शरद, रतन, विचित्र, प्रकाश, हवा मन्दिर); 953 jharokhas (खिड़कियां).
  • Jantar Mantar (जंतर मंतर): Astronomical observatory built by Sawai Jai Singh II (सवाई जयसिंह द्वितीय) (1734 AD); UNESCO Site (2010); world’s largest stone sundial (Samrat Yantra / सम्राट यंत्र).
  • Jaigarh Fort (जयगढ़ दुर्ग): Houses Jaivana Cannon (जयबाण तोप) (Asia's largest wheeled cannon).
  • Nahargarh Fort (नाहरगढ़ / सुदर्शनगढ़): Built by Sawai Jai Singh; Sawai Madho Singh II built 9 identical luxury palaces for his 9 queens (एक जैसे 9 महल).
  • Albert Hall Museum (अल्बर्ट हॉल म्यूजियम - 1876): Indo-Saracenic design by Sir Swinton Jacob.
  • Isarlat / Sargasuli (ईसरलाट / सरगासूली - 1749): 7-story tower by Maharaja Ishwari Singh after Battle of Rajmahal.

# 4. Other Prominent Heritage Landmarks

  • Jhalawar (झालावाड़): Bhawani Natyashala (भवानी नाट्यशाला - 1921 AD) — Rare authentic Parsi-style opera theater with underground hydraulic stage; Sun Temple of Jhalrapatan (City of Bells / घंटियों का शहर).
  • Jaisalmer (जैसलमेर): Patwon ki Haveli (पटवों की हवेली), Salim Singh ki Haveli (सलीम सिंह की हवेली), Nathmal ki Haveli (नथमल की हवेली); Tanot Mata Temple (तनोट माता मन्दिर) (Goddess of Soldiers / थार की वैष्णो देवी where 1965 bombs failed to explode).
  • Sri Ganganagar (श्रीगंगानगर): Buddha Johad Gurudwara (गुरुद्वारा बुड्ढा जोहड़) — Where Sikh warriors Sukha Singh & Mehtab Singh (सूखा सिंह व मेहताब सिंह) brought the severed head of Massa Ranghar in 1740 to avenge Harmandir Sahib's desecration.
