# 📝 Chapter 10: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

Practice Q1 🎯 Tourism Circuits & RTDC Classification
The Shekhawati Tourism Circuit (शेखावाटी पर्यटन परिपथ) developed by Rajasthan Tourism Department (RTDC) comprises which group of districts renowned for their frescoed open-air art gallery havelis?
(A) Sikar, Jhunjhunu and Churu
(B) Jaipur, Dausa and Alwar
(C) Kota, Bundi and Jhalawar
(D) Bikaner, Nagaur and Jodhpur
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Sikar, Jhunjhunu and Churu
Explanation: The Shekhawati Circuit comprises Sikar, Jhunjhunu, and Churu. Towns like Nawalgarh ("Golden City of Havelis"), Mandawa, Fatehpur, and Ramgarh Shekhawati are famous worldwide as an 'Open Art Gallery'.
Practice Q2 🎯 Monumental Architecture & UNESCO Sites
The world-famous Hawa Mahal of Jaipur was constructed in 1799 CE by Sawai Pratap Singh. How many storeys does it have, and who was its chief architect?
(A) 7 Storeys; Vidyadhar Bhattacharya
(B) 5 Storeys; Lal Chand Ustad
(C) 5 Storeys; Mandan
(D) 9 Storeys; Jaita and Napa
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) 5 Storeys; Lal Chand Ustad
Explanation: Hawa Mahal is a 5-storey pyramidical crown structure designed by architect Lal Chand Ustad with 953 jharokhas (casements). Its 5 storeys from base to top are: 1. Sharad Mandir, 2. Ratan Mandir, 3. Vichitra Mandir, 4. Prakash Mandir, and 5. Hawa Mandir.
Practice Q3 🎯 UNESCO World Heritage in Rajasthan
In July 2019 at Baku (Azerbaijan), which monument / urban ensemble of Rajasthan was inscribed on the UNESCO World Heritage Site list under the Cultural category for its exemplary grid-iron town planning based on Vastu Shastra?
(A) Jantar Mantar Observatory Jaipur
(B) Junagarh Fort Bikaner
(C) City Palace Udaipur
(D) Walled City of Jaipur (जयपुर परकोटा शहर)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Walled City of Jaipur (जयपुर परकोटा शहर)
Explanation: The historic Walled City of Jaipur (founded 18 Nov 1727 by Sawai Jai Singh II and architect Vidyadhar Bhattacharya) was inscribed in the UNESCO World Heritage list in 2019. Jantar Mantar was inscribed earlier in 2010, and Keoladeo Ghana in 1985 (Natural category).
Practice Q4 🎯 Luxury Trains & Tourism Taglines
When was India's first ultra-luxury tourist train "Palace on Wheels" (पहियों पर महल) jointly launched by Indian Railways and Rajasthan Tourism Development Corporation (RTDC)?
(A) 1982
(B) 2004
(C) 1978
(D) 1989
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) 1982
Explanation: Palace on Wheels commenced operation on 26 January 1982 to promote royal heritage tourism using authentic saloon coaches of former Rajput maharajas. RTDC was established in 1978 and commenced formal commercial operations on 1 April 1979.

# 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)

🎯 Tourism Policy — Industry Status & Yunus Committee 1989 — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
On 4 March 1989, Rajasthan became the very first state in India to grant the formal status of 'Industry' (उद्योग का दर्जा) to the Tourism sector based on the recommendations of which expert committee?
(A) Kothari Committee
(B) Mohammad Yunus Committee (मोहम्मद यूनुस समिति)
(C) G.V.K. Rao Committee
(D) Shivaraman Committee
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Mohammad Yunus Committee (मोहम्मद यूनुस समिति)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Based on the visionary recommendations of the **Mohammad Yunus Committee (1989)**, the Government of Rajasthan accorded **Industry status to Tourism on 4 March 1989**, entitling heritage hotels and tourism enterprises to industrial power tariffs, tax concessions, and subsidized loans.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Kothari Committee? Kothari Commission was for National Education Reform (1964–66).
  • Why not (C) G.V.K. Rao? G.V.K. Rao Committee was for Panchayati Raj (1985).
  • Why not (D) Shivaraman? Shivaraman Committee recommended the establishment of NABARD (1981).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Tourism as Industry (4 March 1989) = Mohammad Yunus Committee; RTDC started = 1 April 1979; Palace on Wheels = 26 Jan 1982.
🎯 UNESCO World Heritage — Chronology in Rajasthan — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
Match the prestigious UNESCO World Heritage Inscriptions of Rajasthan with their exact years of designation:

Which of the following pairings is INCORRECT?
(A) Keoladeo Ghana National Park (Bharatpur) — Inscribed in 1985 (World Natural Heritage)
(B) Jantar Mantar Astronomical Observatory (Jaipur) — Inscribed in 2010
(C) Jaipur Walled City (परकोटा शहर) — Inscribed in 1999
(D) 6 Hill Forts of Rajasthan (Chittor, Kumbhalgarh, Gagron, Jaisalmer, Ranthambore, Amer) — Inscribed in 2013
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Jaipur Walled City (परकोटा शहर) — Inscribed in 1999
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Jaipur Walled City (परकोटा क्षेत्र)** was officially inscribed as a UNESCO World Heritage City on **6 July 2019** at the 43rd World Heritage Committee session in Baku (Azerbaijan), NOT 1999.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Keoladeo? Keoladeo Ghana National Park was inscribed in **1985** as a UNESCO World Natural Heritage site.
  • Why not (B) Jantar Mantar? Sawai Jai Singh's stone observatory Jantar Mantar was inscribed in **2010** (along with Kalbelia dance as Intangible Heritage).
  • Why not (D) 6 Hill Forts? The 6 Hill Forts were inscribed in **2013** at Phnom Penh, Cambodia.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: UNESCO Years: Keoladeo = 1985; Jantar Mantar & Kalbelia = 2010; 6 Hill Forts = 2013; Jaipur Walled City = 2019.
🎯 Tourism Policy — Mohammad Yunus Committee 1989 — Deep Fact Check RPSC EO/RO & Assistant Professor
On the recommendations of which committee in 1989 did Rajasthan create history by becoming the first state in India to accord official 'Industry Status' to Tourism (पर्यटन को उद्योग का दर्जा)?
(A) B.R. Mehta Committee
(B) Mohammad Yunus Committee (मोहम्मद यूनुस समिति - 1989)
(C) Kothari Commission
(D) Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Mohammad Yunus Committee (मोहम्मद यूनुस समिति - 1989)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: On **4 March 1989**, following the recommendations of the **Mohammad Yunus Committee**, Rajasthan became the pioneering state in the country to declare tourism an **Industry (उद्योग)**, opening access to industrial finance, land concessions, and power subsidies for hotels and heritage resorts.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) B.R. Mehta Committee? The Balwant Rai Mehta Committee (1957) recommended the 3-tier Panchayati Raj system inaugurated in Nagaur on 2 Oct 1959.
  • Why not (C) Kothari Commission? The Kothari Commission (1964) was appointed for national educational reforms.
  • Why not (D) Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission? The Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission investigated the murder of freedom fighter **Sagarmal Gopa** inside Jaisalmer jail in 1946.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Tourism as Industry (1989) = Mohammad Yunus Committee; RTDC Inception = 1978/1979; Sagarmal Gopa Inquiry = Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission.
🎯 UNESCO World Heritage — 6 Hill Forts of Rajasthan — Deep Concept Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
In June 2013 at the 37th World Heritage Committee session in Phnom Penh (Cambodia), six magnificent hill forts of Rajasthan were inscribed collectively into UNESCO's World Heritage List.

Which of the following forts is NOT among those six UNESCO World Heritage Forts?
(A) Gagron Fort (गागरोन दुर्ग, झालावाड़)
(B) Mehrangarh Fort (मेहरानगढ़ दुर्ग, जोधपुर)
(C) Kumbhalgarh Fort (कुम्भलगढ़ दुर्ग, राजसमंद)
(D) Sonargarh / Jaisalmer Fort (सोनारगढ़ दुर्ग, जैसलमेर)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Mehrangarh Fort (मेहरानगढ़ दुर्ग, जोधपुर)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Mehrangarh Fort (Jodhpur) and Junagarh Fort (Bikaner) are magnificent forts but are **NOT included in the UNESCO 6 Hill Forts of Rajasthan**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Gagron? Gagron Fort (Jhalawar) is included as the premier Water/Hill fort (*Jal Durg*).
  • Why not (C) Kumbhalgarh? Kumbhalgarh Fort (Rajsamand) is included for its massive 36-km wall and *Katargarh*.
  • Why not (D) Jaisalmer? Jaisalmer Fort (Sonargarh) is included as the living golden sandstone desert fort on Trikuta hill.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Master Mnemonic for 6 UNESCO Forts (2013): 'चीकू गाजर आम' (Chiku Gajar Aam) = चि (Chittor), कु (Kumbhalgarh), गा (Gagron), ज (Jaisalmer), र (Ranthambore), आम (Amer). Mehrangarh & Junagarh are NOT on this list!
🎯 Heritage Luxury Trains — Palace on Wheels — Deep Concept Check RPSC EO/RO & Assistant Professor
The iconic royal luxury heritage train 'Palace on Wheels' (पैलेस ऑन व्हील्स) was launched as a joint venture between the Indian Railways and RTDC on which historic date?
(A) 15 August 1947
(B) 26 January 1982
(C) 1 April 1979
(D) 1 November 1956
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) 26 January 1982
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: The **Palace on Wheels** was officially launched on Republic Day, **26 January 1982**, using the restored vintage royal saloons of former Rajputana maharajas.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) 15 August 1947? 15 August 1947 is Indian Independence Day.
  • Why not (C) 1 April 1979? 1 April 1979 marks the commencement of official commercial operations of the **RTDC (Rajasthan Tourism Development Corporation)**.
  • Why not (D) 1 November 1956? 1 November 1956 is **Rajasthan Foundation / Reorganization Day** marking the completion of the 7th and final stage of Rajasthan's integration with the merger of Ajmer-Merwara, Abu-Delwara, and Sunel Tappa.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Palace on Wheels = 26 Jan 1982; RTDC operations = 1 April 1979; Tourism as Industry = 1989 (Yunus Committee); Integration complete = 1 Nov 1956.

# 🎯 Chapter 10: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

Q1 RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / SI 2021
Which tourist destinations comprise the famed 'Desert Triangle' (मरु त्रिकोण) of Rajasthan?
(A) Bikaner - Barmer - Nagaur
(B) Jodhpur - Jaisalmer - Bikaner
(C) Jaipur - Jodhpur - Udaipur
(D) Jaisalmer - Barmer - Jalore
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Jodhpur - Jaisalmer - Bikaner
Explanation: The Desert Triangle is the primary arid tourism circuit showcasing sand dunes, camel safaris, Havelis, and desert forts. (Note: Barmer has recently been linked to form the Desert Circuit quadrangle).
Q2 RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / EO/RO 2022
In which year was the 'Rajasthan Tourism Development Corporation' (RTDC) incorporated to promote state tourism infrastructure?
(A) 1985
(B) 1989
(C) 1956
(D) 1978 (Operations started 1 April 1979)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) 1978 (Operations started 1 April 1979)
Explanation: RTDC was established in November 1978 and commenced operational activity on 1 April 1979. It operates state hotels, motels, and luxury heritage tourist trains.
Q3 RPSC RAS Pre 2021 / SI 2021
On the recommendation of which committee in 1989 did Rajasthan become the **first state in India** to grant 'Industry status' (उद्योग का दर्जा) to Tourism?
(A) K.V. Kamath Committee
(B) L.K. Jha Committee
(C) Shiv Charan Mathur Committee
(D) Mohammad Yunus Committee (मोहम्मद यूनुस समिति)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Mohammad Yunus Committee (मोहम्मद यूनुस समिति)
Explanation: On 4 March 1989, Rajasthan declared tourism an industry following the Mohammad Yunus Committee recommendations, opening access to industrial subsidies and institutional finance.
Q4 RPSC 2nd Grade 2022
The prestigious 'Golden Triangle' (स्वर्णिम त्रिकोण) international tourist circuit connects which three historic cities?
(A) Delhi - Agra - Jaipur
(B) Delhi - Jaipur - Ahmedabad
(C) Jaipur - Jodhpur - Udaipur
(D) Agra - Gwalior - Jaipur
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Delhi - Agra - Jaipur
Explanation: One of the world's most heavily traversed tourist corridors, covering the national capital (Delhi), Mughal monuments (Agra), and Rajput royal heritage (Jaipur).
Q5 RPSC 1st Grade 2020 / Patwari 2021
In which year was the world-famous luxury tourist train 'Palace on Wheels' (पैलेस ऑन व्हील्स) launched jointly by RTDC and Indian Railways?
(A) 2001
(B) 1995
(C) 1982
(D) 1989
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation

Explanation: Launched on 26 January 1982 using renovated vintage saloons of former Rajputana maharajas. It remains one of the top luxury trains globally.
Q6 RPSC 2nd Grade 2022
'Abhaneri Festival' and 'Harshat Mata Temple' fall under which official tourism circuit of Rajasthan?
(A) Marwar Circuit
(B) Hadoti Circuit
(C) Matsya / Braj-Bhoomi Circuit (Dausa/Alwar)
(D) Shekhawati Circuit
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Matsya / Braj-Bhoomi Circuit (Dausa/Alwar)
Explanation: Dausa, Alwar, and Bharatpur form the Matsya and Braj-Bhoomi circuits showcasing Gurjara-Pratihara temples, stepwells, and bird sanctuaries.
Q7 RPSC RAS Pre 2016
'Keoladeo Ghana National Park' (Bharatpur) — a UNESCO World Natural Heritage Site (1985) famous for migratory Siberian Cranes — was historically developed as a duck-shooting preserve by which Maharaja?
(A) Maharaja Suraj Mal
(B) Maharaja Kishan Singh
(C) Maharaja Ram Singh
(D) Maharaja Jawahar Singh
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Maharaja Kishan Singh
Explanation: Developed by constructing the Ajan Dam (अजान बांध) to divert water from Banganga and Gambhiri rivers. Declared Ramsar Wetland in 1981 and UNESCO World Natural Heritage in 1985.

CHAPTER 11
Chapter 11: Prominent Personalities of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के प्रमुख व्यक्तित्व)

🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Real Wealth of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के अनमोल रत्न)
"When you look back at Rajasthan's history, its true wealth was never gold or diamonds—it was the character of its people. Foster mothers who sacrificed their own flesh and blood to protect royal heirs, warriors who fought 30-year desert guerrilla campaigns without asking for a crown, saints who walked thousands of miles for social equality, and soldiers who won India's highest wartime gallantry awards."

# 1. Historical Legends & Unsung Heroes (ऐतिहासिक अमर व्यक्तित्व)

  • Panna Dai (पन्ना धाय): Gurjar foster mother of Mewar; sacrificed her own infant son Chandan (चन्दन) in 1535 AD to shield toddler Prince Uday Singh (उदयसिंह) from the murderous sword of usurper Banbir (बनवीर).
  • Gaura Dai (गोरा धाय - 1646–1704 AD): Revered as the "Panna Dai of Marwar" (मारवाड़ की पन्ना धाय). Smuggled infant heir Prince Ajit Singh (अजीत सिंह) out of Aurangzeb's heavily guarded Delhi palace in 1679. Immortalized in Jodhpur's national song Dhusan (धूंसा गीत); 6-pillared chhatri in Jodhpur.
  • Veer Durgadas Rathore (वीर दुर्गादास राठौड़ - 1638–1718 AD): 🔥 Core Legend
    • Son of Askaran (आसकरण); born in Salwa (सालवा). Known as the "Ulysses of Marwar" (मारवाड़ का यूलीसेस / राठौड़ों का यूलीसेस).
    • Fought an unyielding 30-year guerrilla resistance against Aurangzeb to crown Ajit Singh. Educated rebel Prince Akbar’s children (Buland Akhtar & Safiyatunissa / बुलन्द अख्तर व सफियतुलनिसा) with strict Islamic traditions. Died in exile on the banks of Shipra River in Ujjain (उज्जैन) (cenotaph stands there).
  • Gavari Bai (गवरी बाई): Revered as the "Meera of Wagad" (वागड़ की मीरा); Krishna devotee of Dungarpur; Maharawal Shiv Singh built Balmukund Temple (बालमुकुन्द मन्दिर) for her in 1829.
  • Kaviraj Shyamaldas (कविराजा श्यामलदास - 1836–1893 AD): Court historian of Mewar (Sajjan Singh); authored Veer Vinod (वीर विनोद); honored with titles Kaviraja (कविराजा) and Kaiser-e-Hind (कैसर-ए-हिन्द).
  • Gaurishankar Hirachand Ojha (डॉ. गौरीशंकर हीराचंद ओझा - 1863–1947 AD): Born in Rohida (रोहिड़ा - Sirohi); pioneer epigraphist; authored Bharatiya Prachin Lipimala (भारतीय प्राचीन लिपिमाला); titled Mahamahopadhyaya (महामहोपाध्याय).
  • Bavji Chatursingh (बावजी चतुरसिंह जी - 1879–1929 AD): Royal saint-philosopher of Mewar; translated Bhagavad Gita into Mewari (Gangajali commentary / गंगाजली टीका).
  • Birbal Singh (बीरबल सिंह): Bikaner Praja Parishad martyr; died holding the Tricolor on 1 July 1946 at Raisinghnagar (रायसिंहनगर) shouting "Jhanda Ooncha Rahe Hamara" (झंडा ऊंचा रहे हमारा).

# 2. Param Vir Chakra Heroes, Social Reformers & Modern Icons (परमवीर चक्र विजेता एवं आधुनिक विभूतियां)

  • Param Vir Chakra Heroes (परमवीर चक्र विजेता):
    • CHM Piru Singh (कंपनी हवलदार मेजर पीरू सिंह शेखावत): Born in Rampura Beri (रामपुरा बेरी - Jhunjhunu); 6th Rajput Rifles; destroyed multiple enemy bunkers at Tithwal on 18 July 1948. First Param Vir Chakra recipient from Rajasthan (Posthumous / मरणोपरान्त).
    • Major Shaitan Singh (मेजर शैतान सिंह भाटी): Born in Banasar (बाणासर - Phalodi, Jodhpur); "Banasur Ka Shaheed" (बाणासुर का शहीद); 13th Kumaon (Charlie Company); defended Rezang La / Chushul (रेजांग ला, लद्दाख) in 1962 Sino-Indian War; awarded Param Vir Chakra (Posthumous).
  • Swami Keshwanand (स्वामी केशवानन्द - 1883–1972): Born in Magloona (मंगलूणा - Sikar); "Pioneer of Desert Education"; established Gramotthan Vidyapeeth Sangaria (ग्रामोत्थान विद्यापीठ, संगरिया) and 300+ rural schools and mobile libraries.
  • Pt. Jhabarmal Sharma (पं. झाबरमल्ल शर्मा - 1880–1983): Jasrapur (जसरापुर - Khetri); revered as the "Patriarch of Journalism" (पत्रकारिता के भीष्म पितामह); wrote histories of Sikar, Khetri, and Swami Vivekananda.
  • Acharya Tulsi (आचार्य तुलसी - 1914–1997): Born in Ladnun (लाडनूं - Nagaur); 9th Terapanth Acharya; launched the Anuvrata Movement in 1949 (अणुव्रत आन्दोलन) ("इंसान पहले इंसान, फिर हिन्दू या मुसलमान").
  • Komal Kothari (कोमल कोठारी - 1929–2004): Born in Kapasan (कपासन - Chittorgarh); co-founded Rupayan Sansthan (रूपायन संस्थान - Borunda) in 1960 to discover and promote Langa/Manganiyar folk musicians globally; Padma Bhushan.
  • Kripal Singh Shekhawat (कृपाल सिंह शेखावत - 1922–2008): Born in Mau (मऊ - Sikar); "Shilp Guru"; revived Jaipur Blue Pottery (ब्लू पॉटरी) with 25 color shades; Padma Shri (1974).
  • Pt. Vishwa Mohan Bhatt (पं. विश्वमोहन भट्ट): Born in Jaipur; invented the Mohan Veena (मोहन वीणा); won the Grammy Award (1994) for A Meeting by the River.
  • Karpurchand Kulish (कर्पूरचन्द कुलिश - 1926–2006): Born in Soda (सोड़ा - Tonk); launched Rajasthan Patrika (राजस्थान पत्रिका - 1956); authored Mein Dekhta Chala Gaya and Dhundhari column Polampol (पोलम्पोल).
  • Dr. P. K. Sethi (डॉ. पी. के. सेठी) & Mastercraftsman Ramchandra (शिल्पी रामचन्द्र): SMS Hospital Jaipur; co-invented the Jaipur Foot (जयपुर फुट - 1969) artificial limb; awarded Ramon Magsaysay Award (रेमन मैग्सेसे पुरस्कार) and Padma Shri.

# 📝 Chapter 11: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

Practice Q1 🎯 Revolutionary Movement & Historical Quotes
Which revolutionary leader of Rajasthan established the Vardhman Vidyalaya in Jaipur (1907) for training freedom fighters and boldly declared to Maharaja Madho Singh II: "If Arjun Lal accepts state employment, then who will drive the British out of Mother India?"
(A) Seth Damodar Das Rathi (दामोदर दास राठी)
(B) Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ)
(C) Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा)
(D) Arjun Lal Sethi (अर्जुन लाल सेठी)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Arjun Lal Sethi (अर्जुन लाल सेठी)
Explanation: Arjun Lal Sethi was the pioneer of political awakening in Jaipur. He rejected the Diwan post of Jaipur state with this famous statement. His Vardhman Vidyalaya trained revolutionaries like Pratap Singh Barhath and Zorawar Singh Barhath. He lived his last days in Ajmer under the name 'Karim Khan' teaching at Dargah.
Practice Q2 🎯 Women Freedom Fighters of Rajasthan
Who was the first woman in Rajasthan to be awarded the prestigious Padma Bhushan in 1956 for her lifelong dedication to social reform, women upliftment, and the freedom movement alongside Jamnalal Bajaj?
(A) Narayani Devi Verma (नारायणी देवी वर्मा)
(B) Ratna Shastri (रत्ना शास्त्री)
(C) Anjana Devi Chaudhary (अंजना देवी चौधरी)
(D) Janaki Devi Bajaj (जानकी देवी बजाज)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Janaki Devi Bajaj (जानकी देवी बजाज)
Explanation: Smt. Janaki Devi Bajaj (wife of Jamnalal Bajaj) was conferred the Padma Bhushan in 1956 (first woman in Rajasthan to receive this honor). She surrendered her family's private Laxminarayan temple to Harijans in 1928, spearheaded Bhoodan movement with Vinoba Bhave, and authored her autobiography Meri Jeevan Yatra.
Practice Q3 🎯 Barhath Family Revolutionary Saga
Which patriotic bard composed the 13 Sorathas in Dingal titled "Chetavni ra Chungtiya" (चेतावनी रा चूंघट्या) in 1903, which persuaded Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar not to attend Lord Curzon's imperial Delhi Durbar?
(A) Thakur Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ)
(B) Kunwar Pratap Singh Barhath (कुंवर प्रताप सिंह बारहठ)
(C) Thakur Zorawar Singh Barhath (जोरावर सिंह बारहठ)
(D) Kaviraja Shyamaldas (श्यामलदास)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Thakur Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ)
Explanation: Thakur Kesari Singh Barhath (Shahpura, Bhilwara) sent 13 Dingal couplets (Sorathas) to Saradhana station, reminding Maharana Fateh Singh of his illustrious Guhil ancestors' pride (Bappa Rawal, Rana Sanga, Pratap). The Maharana went to Delhi but refused to attend the Durbar. Kesari Singh also established the Veer Bharat Sabha in 1910.
Practice Q4 🎯 Martyrdom & Heroic Resistance
Which courageous young Bhil girl of Rastapal (Dungarpur) attained martyrdom on 19 June 1947 when she sliced the rope tying her teacher Senga Bhai to a police truck using her sickle (दांतली)?
(A) Nanibai
(B) Rupa Bai
(C) Bhimabai
(D) Veerangana Kali Bai (वीरांगना कालीबाई)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Veerangana Kali Bai (वीरांगना कालीबाई)
Explanation: 13-year-old Kali Bai gave her life to save her teacher Senga Bhai and Nana Bhai Khant during the Rastapal incident (1947). The Rajasthan Government instituted the Kali Bai Scooty Scheme and Kali Bai Meritorious Student Award in her sacred memory.

# 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)

🎯 Freedom Fighters — Arjun Lal Sethi & Vardhman Vidyalaya — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre
Which pioneer revolutionary of Rajasthan ('Father of Revolutionary Movement in Rajasthan') rejected a high-salaried Diwan appointment by declaring: 'यदि अर्जुनलाल नौकरी करेगा तो अंग्रेजों को भारत से बाहर कौन निकालेगा?', founded the Vardhman Vidyalaya (1907, Jaipur) to train freedom fighters, and passed his final years teaching under the identity of 'Karim Khan' at the Ajmer Dargah?
(A) Kesari Singh Barhath
(B) Seth Damodar Das Rathi
(C) Arjun Lal Sethi (अर्जुनलाल सेठी - 1880–1941 AD)
(D) Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Arjun Lal Sethi (अर्जुनलाल सेठी - 1880–1941 AD)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Arjun Lal Sethi** of Jaipur founded the secret revolutionary training center **Vardhman Vidyalaya (1907)**. Accused in the Nimej Mahant Murder Case, he was imprisoned for 6 years in Vellore Jail (Madras). He authored revolutionary plays *Madra Parajaya, Parshva Yajna, and Mahendra Kumar*. In his final years, he taught Persian and lived under the secular name **'Karim Khan'** in Ajmer Dargah, where he was buried as per his last wish upon his demise in 1941.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Kesari Singh Barhath? Kesari Singh Barhath founded the *Veer Bharat Sabha (1910)* in Kota.
  • Why not (B) Damodar Das Rathi? Seth Damodar Das Rathi financed the armed revolution from Beawar.
  • Why not (D) Gopal Singh Kharwa? Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa planned the 1915 armed uprising in Todgarh.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Vardhman Vidyalaya + 'If Arjunlal takes a job...' + Lived as Karim Khan = Arjun Lal Sethi; Veer Bharat Sabha = Kesari Singh Barhath.
🎯 Freedom Fighters — Balmukund Bissa 'Jatin Das of Rajasthan' — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade
Which heroic freedom fighter and merchant of Didwana/Jodhpur, who established the 'Jawahar Khadi Bhandar' in Jodhpur (1934) and organized protests against the Marwar princely state, was martyred following a prolonged hunger strike against brutal jail conditions on 19 June 1942, earning the title 'Jatin Das of Rajasthan'?
(A) Sagarmal Gopa (जैसलमेर)
(B) Ganesh Shankar Vidyarthi
(C) Rameshwar Dayal Sharma
(D) Balmukund Bissa (बालमुकुन्द बिस्सा - 1908–1942 AD)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Balmukund Bissa (बालमुकुन्द बिस्सा - 1908–1942 AD)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Balmukund Bissa** joined the Marwar Prajamandal and launched civil disobedience against the Maharaja of Jodhpur. Arrested in 1942, he went on a relentless **hunger strike in Jodhpur Central Jail** to protest unhygienic food and police brutality, attaining martyrdom on **19 June 1942**. For this supreme sacrifice through fasting, he is revered as the **'Jatin Das of Rajasthan'**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Sagarmal Gopa? Sagarmal Gopa was **burnt alive** with kerosene in Jaisalmer jail on 3 April 1946.
  • Why not (B)? Ganesh Shankar Vidyarthi was the editor of *Pratap* (Kanpur) who publicized the Bijolia movement.
  • Why not (C)? Rameshwar Dayal Sharma was an activist in Alwar.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Hunger strike martyr in Jodhpur Jail (1942) = Balmukund Bissa ('Jatin Das of Rajasthan'); Burnt alive in Jaisalmer (1946) = Sagarmal Gopa.
🎯 Tribal Martyrs — Veerangana Kali Bai of Rastapal 1947 — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
On 19 June 1947, at the historic Rastapal Incident (डूंगरपुर), 13-year-old Bhil schoolgirl Veerangana Kali Bai attained immortality by doing which heroic act to save her teacher Sengabhai from princely state police?
(A) She ran forward with her sickle (दातली / हंसिया) and cut the rope with which the police were dragging her teacher behind their vehicle
(B) She hurled a hand grenade at the police truck
(C) She hid the teacher inside a secret mountain cave
(D) She went on a 21-day hunger strike outside the royal palace
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) She ran forward with her sickle (दातली / हंसिया) and cut the rope with which the police were dragging her teacher behind their vehicle
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: When the Dungarpur state police raided the Prajamandal school at Rastapal and tied teacher **Sengabhai** behind a truck with ropes, **13-year-old Bhil girl Kali Bai** fearlessly dashed into the convoy wielding her grass-cutting sickle (*Daatli*) and severed the rope. The police opened fire, and Kali Bai was martyred on **21 June 1947**. A memorial statue stands in her honor at **Gaib Sagar Lake in Dungarpur**.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B)? Grenades were used in the Delhi Conspiracy case by Zorawar Singh Barhath.
  • Why not (C)? Underground hiding was practiced by Prajamandal activists.
  • Why not (D)? Fasting was used by Balmukund Bissa.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Rastapal Incident (19 June 1947) = Kali Bai + Cut rope with sickle to save teacher Sengabhai; Memorial = Gaib Sagar Lake (Dungarpur).
🎯 Freedom Fighters — Barhath Family of Shahpura — Deep Fact Check RPSC College Lecturer & EO/RO
Which member of the immortal Barhath family of Shahpura, who threw the bomb at Viceroy Lord Hardinge during the Delhi Conspiracy Case in 1912 at Chandni Chowk, lived underground for 27 years under the ascetic identity of 'Amardas Bairagi' without ever being caught by British police?
(A) Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ)
(B) Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa
(C) Kunwar Pratap Singh Barhath (प्रताप सिंह बारहठ)
(D) Thakur Zorawar Singh Barhath (ठाकुर जोरावर सिंह बारहठ - अमरदास बैरागी)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Thakur Zorawar Singh Barhath (ठाकुर जोरावर सिंह बारहठ - अमरदास बैरागी)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Thakur Zorawar Singh Barhath** (brother of Kesari Singh and uncle of Pratap Singh) disguised himself as a woman in a Haveli balcony and hurled the bomb at Viceroy Hardinge's elephant in Delhi (1912). He successfully evaded British intelligence for 27 straight years by adopting the guise of ascetic sadhu **'Amardas Bairagi' (अमरदास बैरागी)** in the ravines of Kota, MP, and Malwa until his peaceful demise in 1939.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Kesari Singh? Kesari Singh Barhath was imprisoned for 20 years in Hazaribagh Jail for the murder of Mahant Pyare Lal.
  • Why not (B) Gopal Singh Kharwa? Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa was interned at Todgarh Fort for planning the 1915 armed uprising.
  • Why not (C) Pratap Singh? Pratap Singh Barhath was arrested in the Benaras Conspiracy Case and tortured to death in Bareilly Jail (1918).

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Amardas Bairagi = Zorawar Singh Barhath (Bomb at Hardinge 1912); Bareilly Jail martyr = Pratap Singh Barhath; Chetavni ra Chungtiya = Kesari Singh Barhath.
🎯 Freedom Fighters — Sagarmal Gopa of Jaisalmer — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade
Which brave revolutionary of Jaisalmer authored the explosive exposés 'Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj' (जैसलमेर में गुण्डाराज), 'Raghunath Singh ka Mukadma', and 'Azadi ke Deewane', and was brutally burnt alive inside Jaisalmer jail on 3 April 1946?
(A) Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा)
(B) Balmukund Bissa (बालमुकुन्द बिस्सा)
(C) Rameshwar Dayal Sharma
(D) Bhogilal Pandya
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: **Sagarmal Gopa** waged a fearless crusade against the autocratic misrule of Maharawal Jawahar Singh of Jaisalmer. Arrested in May 1941, he was subjected to horrific police torture by jailor Guman Singh and **burnt alive with kerosene inside Jaisalmer jail on 3 April 1946**. The outrage prompted the appointment of the *Gopal Swaroop Pathak Inquiry Commission*.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Balmukund Bissa? Balmukund Bissa ('Jatin Das of Rajasthan') died after a hunger strike in Jodhpur Central Jail on 19 June 1942.
  • Why not (C)? Rameshwar Dayal Sharma was an activist in Alwar.
  • Why not (D) Bhogilal Pandya? Bhogilal Pandya ('Gandhi of Vagad') founded Dungarpur Prajamandal in 1944.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj & burnt alive 1946 = Sagarmal Gopa; Hunger strike martyr Jodhpur 1942 = Balmukund Bissa; Gandhi of Vagad = Bhogilal Pandya.
🎯 Women Freedom Movement — Katrathal Conference 1934 — Deep Fact Check RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO
Under whose dynamic leadership was the historic Katrathal Women's Conference (कटराथल महिला सम्मेलन - 25 April 1934, Sikar) organized, where over 10,000 peasant women gathered in defiance of feudal bans against the atrocities of the Thakur of Sihanot?
(A) Anjana Devi Chaudhary (अंजना देवी चौधरी)
(B) Kishori Devi (किशोरी देवी - Wife of Sardar Hararlal Singh)
(C) Janakidevi Bajaj (जानकीदेवी बजाज)
(D) Ratna Shastri (रत्ना शास्त्री)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Kishori Devi (किशोरी देवी - Wife of Sardar Hararlal Singh)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: On **25 April 1934**, **Kishori Devi** (wife of Shekhawati leader Sardar Hararlal Singh of Hanumanpura) presided over the mammoth gathering of **10,000 Jat peasant women at Katrathal (Sikar)** to protest the misbehavior and humiliation of women by the feudal Thakur of Sihanot (Maan Singh). Eminent orator **Uttama Devi** (wife of Thakur Deshraj) delivered the keynote speech.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Anjana Devi? Anjana Devi Chaudhary was the first woman in Rajasthan arrested during the Bijolia and Begun peasant movements.
  • Why not (C) Janakidevi Bajaj? Janakidevi Bajaj presided over the Jaipur Prajamandal 1944 session and was awarded Padma Vibhushan (1956).
  • Why not (D) Ratna Shastri? Ratna Shastri co-founded *Banasthali Vidyapith* (Shanta Kuteer) alongside Pandit Hiralal Shastri.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: 10,000 Women at Katrathal (1934) = Led by Kishori Devi (Keynote: Uttama Devi); First arrested woman = Anjana Devi Chaudhary; Banasthali Vidyapith = Ratna Shastri.
🎯 Freedom Fighters — Arjun Lal Sethi & Vardhman Vidyalaya — Deep Fact Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
Which pioneer revolutionary established the Vardhman Vidyalaya (1907) in Jaipur as a secret training ground for freedom fighters and famously declared: 'यदि अर्जुनलाल नौकरी करेगा तो अंग्रेजों को भारत से बाहर कौन निकालेगा?'?
(A) Kesari Singh Barhath
(B) Seth Damodar Das Rathi
(C) Arjun Lal Sethi (अर्जुन लाल सेठी)
(D) Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (C) Arjun Lal Sethi (अर्जुन लाल सेठी)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Padmashri **Arjun Lal Sethi** (born 1880 in Jaipur) was the vanguard of Rajasthan's armed national awakening. He rejected the lucrative post of Prime Minister / Diwan of Jaipur offered by Maharaja Madho Singh II with the iconic statement: 'यदि अर्जुनलाल नौकरी करेगा तो अंग्रेजों को भारत से बाहर कौन निकालेगा?'. He authored *'Shudra Mukti'*, *'Stri Mukti'*, and *'Mahendra Kumar'*.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Kesari Singh Barhath? Kesari Singh Barhath founded the *Veer Bharat Sabha* (1910) and penned *Chetavni ra Chungtiya*.
  • Why not (B) Seth Damodar Das Rathi? Seth Damodar Das Rathi was the financier / Bhamashah of the revolutionaries in Beawar.
  • Why not (D) Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa? Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa led armed revolt in Ajmer-Merwara alongside Vijay Singh Pathik.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Vardhman Vidyalaya (1907) & 'If Sethi does job...' = Arjun Lal Sethi; Veer Bharat Sabha = Kesari Singh Barhath; Bhamashah of Revolution = Damodar Das Rathi.
🎯 Women Martyrs — Veerangana Kali Bai of Rastapal — Deep Fact Check RPSC 2nd Grade & SI
On 19 June 1947 in the princely state of Dungarpur, 13-year-old Bhil girl Kali Bai (काली बाई) sacrificed her life during the Rastapal Incident (रास्तापाल काण्ड) while rescuing which revered teacher from being dragged behind a police truck?
(A) Nanabhai Khant (नानाभाई खांट)
(B) Manikyalal Verma (माणिक्यलाल वर्मा)
(C) Bhogilal Pandya (भोगीलाल पण्ड्या)
(D) Sengabhai (सेंगाभाई - school teacher)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Sengabhai (सेंगाभाई - school teacher)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: During the Rastapal incident (June 1947), when Dungarpur state police tied schoolteacher **Sengabhai** to their vehicle and dragged him on the road for refusing to close the Sewa Sangh school, brave 13-year-old **Kali Bai** rushed forward and cut the rope with her sickle (*Danti*). The police opened fire, and she was martyred on 21 June 1947. A memorial statue stands at Gaib Sagar Lake in Dungarpur.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Nanabhai Khant? *Nanabhai Khant* was the village school patron who was beaten to death inside the schoolroom by police before Sengabhai was dragged.
  • Why not (B) Manikyalal Verma? Manikyalal Verma was the leader of the Mewar Prajamandal and author of the song *Panchhida*.
  • Why not (C) Bhogilal Pandya? Bhogilal Pandya ('Gandhi of Vagad') was the founder-president of the Dungarpur Prajamandal.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Kali Bai rescued teacher = Sengabhai (Rastapal, 1947); Teacher killed in schoolroom = Nanabhai Khant; Weapon used by Kali Bai = Sickle (दांती).
🎯 Freedom Fighters — Kesari Singh Barhath & Chetavni ra Chungtiya — Deep Concept Check RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade
In 1903 AD, revolutionary Charan poet Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ) sent 13 Dingal couplets (सोरठे) titled 'Chetavni ra Chungtiya' (चेतावनि रा चूंघट्या) to which Rajput sovereign, successfully preventing him from attending Lord Curzon's imperial Delhi Durbar?
(A) Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar (महाराणा फतेह सिंह)
(B) Maharaja Ganga Singh of Bikaner (महाराजा गंगा सिंह)
(C) Maharaja Man Singh II of Jaipur (महाराजा मानसिंह द्वितीय)
(D) Maharana Sajjan Singh of Mewar (महाराणा सज्जन सिंह)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (A) Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar (महाराणा फतेह सिंह)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Kesari Singh Barhath sent 13 inspiring Dingal Sorathas titled *Chetavni ra Chungtiya* to **Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar** before the 1903 Delhi Durbar. The couplets reminded the Maharana of Bappa Rawal, Rana Sanga, and Maharana Pratap's unblemished dignity (*'पग-पग भम्या पहाड़, धरा छोड़ राख्यो धरम...'*) causing Fateh Singh to reach Delhi but refuse to enter Curzon's imperial court.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (B) Ganga Singh of Bikaner? Maharaja Ganga Singh attended imperial conferences, signed the Treaty of Versailles (1919), and brought the Gang Canal to Bikaner (1927).
  • Why not (C) Man Singh II? Maharaja Sawai Man Singh II was the last ruling Maharaja of Jaipur and became the first Rajpramukh of Rajasthan upon Greater Rajasthan's formation in 1949.
  • Why not (D) Sajjan Singh? Maharana Sajjan Singh ruled Mewar from 1874 to 1884 and hosted Swami Dayanand Saraswati during the writing of *Satyarth Prakash* in Udaipur.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Chetavni ra Chungtiya recipient = Maharana Fateh Singh (1903); Author = Kesari Singh Barhath; Host of Swami Dayanand = Maharana Sajjan Singh.
🎯 Freedom Fighters — Martyrdom of Sagarmal Gopa — Deep Concept Check RPSC College Lecturer & EO/RO
Which brave freedom fighter and author of 'Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj' (जैसलमेर में गुंडाराज) and 'Raghunath Singh ka Mukadma' was subjected to inhuman torture and burnt alive inside Jaisalmer Central Jail on 3 April 1946?
(A) Pratap Singh Barhath (प्रताप सिंह बारहठ - Bareilly Jail Martyr)
(B) Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा - Jaisalmer Jail Martyr)
(C) Zorawar Singh Barhath (जोरावर सिंह बारहठ - Amardas Bairagi)
(D) Bhogilal Pandya (भोगीलाल पण्ड्या - "Gandhi of Vagad")
👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा - Jaisalmer Jail Martyr)
🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer: Sagarmal Gopa of Jaisalmer fearlessly exposed the tyrannical autocracy of Maharawal Jawahar Singh through his revolutionary books *'Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj'*, *'Raghunath Singh ka Mukadma'*, and *'Azadi ke Deewane'*. On 3 April 1946, royal jailer Guman Singh poured kerosene over him and burnt him alive inside Jaisalmer jail. The murder was investigated by the Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission.

🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):
  • Why not (A) Pratap Singh Barhath? Kunwar Pratap Singh Barhath (son of Kesari Singh) was arrested in the Benaras Conspiracy Case and tortured to death in **Bareilly Central Jail** (1918) by Charles Cleaveland, refusing to betray his comrades: 'I cannot make thousands of mothers weep to stop my single mother weeping.'
  • Why not (C) Zorawar Singh Barhath? Zorawar Singh Barhath threw the bomb at Viceroy Lord Hardinge in Delhi Chandni Chowk (1912) and eluded capture for 27 years, living under the ascetic identity of **Amardas Bairagi** in the ravines of Kota-Gwalior.
  • Why not (D) Bhogilal Pandya? Bhogilal Pandya is celebrated as the **'Gandhi of Vagad' (वागड़ के गांधी)** who established the Dungarpur Prajamandal (1944) and Sewa Sangh to uplift tribal communities.

💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert: Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj & burnt in jail = Sagarmal Gopa (1946); Bareilly Jail martyrdom = Pratap Singh Barhath (1918); Hardinge Bomb & Amardas Bairagi = Zorawar Singh Barhath; Gandhi of Vagad = Bhogilal Pandya.

# 🎯 Chapter 11: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

Q1 RPSC RAS Pre 2018, 2021 / EO/RO 2022
Who was the revolutionary leader originally named 'Bhup Singh' who led the historic 44-year-long non-violent Bijolia Peasant Movement (1916 onwards)?
(A) Kesari Singh Barhath
(B) Vijay Singh Pathik (विजय सिंह पथिक)
(C) Sadhu Sitaram Das
(D) Manikyalal Verma
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Vijay Singh Pathik (विजय सिंह पथिक)
Explanation: Born in Gouthavali (Bulandshahr, UP). Joined the Ras Bihari Bose revolutionary network. He established the *Bijolia Kisan Panchayat* (1917) and *Rajasthan Seva Sangh* (Wardha, 1919).
Q2 RPSC 1st Grade 2020 / SI 2021 / EO/RO 2022
Who composed the fiery 13 Dingal Sorathas titled *Chetavani ra Chungtiya* (चेतावणी रा चूंघट्या), which prevented Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar from attending the 1903 Delhi Durbar of Lord Curzon?
(A) Zorawar Singh Barhath
(B) Arjun Lal Sethi
(C) Pratap Singh Barhath
(D) Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ)
Explanation: Kesari Singh Barhath stirred the Maharana's patriotic pride through 13 Dingal couplets sent to Sarai Rohilla station, reminding him that the Mewar dynasty had never bowed before foreign emperors.
Q3 RPSC 2nd Grade 2022 / RAS Pre 2016
Which freedom fighter wrote the rebellious book *Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj* (जैसलमेर में गुंडाराज) and was martyred by being burnt alive in Jaisalmer jail in April 1946?
(A) Raghunath Singh
(B) Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा)
(C) Balmukund Bissa
(D) Jwala Prasad
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा)
Explanation: Sagarmal Gopa exposed autocratic oppression in *Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj* and *Raghunath Singh ka Mukadma*. He was burnt alive in jail on 4 April 1946.
Q4 RPSC SI 2021 / College Lecturer 2021
Which leader of Marwar was revered as "Loknayak" (लोकनायक) and "Sher-e-Rajasthan" (शेर-ए-राजस्थान) and founded the Hindi weekly *Aageevan* (आगीबाण)?
(A) Anandraj Surana
(B) Bhanwarlal Saraf
(C) Mathuradas Mathur
(D) Jai Narayan Vyas (जयनारायण व्यास)
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Jai Narayan Vyas (जयनारायण व्यास)
Explanation: Pioneer of Marwar Prajamandal (1934), editor of *Tarun Rajasthan*, *Peeph* (English), and *Aageevan* (first political newspaper in Rajasthani language from Beawar).
Q5 RPSC EO/RO 2022
Who among the following was the President of the "Rajasthan Aandolan Samiti" (राजस्थान आंदोलन समिति) formed in 1949 to advocate the immediate merger of Rajputana states into Greater Rajasthan?
(A) Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
(B) Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia (डॉ. राम मनोहर लोहिया)
(C) Jai Narayan Vyas
(D) Hiralal Shastri
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia (डॉ. राम मनोहर लोहिया)
Explanation: Socialist leader Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia presided over the Rajasthan Aandolan Samiti in 1949, speeding up the integration of Jaipur, Jodhpur, Bikaner, and Jaisalmer into Greater Rajasthan (30 March 1949).
Q6 RPSC EO/RO 2022
Which British Political Officer put pressure on the ruler of Bundi, Rao Raja Bishan Singh, and successfully helped ban the Sati system in Bundi in 1822 (first state in Rajasthan)?
(A) C.E. Burton
(B) James Tod
(C) John Ludlow
(D) Robinson
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (D) Robinson
Explanation: Bundi was the first princely state in Rajasthan to legally outlaw Sati in 1822 under British pressure (Robinson/Tod). Later in 1844, Major John Ludlow influenced Jaipur State to ban Sati and female infanticide.
Q7 RPSC EO/RO 2022 / 1st Grade
The 'Kangada Incident' (कांगड़ा काण्ड - 1946), where armed feudal Jagirdars brutally attacked famine-stricken protesting farmers, took place during which peasant movement?
(A) Shekhawati
(B) Bikaner
(C) Marwar
(D) Begun
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (B) Bikaner
Explanation: In autumn 1946, during severe crop failure in Kangada village (Ratangarh, Bikaner), Jagirdars extracted tyrannical land revenue and tortured peasants, leading to nationwide condemnation.
Q8 RPSC EO/RO 2022
Consider the following statements about Prajamandal movements: (A) Banswara Prajamandal was established in 1943 due to the efforts of Bhupendranath Trivedi; (B) Dholpur Prajamandal was established under the Chairmanship of Krishna Dutt Paliwal.
(A) (A) and (B) both are true
(B) (A) and (B) both are false
(C) Only (B) is true
(D) Only (A) is true
👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation
✅ Correct Answer: (A) (A) and (B) both are true
Explanation: Both statements are 100% accurate facts from Rajasthan Hindi Granth Academy and RBSE textbooks.
